{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\psi_{1}^{[1]}\,,\dots,{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\psi_{\ell^{\prime}}^{[\ell^{\prime}]}\in{\mathcal{Q}}({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2})
;

(Sc)_{\ell}

the coefficients

{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}c_{\ell^{\prime}}
,
\ell^{\prime}=1,\ldots,\ell-1

belong to

{\mathcal{Q}}({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2})
and vanish for any
\ell^{\prime}
odd.

Initialization.

For
\ell=1
the statements
(S\eta)_{1}
,
(S\psi)_{1}

hold since
\eta_{1}(x)=\cos(x)
,

\psi_{1}(x)={\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-1}\sin(x)
, and
(Sc)_{1}
is empty.

Inductive step.
Assuming the statements
(S\eta)_{\ell-1}
,
(S\psi)_{\ell-1}
,
(Sc)_{\ell-1}

for some
\ell\geq 2
we now prove

(S\eta)_{\ell}
,
(S\psi)_{\ell}
,
(Sc)_{\ell}
.
We write the water waves system (
2.6
) as

\begin{cases}\eta_{\epsilon}-{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}(\psi_{\epsilon})_{x}=\widetilde{c}_{\epsilon}(\psi_{\epsilon})_{x}-\dfrac{1}{2}(\psi_{\epsilon})_{x}^{2}+\dfrac{1}{2}\dfrac{(\eta_{\epsilon})_{x}^{2}[(\psi_{\epsilon})_{x}-c_{\epsilon}]^{2}}{1+(\eta_{\epsilon})_{x}^{2}}\\
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}(\eta_{\epsilon})_{x}+G_{0}\psi_{\epsilon}=-\widetilde{c}_{\epsilon}(\eta_{\epsilon})_{x}-\widetilde{G}(\eta_{\epsilon})\psi_{\epsilon}\end{cases}

(3.10)

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{c}_{\epsilon}:=c_{\epsilon}-{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\,,\quad\widetilde{G}(\eta):=G(\eta)-G_{0}=\sum_{j\geq 1}G_{j}(\eta)\,

(3.11)

and
G_{j}(\eta)
is defined in (
3.8
).
In view of (
3.10
) the Taylor coefficients

\eta_{\ell}(x),\psi_{\ell}(x),{c_{\ell-1}}
of the Stokes wave in (
2.7
) are determined recursively
for any
\ell\geq 2
by the relation

{\mathcal{B}}_{0}\begin{bmatrix}\eta_{\ell}\\
\psi_{\ell}\end{bmatrix}=c_{\ell-1}\begin{bmatrix}(\psi_{1})_{x}\\
-(\eta_{1})_{x}\end{bmatrix}+\begin{bmatrix}F_{\ell}\\
G_{\ell}\end{bmatrix}\,,\quad\begin{bmatrix}F_{\ell}\\
G_{\ell}\end{bmatrix}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell\atop\ell_{1}\text{even},\ 2\leq\ell_{1}\leq\ell-2\end{subarray}}c_{\ell_{1}}\begin{bmatrix}(\psi_{\ell_{2}})_{x}\\
-(\eta_{\ell_{2}})_{x}\end{bmatrix}+\begin{bmatrix}f_{\ell}\\
g_{\ell}\end{bmatrix}

(3.12)

(the second sum is empty for
\ell=2,3
)
where
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
is the self-adjoint operator

{\mathcal{B}}_{0}:=\begin{bmatrix}1&-{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\partial_{x}\\
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\partial_{x}&G_{0}\end{bmatrix}\,,\qquad G_{0}=|D|\tanh(\mathtt{h}|D|)\,,

and
f_{\ell}(x)
, resp.
g_{\ell}(x)
, are the
\epsilon^{\ell}
Taylor coefficients of the functions

f_{\epsilon}:=-\dfrac{1}{2}(\psi_{\epsilon}^{<\ell})_{x}^{2}+\dfrac{1}{2}\dfrac{(\eta_{\epsilon}^{<\ell})_{x}^{2}\big[(\psi_{\epsilon}^{<\ell})_{x}-c_{\epsilon}^{<\ell}\big]^{2}}{1+(\eta_{\epsilon}^{<\ell})_{x}^{2}}\,,\qquad g_{\epsilon}:=-\big(G(\eta_{\epsilon}^{<\ell})-G_{0}\big)\psi_{\epsilon}^{<\ell}\,,

(3.13)

with

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\eta_{\epsilon}^{<\ell}(x):=\epsilon\cos(x)+\sum_{2\leq\ell^{\prime}<\ell}\epsilon^{\ell^{\prime}}\eta_{\ell^{\prime}}(x),\ \ \ \ \psi_{\epsilon}^{<\ell}(x):=\epsilon{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-1}\sin(x)+\sum_{2\leq\ell^{\prime}<\ell}\epsilon^{\ell^{\prime}}\psi_{\ell^{\prime}}(x),

(3.14)

\displaystyle c_{\epsilon}^{<\ell}:={\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}+\sum_{2\leq\ell^{\prime}<\ell,\,\ell^{\prime}\textup{ even}}\epsilon^{\ell^{\prime}}c_{\ell^{\prime}}.

The next lemma follows by
the inductive assumptions
(S\eta)_{\ell}
,
(S\psi)_{\ell}
,

(Sc)_{\ell}

and Lemmata

3.2
-
3.7
.

Lemma 3.9
.

The Taylor coefficients

F_{\ell}(x),G_{\ell}(x)

in (
3.12
)
satisfy,
for any
\ell\geq 2
,

1.

F_{\ell}(x)\in\mathtt{Evn}_{\ell}
with coefficients

F_{\ell}^{[0]}\,,\ldots,F_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\in{\mathcal{Q}}({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2})
;

2.

G_{\ell}(x)\in\mathtt{Odd}_{\ell}
with

{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}G_{\ell}^{[1]}\,,\ldots,{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}G_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\in{\mathcal{Q}}({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2})
.

In order to solve the linear system (
3.12
) we report the following lemma.

Lemma 3.10
.

The Kernel of
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
is

\text{Ker}\,{\mathcal{B}}_{0}=\text{span}\begin{bmatrix}\cos(x)\\
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-1}\sin(x)\end{bmatrix}\,.

The Range of
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
is the
L^{2}
-orthogonal to

\text{Ker}\,{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
and it is given by

R=R_{0}\oplus R_{1}\oplus R_{\geq 2}\quad\text{where}\quad\quad R_{0}:=\text{span}\,\Big\{\begin{bmatrix}1\\
0\end{bmatrix}\Big\}\,,\ \ R_{1}:=\text{span}\,\Big\{\begin{bmatrix}-\cos(x)\\
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\sin(x)\end{bmatrix}\Big\}\,

and

R_{\geq 2}:=\overline{\bigoplus_{k=2}^{\infty}R_{k}}
with

R_{k}:=\text{span}\,\Big\{\begin{bmatrix}\cos(kx)\\
0\end{bmatrix}\,,\;\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\sin(kx)\end{bmatrix}\Big\}
.
The linear operator
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}^{-1}:R\to R
defined by

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}_{0}^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}1\\
0\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}1\\
0\end{bmatrix}\,,\quad{\mathcal{B}}_{0}^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}-\cos(x)\\
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\sin(x)\end{bmatrix}=\frac{1}{1+{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}}\begin{bmatrix}-\cos(x)\\
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\sin(x)\end{bmatrix}\quad\text{and}\quad\forall k\geq 2

(3.15)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}_{0}^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}f^{[k]}\cos(kx)\\
g^{[k]}\sin(kx)\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\eta^{[k]}\cos(kx)\\
\psi^{[k]}\sin(kx)\end{bmatrix},\ \begin{bmatrix}\eta^{[k]}\\
\psi^{[k]}\end{bmatrix}:=\frac{1}{k\tanh(\mathtt{h}k)-\tanh(\mathtt{h})k^{2}}\begin{bmatrix}k\tanh(\mathtt{h}k)&{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}k\\
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}k&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}f^{[k]}\\
g^{[k]}\end{bmatrix},

solves
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}{\mathcal{B}}_{0}^{-1}={\mathcal{B}}_{0}^{-1}{\left.\kern-1.2pt{\mathcal{B}}_{0}\vphantom{\big|}\right|_{R}}=\mathrm{Id}_{R}
.
For any
k\geq 2

the function
(k\tanh(\mathtt{h}k)-k^{2}\tanh(\mathtt{h}))^{-1}
in (
3.15
)
belongs to
{\mathcal{Q}}({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2})
and,
for any
\mathtt{h}_{*}>0
,

\displaystyle\inf_{\mathtt{h}\geq\mathtt{h}_{*}}\inf_{k\geq 2}k\big(k\tanh(\mathtt{h})-\tanh(\mathtt{h}k)\big)

\displaystyle=\inf_{\mathtt{h}\geq\mathtt{h}_{*}}2\big(2\tanh(\mathtt{h})-\tanh(\mathtt{h}2)\big)

(3.16)

\displaystyle=2\big(2\tanh(\mathtt{h}_{*})-\tanh(\mathtt{h}_{*}2)\big)>0\,.

Proof.

The first part is proved in

[
9
, Lemma A.3]
.
Let us prove (
3.16
) and the preservation of the rational structure
{\mathcal{Q}}({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2})
.
By the strict convexity of
z\mapsto\tanh(\mathtt{h}z)
for
z>0
and
\tanh(0)=0

we deduce that, for any
k\geq 2
, the function

\mathtt{h}\mapsto\tanh(\mathtt{h}k)-k\tanh(\mathtt{h})
is strictly increasing and positive.
Furthermore,
for any
\mathtt{h}>0
, the sequence

k\mapsto\tanh(\mathtt{h}k)-k\tanh(\mathtt{h})
is increasing.
This proves (
3.16
). Finally, by (
3.5
), the function

k\tanh(\mathtt{h}k)-k^{2}\tanh(\mathtt{h})=\frac{p({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2})}{q({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2})}

with polynomials

p(x),q(x)
with integer coefficients
and
p(x),q(x)\neq 0
for any
0<x\leq 1
.
∎

Remark 3.11
.

The lower bound in (
3.16
), which is uniform for all depths
\mathtt{h}>\mathtt{h}_{*}>0
, provides the existence of the Stokes wave for any amplitude
|\epsilon|<\epsilon_{*}(\mathtt{h}_{*})
. Clearly if
\mathtt{h}_{*}\to 0
then
\epsilon_{*}(\mathtt{h}_{*})\to 0
.

We now solve (
3.12
).
We first determine
c_{\ell-1}
.
The right hand side in (
3.12
) has to be orthogonal to
the Kernel of
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
,

c_{\ell-1}\Big(\begin{bmatrix}(\psi_{1})_{x}\\
-(\eta_{1})_{x}\end{bmatrix},\begin{bmatrix}\cos(x)\\
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-1}\sin(x)\end{bmatrix}\Big)+\Big(\begin{bmatrix}F_{\ell}(x)\\
G_{\ell}(x)\end{bmatrix},\begin{bmatrix}\cos(x)\\
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-1}\sin(x)\end{bmatrix}\Big)=0\,.

(3.17)

It results

\Big(\begin{bmatrix}(\psi_{1})_{x}\\
-(\eta_{1})_{x}\end{bmatrix},\begin{bmatrix}\cos(x)\\
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-1}\sin(x)\end{bmatrix}\Big)={\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-1}\,.

(3.18)

By Lemma
3.9
, if
\ell
is even then

\Big(\begin{bmatrix}F_{\ell}(x)\\
G_{\ell}(x)\end{bmatrix},\begin{bmatrix}\cos(x)\\
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-1}\sin(x)\end{bmatrix}\Big)=0

(3.19)

and therefore
c_{\ell-1}=0
by
(
3.17
). If
\ell
is odd then

\Big(\begin{bmatrix}F_{\ell}(x)\\
G_{\ell}(x)\end{bmatrix},\begin{bmatrix}\cos(x)\\
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-1}\sin(x)\end{bmatrix}\Big)\in{\mathcal{Q}}({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2})

and so, by (
3.18
) and
(
3.17
), we deduce that

{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}c_{\ell-1}\in{\mathcal{Q}}({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2})
. This proves
(Sc)_{\ell}
.

Then we determine
\eta_{\ell}(x)
and
\psi_{\ell}(x)
.
By Lemma
3.9

and Definition
2.1

if
\ell
is even then,
since
c_{\ell-1}=0
,
the coefficients

\eta_{\ell}^{[j]}
,
\psi_{\ell}^{[j]}

are determined
as the unique solutions of

\begin{bmatrix}1&-{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}j\\
-{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}j&j\tanh(\mathtt{h}j)\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\eta_{\ell}^{[j]}\\
\psi_{\ell}^{[j]}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}F_{\ell}^{[j]}\\
G_{\ell}^{[j]}\end{bmatrix}\,.

(3.20)

For any
j
odd
then

F_{\ell}^{[j]}=G_{\ell}^{[j]}=0
by
Lemma
3.9
and
so for
j\neq 1
we have

\eta_{\ell}^{[j]}=\psi_{\ell}^{[j]}=0
.
For
j=1

we choose

\eta_{\ell}^{[1]}=\psi_{\ell}^{[1]}=0
. For
j
even
system (
3.20
)
is solved, in view of Lemmata

3.10
and
3.9
, by

\begin{bmatrix}\eta_{\ell}^{[0]}\\
\psi_{\ell}^{[0]}\end{bmatrix}=F_{\ell}^{[0]}\begin{bmatrix}1\\
0\end{bmatrix}\,,\quad\begin{bmatrix}\eta_{\ell}^{[j]}\\
\psi_{\ell}^{[j]}\end{bmatrix}=\frac{1}{j\tanh(\mathtt{h}j)-j^{2}\tanh(\mathtt{h})}\begin{bmatrix}j\tanh(\mathtt{h}j)&{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}j\\
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}j&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}F_{\ell}^{[j]}\\
G_{\ell}^{[j]}\end{bmatrix}

(3.21)

for any
j\geq 2
.
If
j
is odd
then

F_{\ell}^{[j]}=G_{\ell}^{[j]}=0

and so

\eta_{\ell}^{[j]}=\psi_{\ell}^{[j]}=0
.
Note that
j\neq 1
since
j
is even. By Lemmata

3.9
and
3.10

we deduce

(S\eta)_{\ell}
,
(S\psi)_{\ell}
for any
\ell
even.

If
\ell
is odd then, similarly, we have to determine
only the
\eta_{\ell}^{[j]}
,
\psi_{\ell}^{[j]}

for
j
odd as solutions of (
3.20
), which,
for any
j\neq 1
, are given by the right hand side in (
3.21
).
On the other hand, for
j=1
,
the first Fourier component of
the right hand side in (
3.12
),

\begin{bmatrix}c_{\ell-1}(\psi_{1})_{x}+F_{\ell}(x)\\
-c_{\ell-1}(\eta_{1})_{x}+G_{\ell}(x)\end{bmatrix}
, is equal to

\frac{1}{2}({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}G_{\ell}^{[1]}-F_{\ell}^{[1]})\begin{bmatrix}-\cos(x)\\
\sin(x)\end{bmatrix}
by (
3.17
). Therefore,
by (
3.15
) we have

\begin{bmatrix}\eta_{\ell}^{[1]}\\
\psi_{\ell}^{[1]}\end{bmatrix}=\frac{1}{2(1+{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2})}\begin{bmatrix}-({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}G_{\ell}^{[1]}-F_{\ell}^{[1]})\\
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}G_{\ell}^{[1]}-F_{\ell}^{[1]})\end{bmatrix}\,.

By Lemmata

3.9
and
3.10

we deduce that

(S\eta)_{\ell}
,
(S\psi)_{\ell}
also for any
\ell
odd. This concludes the proof of Theorem
2.3
.

Remark 3.12
.

There is no loss of generality in selecting the solution
(
3.15
) on the mode
1
.
Adding an element of the kernel
\begin{bmatrix}\cos(x)\\
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-1}\sin(x)\end{bmatrix}
just amounts
to a reparametrization of the
\epsilon
of the Stokes wave.

Proof of Lemma
2.5
.
We first recall
[
8
, Section 2]
that
the functions

p_{\epsilon}(x)
,
a_{\epsilon}(x)

are given by

{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}+p_{\epsilon}(x):=\displaystyle{\frac{c_{\epsilon}-V(x+\mathfrak{p}_{\epsilon}(x))}{1+\mathfrak{(p_{\epsilon})}_{x}(x)}}\,,\quad 1+a_{\epsilon}(x):=\displaystyle{\frac{1+(V(x+\mathfrak{p}_{\epsilon}(x))-c_{\epsilon})B_{x}(x+\mathfrak{p}_{\epsilon}(x))}{1+\mathfrak{(p_{\epsilon})}_{x}(x)}}\,,

(3.22)

where

(V,B):={\left.\kern-1.2pt(\Phi_{x},\Phi_{y})\vphantom{\big|}\right|_{y=\eta_{\epsilon}(x)}}
are the horizontal and vertical components of the velocity field evaluated at the Stokes wave, given explicitly by

V:=V_{\epsilon}=-B(\eta_{\epsilon})_{x}+(\psi_{\epsilon})_{x}=\frac{(\psi_{\epsilon})_{x}+c_{\epsilon}(\eta_{\epsilon})_{x}^{2}}{1+(\eta_{\epsilon})_{x}^{2}}\,,\qquad B:=B_{\epsilon}=\frac{(\psi_{\epsilon})_{x}-c_{\epsilon}}{1+(\eta_{\epsilon})_{x}^{2}}(\eta_{\epsilon})_{x}\,,

(3.23)

and

\mathfrak{p}_{\epsilon}(x)
is the solution of the fixed-point equation

\mathfrak{p}_{\epsilon}(x)=\frac{\mathcal{H}}{\tanh\big((\mathtt{h}+\mathtt{f}_{\epsilon})|D|\big)}[\eta_{\epsilon}(x+\mathfrak{p}_{\epsilon}(x))]\,,\qquad\mathtt{f}_{\epsilon}:=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\eta_{\epsilon}(x+\mathfrak{p}_{\epsilon}(x))\mathrm{d}x\,,

(3.24)

where
\mathcal{H}=-\mathrm{i}\,\mathrm{sgn}(D)

is the Hilbert transform.
Note that

\mathcal{H}\cos(kx)=\sin(kx)\,,\,\quad\mathcal{H}\sin(kx)=-\cos(kx)\,,\ \ \forall k\in\mathbb{N}\,,\quad\mathcal{H}(1)=0\,.

(3.25)

Lemma
2.5
directly descends from the following one.

Lemma 3.13
.

The
2\pi
-periodic functions
V_{\epsilon}(x)
,
B_{\epsilon}(x)
,
\mathfrak{p}_{\epsilon}(x)
,
p_{\epsilon}(x)
and
a_{\epsilon}(x)

are real analytic w.r.t.

x
and
\epsilon

and

V_{\epsilon}\in{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-1}\mathtt{Evn}(\epsilon)
,
B_{\epsilon}\in{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\mathtt{Odd}(\epsilon)
,

\mathfrak{p}_{\epsilon}\in\mathtt{Odd}(\epsilon)
,

\mathtt{f}_{\epsilon}\in\mathbb{R}\cap\mathtt{Evn}(\epsilon)
,

p_{\epsilon}\in{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\mathtt{Evn}(\epsilon)
and

a_{\epsilon}\in\mathtt{Evn}(\epsilon)
.

We shall denote

B_{\epsilon}(x)=\sum_{\ell\geq 1}\epsilon^{\ell}B_{\ell}(x)\,,\quad V_{\epsilon}(x)=\sum_{\ell\geq 1}\epsilon^{\ell}V_{\ell}(x)\,,\quad\mathfrak{p}_{\epsilon}(x)=\sum_{\ell\geq 1}\epsilon^{\ell}\mathfrak{p}_{\ell}(x)\,.

Proof.

Using that

\eta_{\epsilon}\in\mathtt{Evn}(\epsilon)
,

\psi_{\epsilon}\in{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-1}\mathtt{Odd}(\epsilon)

and
c_{\epsilon}\in\mathbb{R}\cap{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\mathtt{Evn}(\epsilon)
,
we deduce, by Lemmata
3.2
-
3.6
, that
the functions
V_{\epsilon}
and
B_{\epsilon}
in (
3.23
) belong to
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\mathtt{Evn}(\epsilon)
and
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\mathtt{Odd}(\epsilon)
respectively.

We deduce that

\mathfrak{p}_{\epsilon}
, resp.

\mathtt{f}_{\epsilon}
in (
3.24
), belong to
\mathtt{Odd}(\epsilon)
, resp.

\mathbb{R}\cap\mathtt{Evn}(\epsilon)
, by
the following inductive statements: for any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}

(S\mathfrak{p})_{\ell}

the functions
\mathfrak{p}_{\ell^{\prime}}(x)
,
\ell^{\prime}=1,\ldots,\ell
,
belong to

\mathtt{Odd}_{\ell^{\prime}}
with coefficients

\mathfrak{p}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{[1]}\,,\ldots,\mathfrak{p}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{[\ell^{\prime}]}\in{\mathcal{Q}}({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2})
;

(S\mathtt{f})_{\ell}

the coefficients

\mathtt{f}_{\ell^{\prime}}
,
\ell^{\prime}=1,\ldots,\ell
, belong to
{\mathcal{Q}}({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2})
and vanish for any
\ell^{\prime}
odd.

The statements

(S\mathfrak{p})_{1}

and
(S\mathtt{f})_{1}
follow
by (
3.24
), (
3.25
)
with
\mathfrak{p}_{1}(x)={\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-2}\sin(x)
and

\mathtt{f}_{1}=0
.
Then supposing

(S\mathfrak{p})_{\ell-1}

and
(S\mathtt{f})_{\ell-1}
hold
for some
\ell\geq 2

we now prove

(S\mathfrak{p})_{\ell}

and
(S\mathtt{f})_{\ell}
.
By (
3.24
) we have

\mathfrak{p}_{\ell}(x)=\Big[\frac{\mathcal{H}}{\tanh\big((\mathtt{h}+\mathtt{f}_{\epsilon}^{<\ell})|D|)}[\eta_{\epsilon}^{\leq\ell}(x+\mathfrak{p}_{\epsilon}^{<\ell}(x))]\Big]_{\ell}

where
\eta_{\epsilon}^{\leq\ell}(x)
is defined as in (
3.14
)
summing up to
\ell^{\prime}\leq\ell

and

\mathfrak{p}_{\epsilon}^{<\ell}(x):=\sum_{1\leq\ell^{\prime}<\ell}\epsilon^{\ell^{\prime}}\mathfrak{p}_{\ell^{\prime}}(x),\quad\mathtt{f}_{\epsilon}^{<\ell}:=\sum_{2\leq\ell^{\prime}<\ell,\atop\ell^{\prime}\textup{ even}}\epsilon^{\ell^{\prime}}\mathtt{f}_{\ell^{\prime}}\,.

Moreover

\mathtt{f}_{\ell}=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\big[\eta_{\epsilon}^{\leq\ell}(x+\mathfrak{p}_{\epsilon}^{<\ell}(x))\big]_{\ell}\mathrm{d}x\,.

By the inductive assumption
and Lemma
3.5

the function

\eta_{\epsilon}^{\leq\ell}(x+\mathfrak{p}_{\epsilon}^{<\ell}(x))

belongs to

\mathtt{Evn}(\epsilon)
.
Then if
\ell
is odd then

\mathtt{f}_{\ell}=0
whereas if

\ell
is even then

\mathtt{f}_{\ell}
belongs to

\mathbb{R}\cap\mathtt{Evn}(\epsilon)
.
Moreover
by Lemma
3.8
the function

\mathfrak{p}_{\ell}(x)

is in

\mathtt{Odd}_{\ell}
with coefficients

\mathfrak{p}_{\ell}^{[1]}\,,\ldots,\mathfrak{p}_{\ell}^{[\ell]}
in
{\mathcal{Q}}({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2})
.

Finally we deduce that the functions
p_{\epsilon}(x),a_{\epsilon}(x)
in (
3.22
) are respectively in
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\mathtt{Evn}(\epsilon)
and
\mathtt{Evn}(\epsilon)
by Lemmata
3.2
,
3.3
,
3.5
and
3.6
.
∎

3.2
Asymptotics of the Stokes wave as
\mathtt{h}\to 0^{+}

We now prove a second-order asymptotic expansion of the leading Fourier coefficients

\eta_{\ell}^{[\ell]},\psi_{\ell}^{[\ell]}

of the Stokes waves
(\eta_{\epsilon},\psi_{\epsilon})
in (
2.7
)
as
\mathtt{h}\to 0^{+}
, at
any
order
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proposition 3.14
.

For any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, the
\ell
-th Fourier coefficients

\eta_{\ell}^{[\ell]},\psi_{\ell}^{[\ell]}
of the
2\pi
-periodic
coefficients
\eta_{\ell}(x)
,
\psi_{\ell}(x)
of the Stokes wave
in (
2.7
) have the asymptotic expansion as
\mathtt{h}\to 0^{+}

\displaystyle\eta_{\ell}^{[\ell]}=\ell\,x_{\ell}\mathtt{h}^{3-3\ell}+z_{\ell}\mathtt{h}^{5-3\ell}+O(\mathtt{h}^{7-3\ell})\,,

(3.26)

\displaystyle\psi_{\ell}^{[\ell]}=x_{\ell}\mathtt{h}^{3-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}}+y_{\ell}\mathtt{h}^{5-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}}+O(\mathtt{h}^{7-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}})\,,

where

\displaystyle x_{\ell}:=\Big(\frac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-1}\,,\quad y_{\ell}:=\frac{5\ell^{2}+3\ell-5}{18}\Big(\frac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-1}\,,\quad z_{\ell}:=\frac{\ell(\ell-1)(\ell+2)}{6}\Big(\frac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-1}\,.

(3.27)

As a consequence
for any
\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
, setting
\ell:=\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}
, as

\mathtt{h}\to 0^{+}
,

\eta_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\eta_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}=O(\mathtt{h}^{6-3\ell})\,,\quad\eta_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\psi_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}=O(\mathtt{h}^{6-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}})\,,\quad\psi_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\psi_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}=x_{\ell_{1}}x_{\ell_{2}}\mathtt{h}^{5-3\ell}+O(\mathtt{h}^{7-3\ell})\,.

(3.28)

Proof.

The case
\ell=1
holds by (
2.7
) and since

{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}=\sqrt{\tanh(\mathtt{h})}\,,\quad\tanh(\mathtt{h})=\mathtt{h}\big(1-\tfrac{1}{3}\mathtt{h}^{2}+r(\mathtt{h}^{4})\big)\quad\text{as}\quad\mathtt{h}\to 0^{+}\,.

(3.29)

We now suppose
by induction
that
(
3.26
), (
3.27
) hold for some
\ell\geq 2
and any

\ell^{\prime}=1,\dots,\ell-1
. Consequently formula (
3.28
) holds for any
\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell
, since
1\leq\ell_{1},\ell_{2}<\ell
.

Recall that the pair
\big(\eta_{\ell}(x),\psi_{\ell}(x)\big)
fulfills (
3.12
). We now compute the equations fulfilled by the
\ell
-th harmonics

\big(\eta_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\cos(\ell x),\psi_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\sin(\ell x)\big)
.
Since each vector
c_{\ell_{1}}\begin{bmatrix}(\psi_{\ell_{2}})_{x}\\
-(\eta_{\ell_{2}})_{x}\end{bmatrix}
do not possess
\ell
-harmonics (recall that
\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell
,
\ell_{1}\geq 1
and that
\eta_{\ell}(x)
, resp.
\psi_{\ell}(x)
,
is a trigonometric polynomial in

\mathtt{Evn}_{\ell}
, resp.
in
\mathtt{Odd}_{\ell}
), we get the linear system

\mathcal{B}_{0}\begin{bmatrix}\eta_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\cos(\ell x)\\
\psi_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\sin(\ell x)\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}f_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\cos(\ell x)\\
g_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\sin(\ell x)\end{bmatrix}\,,\quad\mathcal{B}_{0}=\begin{bmatrix}1&-{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\partial_{x}\\
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\partial_{x}&G_{0}\end{bmatrix}\,,

(3.30)

where

f_{\ell}^{[\ell]}:=\Big[-\dfrac{1}{2}(\psi_{\epsilon}^{<\ell})_{x}^{2}+\dfrac{1}{2}\dfrac{(\eta_{\epsilon}^{<\ell})_{x}^{2}\big[(\psi_{\epsilon}^{<\ell})_{x}-c_{\epsilon}^{<\ell}\big]^{2}}{1+(\eta_{\epsilon}^{<\ell})_{x}^{2}}\Big]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\,,\quad g_{\ell}^{[\ell]}:=\Big[-\big(G(\eta_{\epsilon}^{<\ell})-G_{0}\big)\psi_{\epsilon}^{<\ell}\Big]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\,.

(3.31)

Its solution, given by (
3.15
), is

\begin{bmatrix}\eta_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\\
\psi_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\end{bmatrix}:=\frac{1}{\ell\tanh(\mathtt{h}\ell)-\tanh(\mathtt{h})\ell^{2}}\begin{bmatrix}\ell\tanh(\mathtt{h}\ell)&{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\ell\\
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\ell&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}f_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\\
g_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\end{bmatrix}\,.

(3.32)

We are left to compute the expansion when
\mathtt{h}\to 0^{+}
of the terms in the right hand side.
We will use systematically Lemma
3.4
.

Expansion of
f_{\ell}^{[\ell]}
.

By (
3.11
) we have
[c_{\epsilon}^{<\ell}]_{j}^{[\ell^{\prime}]}=0
for any
\ell^{\prime}\neq 0
and
j\in\mathbb{N}
, and by exploiting (
2.7
)-(
2.8
) and the multiplicative structure of the spaces
\mathtt{Evn}_{j}
and
\mathtt{Odd}_{j}
in (
2.3
)-(
2.4
), we obtain

\displaystyle f_{\ell}^{[\ell]}

\displaystyle=\Big[-\frac{1}{2}(\psi_{\epsilon}^{<\ell})_{x}^{2}+\dfrac{1}{2}\dfrac{(\eta_{\epsilon}^{<\ell})_{x}^{2}[{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}-(\psi_{\epsilon}^{<\ell})_{x}]^{2}}{1+(\eta_{\epsilon}^{<\ell})_{x}^{2}}\Big]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}

(3.33)

\displaystyle=\Big[-\frac{1}{2}(\psi_{\epsilon}^{<\ell})_{x}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}{{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}}(\eta_{\epsilon}^{<\ell})_{x}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\dfrac{(\psi_{\epsilon}^{<\ell})_{x}^{2}(\eta_{\epsilon}^{<\ell})_{x}^{2}-2{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}(\psi_{\epsilon}^{<\ell})_{x}(\eta_{\epsilon}^{<\ell})_{x}^{2}-{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}(\eta_{\epsilon}^{<\ell})_{x}^{4}}{1+(\eta_{\epsilon}^{<\ell})_{x}^{2}}\Big]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\,.

By
(
3.28
) and
(
3.29
) we have, for any
\ell^{\prime}=1,\dots,\ell
,

\displaystyle[(\eta_{\epsilon}^{<\ell^{\prime}})_{x}^{2}(\psi_{\epsilon}^{<\ell^{\prime}})_{x}^{2}]_{\ell^{\prime}}^{[\ell^{\prime}]}=O(\mathtt{h}^{11-3\ell^{\prime}})\,,\quad[{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}(\eta_{\epsilon}^{<\ell^{\prime}})_{x}^{2}(\psi_{\epsilon}^{<\ell^{\prime}})_{x}]_{\ell^{\prime}}^{[\ell^{\prime}]}=O(\mathtt{h}^{9-3\ell^{\prime}})\,,

\displaystyle[{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}(\eta_{\epsilon}^{<\ell^{\prime}})_{x}^{4}]_{\ell^{\prime}}^{[\ell^{\prime}]}=O(\mathtt{h}^{9-3\ell^{\prime}})\,,\quad\Big[\frac{1}{1+(\eta_{\epsilon}^{<\ell^{\prime}})_{x}^{2}}\Big]_{\ell^{\prime}}^{[\ell^{\prime}]}=O(\mathtt{h}^{6-3\ell^{\prime}})\,,

and we deduce from (
3.33
) that

f_{\ell}^{[\ell]}=\Big[-\frac{1}{2}(\psi_{\epsilon}^{<\ell})_{x}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}(\eta_{\epsilon}^{<\ell})_{x}^{2}\Big]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}+O(\mathtt{h}^{9-3\ell})\,.

(3.34)

Thus, by the inductive assumption (
3.26
)-(
3.27
)
and Lemma
3.4
, we have

\displaystyle f_{\ell}^{[\ell]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\,\psi_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\psi_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}[\cos(\ell_{1}x)\cos(\ell_{2}x)]^{[\ell]}+\frac{\mathtt{h}}{2}\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\,\eta_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\eta_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}[\sin(\ell_{1}x)\sin(\ell_{2}x)]^{[\ell]}+O(\mathtt{h}^{9-3\ell})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathtt{h}^{5-3\ell}}{4}\Big(\frac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-2}\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}-\frac{\mathtt{h}^{7-3\ell}}{2}\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\,x_{\ell_{1}}y_{\ell_{2}}-\frac{\mathtt{h}^{7-3\ell}}{4}\Big(\frac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-2}\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{2}^{2}+O(\mathtt{h}^{9-3\ell})

\displaystyle=-\frac{3^{\ell-3}}{8^{\ell-1}}(\ell^{2}-1)\ell\,\mathtt{h}^{5-3\ell}-\frac{3^{\ell-4}}{8^{\ell-1}}\frac{(\ell^{2}-1)\ell(11\ell^{2}+5\ell-14)}{10}\mathtt{h}^{7-3\ell}+O(\mathtt{h}^{9-3\ell})\,,

(3.35)

using in the last step the algebraic identities

{\begin{matrix}\sum\limits_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell\atop 1\leq\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\leq\ell-1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}=\frac{\ell(\ell^{2}-1)}{6}\ ,\sum\limits_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell\atop 1\leq\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\leq\ell-1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}(5\ell_{2}^{2}+3\ell_{2}-5)=\frac{(\ell^{2}-1)\ell(\ell^{2}+\ell-4)}{4}\,,\sum\limits_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell\atop 1\leq\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\leq\ell-1}\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{2}^{2}=\frac{(\ell^{2}-1)\ell(\ell^{2}+1)}{30}\end{matrix}}\ .

(3.36)

Expansion of
g_{\ell}^{[\ell]}
.

The operators
G_{j}(\eta)
fulfill, for
\mathtt{h}\to 0^{+}
,

[G_{j}(\eta_{\epsilon}^{<\ell})\psi_{\epsilon}^{<\ell}]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}=O(\mathtt{h}^{3j+4-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}})\ \mbox{ if }j\mbox{ is even}\,,\quad[G_{j}(\eta_{\epsilon}^{<\ell})\psi_{\epsilon}^{<\ell}]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}=O(\mathtt{h}^{3j+3-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}})\ \mbox{ if }j\mbox{ is odd}\,,

(3.37)

as one can prove by induction using (
3.8
),
(
3.26
)
and Lemma

3.4
.
Thus by
(
3.31
)
and (
3.11
)

g_{\ell}^{[\ell]}=\big[-\big(G(\eta_{\epsilon}^{<\ell})-G_{0}\big)\psi_{\epsilon}^{<\ell}\big]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}=\Big[-G_{1}(\eta_{\epsilon}^{<\ell})\psi_{\epsilon}^{<\ell}\Big]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}+O(\mathtt{h}^{10-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}})\,.

(3.38)

Recall that

G_{1}(\eta)\psi=-\partial_{x}(\eta\psi_{x})-G_{0}[\eta G_{0}\psi)]\ .

By the inductive assumption (
3.26
)-(
3.27
) and exploiting that, in view of (
3.11
), for any
\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell
,

\displaystyle\big[\partial_{x}(\cos(\ell_{1}x)\partial_{x}\sin(\ell_{2}x))\big]^{[\ell]}=-\ell\frac{\ell_{2}}{2}\sin(\ell x)\,,\ \ \big[G_{0}(\cos(\ell_{1}x)G_{0}\sin(\ell_{2}x))\big]^{[\ell]}=\mathtt{h}^{2}\ell^{2}\frac{\ell_{2}^{2}}{2}\sin(\ell x)\big(1+O(\mathtt{h}^{2})\big)\,,

we have

\displaystyle g_{\ell}^{[\ell]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\big[\partial_{x}\eta_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\cos(\ell_{1}x)\partial_{x}\psi_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}\sin(\ell_{2}x)\big]^{[\ell]}+\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\big[G_{0}\eta_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\cos(\ell_{1}x)G_{0}\psi_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}\sin(\ell_{2}x)\big]^{[\ell]}+O(\mathtt{h}^{10-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}})

\displaystyle=-\ell\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\eta_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\psi_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\frac{\ell_{2}}{2}+\mathtt{h}^{2}\ell^{2}\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\eta_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\psi_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\frac{\ell_{2}^{2}}{2}+O(\mathtt{h}^{10-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}})

\displaystyle=-\ell\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\frac{\ell_{2}}{2}\big(\ell_{1}x_{\ell_{1}}\mathtt{h}^{3-3\ell_{1}}+z_{\ell_{1}}\mathtt{h}^{5-3\ell_{1}}+O(\mathtt{h}^{7-3\ell_{1}})\big)\big(x_{\ell_{2}}\mathtt{h}^{3-3\ell_{2}-\frac{1}{2}}+y_{\ell_{2}}\mathtt{h}^{5-3\ell_{2}-\frac{1}{2}}+O(\mathtt{h}^{7-3\ell_{2}-\frac{1}{2}})\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathtt{h}^{2}\ell^{2}\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\frac{\ell_{2}^{2}}{2}\big(\ell_{1}x_{\ell_{1}}\mathtt{h}^{3-3\ell_{1}}+O(\mathtt{h}^{5-3\ell_{1}})\big)\big(x_{\ell_{2}}\mathtt{h}^{3-3\ell_{2}-\frac{1}{2}}+O(\mathtt{h}^{5-3\ell_{2}-\frac{1}{2}})\big)+O(\mathtt{h}^{10-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}})

\displaystyle=-\ell\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\frac{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}{2}x_{\ell_{1}}x_{\ell_{2}}\mathtt{h}^{6-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}}+\Big(\ell^{2}\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\frac{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}^{2}}{2}x_{\ell_{1}}x_{\ell_{2}}-\ell\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\frac{\ell_{2}}{2}(\ell_{1}x_{\ell_{1}}y_{\ell_{2}}+z_{\ell_{1}}x_{\ell_{2}})\Big)\mathtt{h}^{8-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+O(\mathtt{h}^{10-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}})

\displaystyle=-2\frac{3^{\ell-3}}{8^{\ell-1}}(\ell^{2}-1)\ell^{2}\mathtt{h}^{6-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}}+\Big[\frac{3^{\ell-3}}{8^{\ell-1}}\ell^{4}(\ell^{2}-1)-\frac{3^{\ell-4}}{8^{\ell-1}}\frac{(\ell^{2}-1)\ell^{2}(\ell^{2}+\ell-4)}{2}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{3^{\ell-4}}{8^{\ell-1}}\frac{(\ell-2)(\ell^{2}-1)\ell^{2}(3\ell+11)}{10}\Big]\mathtt{h}^{8-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}}+O(\mathtt{h}^{10-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}})

\displaystyle=-2\frac{3^{\ell-3}}{8^{\ell-1}}(\ell^{2}-1)\ell^{2}\mathtt{h}^{6-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{3^{\ell-4}}{8^{\ell-1}}\frac{(\ell^{2}-1)\ell^{2}(11\ell^{2}-5\ell+21)}{5}\mathtt{h}^{8-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}}+O(\mathtt{h}^{10-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}})

(3.39)

where in the final step we used (
3.36
) together with the following identities

\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell\atop 1\leq\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\leq\ell-1}\ell_{2}\ell_{1}(\ell_{1}-1)(\ell_{1}+2)=\frac{(\ell^{2}-1)\ell(\ell-2)(3\ell+11)}{60}\ ,\quad\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell\atop 1\leq\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\leq\ell-1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}^{2}=\frac{(\ell^{2}-1)\ell^{2}}{12}\ .

(3.40)

Expansion of the matrix elements in (
3.32
).

As
\mathtt{h}\to 0^{+}
, the matrix elements in (
3.32
) expand as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\ell\tanh(\mathtt{h}\ell)-\tanh(\mathtt{h})\ell^{2}}\begin{bmatrix}\ell\tanh(\mathtt{h}\ell)&{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\ell\\
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\ell&1\end{bmatrix}

(3.41)

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{\ell^{2}(1-\ell^{2})\mathtt{h}^{3}}\big(1+\tfrac{2}{5}\mathtt{h}^{2}(1+\ell^{2})+O(\mathtt{h}^{4})\big)\begin{pmatrix}\ell^{2}\,\mathtt{h}\big(1-\frac{\ell^{2}\mathtt{h}^{2}}{3}+O(\mathtt{h}^{4})\big)&\ell\,\mathtt{h}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(1-\frac{\mathtt{h}^{2}}{6}+O(\mathtt{h}^{4})\big)\\[5.69054pt]
\ell\,\mathtt{h}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(1-\frac{\mathtt{h}^{2}}{6}+O(\mathtt{h}^{4})\big)&1\end{pmatrix}\,.

In view of (
3.32
) and using the expansion in (
3.41
), we obtain

\begin{pmatrix}\eta_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\\[5.69054pt]
\psi_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\end{pmatrix}={\footnotesize\begin{matrix}\begin{pmatrix}\dfrac{3\big(\ell\,\mathtt{h}^{\frac{1}{2}}f_{\ell}^{[\ell]}+g_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\big)}{\ell(1-\ell^{2})\mathtt{h}^{\frac{5}{2}}}\\[10.0pt]
\dfrac{3\big(\ell\,\mathtt{h}^{\frac{1}{2}}f_{\ell}^{[\ell]}+g_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\big)}{\ell^{2}(1-\ell^{2})\mathtt{h}^{3}}\end{pmatrix}-\begin{pmatrix}\dfrac{2\ell^{3}\,\mathtt{h}^{\frac{1}{2}}f_{\ell}^{[\ell]}+g_{\ell}^{[\ell]}}{2\ell(1-\ell^{2})\mathtt{h}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\\[10.0pt]
\dfrac{f_{\ell}^{[\ell]}}{2\ell(1-\ell^{2})\mathtt{h}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}\dfrac{6(1+\ell^{2})\big(\ell\,\mathtt{h}^{\frac{1}{2}}f_{\ell}^{[\ell]}+g_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\big)}{5\ell(1-\ell^{2})\mathtt{h}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\\[10.0pt]
\dfrac{6(1+\ell^{2})\big(\ell\,\mathtt{h}^{\frac{1}{2}}f_{\ell}^{[\ell]}+g_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\big)}{5\ell^{2}(1-\ell^{2})\mathtt{h}}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}O(\mathtt{h}^{2}f_{\ell}^{[\ell]}+\mathtt{h}^{\frac{3}{2}}g_{\ell}^{[\ell]})\\[5.0pt]
O(\mathtt{h}^{\frac{3}{2}}f_{\ell}^{[\ell]}+\mathtt{h}g_{\ell}^{[\ell]})\end{pmatrix}\end{matrix}}\,.\,

(3.42)

By (
3.35
), (
3.39
) and (
3.42
) we obtain the asymptotic expansions (
3.26
) for
\psi_{\ell}^{[\ell]}
and
\eta_{\ell}^{[\ell]}
.
∎

We deduce a similar property for the coefficients
p_{\epsilon},a_{\epsilon}

of the linearized operator
{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}
in (
2.10
).

Lemma 3.15
.

For any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
the
\ell
-th Fourier
coefficients
p_{\ell}^{[\ell]}
,
a_{\ell}^{[\ell]}
of the
2\pi
-periodic
functions

p_{\ell}(x)
,
a_{\ell}(x)

in (
2.11
) have the asymptotic expansion, as
\mathtt{h}\to 0^{+}
,

\displaystyle p_{\ell}^{[\ell]}=-2\ell\Big(\frac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-1}\mathtt{h}^{3-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}}-\ell\Big(\frac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-1}\,\frac{11\ell^{2}-9\ell+1}{9}\mathtt{h}^{5-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}}+O(\mathtt{h}^{7-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}})\,,

(3.43)

\displaystyle a_{\ell}^{[\ell]}=-\ell\Big(\frac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-1}\mathtt{h}^{2-3\ell}-\ell\Big(\frac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-1}\,\frac{31\ell^{2}-9\ell+2}{18}\mathtt{h}^{4-3\ell}+O(\mathtt{h}^{6-3\ell})\,.

Proof.

We shall use systematically Lemma

3.4
.
We first deduce the following expansions, as
\mathtt{h}\to 0^{+}
,

\displaystyle B_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{espVB}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\Big[\frac{(\psi_{\epsilon})_{x}-c_{\epsilon}}{1+(\eta_{\epsilon})_{x}^{2}}(\eta_{\epsilon})_{x}\Big]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{multeffect}}}{{=}}[-{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}(\eta_{\epsilon})_{x}+(\psi_{\epsilon})_{x}(\eta_{\epsilon})_{x}]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}+O(\mathtt{h}^{8-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}})

(3.44)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{expch}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\mathtt{h}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(1-\frac{\mathtt{h}^{2}}{6}+O(\mathtt{h}^{4})\Big)\ell\eta_{\ell}^{[\ell]}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\psi_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\eta_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}+O(\mathtt{h}^{8-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{expetajjpsijj}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ell^{2}\Big(\frac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-1}\mathtt{h}^{4-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}}+\ell^{2}\Big(\frac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-1}\ \frac{\ell^{2}+3\ell-7}{18}\mathtt{h}^{6-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}}+O(\mathtt{h}^{8-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}})

using also the second identity in (
3.40
). Moreover

\displaystyle V_{\ell}^{[\ell]}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{espVB}}}{{=}}\big[-B(\eta_{\epsilon})_{x}+(\psi_{\epsilon})_{x}\big]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{expetajjpsijj},\eqref{Basymp}}}{{=}}[(\psi_{\epsilon})_{x}]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}+O(\mathtt{h}^{7-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}})

(3.45)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{expetajjpsijj}}}{{=}}\ell\Big(\frac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-1}\mathtt{h}^{3-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}}+\ell\Big(\frac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-1}\frac{5\ell^{2}+3\ell-5}{18}\mathtt{h}^{5-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}}+O(\mathtt{h}^{7-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}})\,.

We now claim that
for any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
the
\ell
-th Fourier coefficient of the function

\mathfrak{p}_{\ell}(x)
defined by (
3.24
) has the asymptotic expansion as
\mathtt{h}\to 0^{+}

\mathfrak{p}_{\ell}^{[\ell]}=\Big(\frac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-1}\mathtt{h}^{2-3\ell}+\Big(\frac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-1}\frac{17\ell^{2}-9\ell-2}{18}\mathtt{h}^{4-3\ell}+O(\mathtt{h}^{6-3\ell})\,.

(3.46)

For
\ell=1
we have, by (
3.24
),

\mathfrak{p}_{1}(x)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{Stokespwseries}}}{{=}}\frac{\sin(x)}{\tanh(\mathtt{h})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{expch}}}{{=}}\frac{\sin(x)}{\mathtt{h}}\big(1+\frac{\mathtt{h}^{2}}{3}+O(\mathtt{h}^{4})\big)

and (
3.46
)
for
\ell=1
follows. Then we argue by induction supposing that (
3.46
) holds for some
\ell\geq 2
and any

\ell^{\prime}=1,\dots\ell-1
.
Consequently

\mathfrak{p}_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\mathfrak{p}_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}=O(\mathtt{h}^{4-3(\ell_{1}+\ell_{2})})
for any
1\leq\ell_{1},\ell_{2}<\ell
.
Recall that
\mathfrak{p}_{\epsilon}
is
\mathtt{Odd}(\epsilon)
, and
\eta_{\epsilon}(x)
,
\eta_{\epsilon}(x+\mathfrak{p}_{\epsilon}(x))
are in
\mathtt{Evn}(\epsilon)
(cfr Lemma
3.5
), so
we get, by exploiting formula (
3.7
),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{\ell}^{[\ell]}

\displaystyle\quad\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{def:ttf}}}{{=}}\quad\,\Big[\frac{\mathcal{H}}{\tanh\big((\mathtt{h}+\mathtt{f}_{\epsilon})|D|\big)}\big[\eta_{\epsilon}^{\leq\ell}\big(x+\mathfrak{p}^{<\ell}_{\epsilon}(x)\big)\big]\Big]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}

\displaystyle\quad\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{hilbert}}}{{=}}\quad\,\frac{1}{\tanh\big(\mathtt{h}\ell\big)}\big[\eta_{\epsilon}^{\leq\ell}\big(x+\mathfrak{p}_{\epsilon}^{<\ell}(x)\big)\big]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{expetajjpsijj},\eqref{expch}}}{{=}}\frac{1+\frac{\ell^{2}\mathtt{h}^{2}}{3}+O(\mathtt{h}^{4})}{\ell\mathtt{h}}\Big(\eta_{\ell}^{[\ell]}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\ell_{1}\eta_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\mathfrak{p}_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}+O(\mathtt{h}^{7-3\ell})\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{claimpgot}}}{{=}}\quad\,\Big(\frac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-1}\,\frac{1+\frac{\ell^{2}\mathtt{h}^{2}}{3}+O(\mathtt{h}^{4})}{\mathtt{h}}\Big(\mathtt{h}^{3-3\ell}+\frac{(\ell-1)(\ell+2)}{6}\mathtt{h}^{5-3\ell}+\frac{4\ell^{2}-6\ell+2}{9}\mathtt{h}^{5-3\ell}+O(\mathtt{h}^{7-3\ell})\Big)

using also
\sum\limits_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\ell_{1}^{2}=\tfrac{1}{6}\ell(\ell-1)(2\ell-1)
.
This proves the asymptotic expansion (
3.46
) for
\mathfrak{p}_{\ell}^{[\ell]}
.

Finally we have, denoting
\mathfrak{p}\equiv\mathfrak{p}_{\epsilon}
for brevity,

\displaystyle p_{\ell}^{[\ell]}

\displaystyle\quad\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{def:pa}}}{{=}}\quad\,\Big[\displaystyle{\frac{c_{\epsilon}-V^{\leq\ell}(x+\mathfrak{p}^{\leq\ell}(x))}{1+\mathfrak{p}^{\leq\ell}_{x}(x)}}\Big]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{Basymp}-\eqref{claimpgot}}}{{=}}\Big[-{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\mathfrak{p}^{\leq\ell}_{x}(x)+{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}(\mathfrak{p}^{<\ell}_{x})^{2}(x)-V^{\leq\ell}(x)-V_{x}^{<\ell}(x)\mathfrak{p}^{<\ell}(x)+V^{<\ell}(x)\mathfrak{p}^{<\ell}_{x}(x)\Big]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}+O(\mathtt{h}^{7-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}})

\displaystyle=-{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\,\ell\,\mathfrak{p}_{\ell}^{[\ell]}+\frac{{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}}{2}\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\mathfrak{p}_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\mathfrak{p}_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}-V_{\ell}^{[\ell]}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}(\ell_{2}-\ell_{1})V_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\mathfrak{p}_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}+O(\mathtt{h}^{7-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}})

\displaystyle=-\ell\Big(\frac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-1}\mathtt{h}^{3-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}}-\ell\Big(\frac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-1}\Big(\frac{17\ell^{2}-9\ell-2}{18}-\frac{1}{6}\Big)\mathtt{h}^{5-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{2}{9}\ell\Big(\frac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-1}(\ell^{2}-1)\mathtt{h}^{5-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad-\ell\Big(\frac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-1}\mathtt{h}^{3-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}}-\ell\Big(\frac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-1}\frac{5\ell^{2}+3\ell-5}{18}\mathtt{h}^{5-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}}-\frac{2}{9}\ell\Big(\frac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-1}(\ell^{2}-3\ell+2)\mathtt{h}^{5-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}}+O(\mathtt{h}^{7-3\ell-\frac{1}{2}})\,,

where in the last step we used (
3.29
),(
3.44
)-(
3.46
) and (
3.36
).
Summing up we obtain the first line in (
3.43
).

Similarly,

\displaystyle a_{\ell}^{[\ell]}

\displaystyle\quad\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{def:pa}}}{{=}}\quad\,\Big[\displaystyle{\frac{1+(V(x+\mathfrak{p}(x))-c_{\epsilon})B_{x}(x+\mathfrak{p}(x))}{1+\mathfrak{p}_{x}(x)}}\Big]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}

\displaystyle\quad\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{def:pa}}}{{=}}\quad\,\Big[\displaystyle{\frac{1}{1+\mathfrak{p}_{x}^{\leq\ell}(x)}}-({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}+p_{\epsilon}^{\leq\ell}(x))B_{x}(x+\mathfrak{p}^{\leq\ell}(x))\Big]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{exppjjajj}-\eqref{claimpgot}}}{{=}}\Big[-\mathfrak{p}_{x}^{\leq\ell}(x)+(\mathfrak{p}_{x}^{\leq\ell})^{2}(x)-{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}B_{x}^{\leq\ell}(x)\Big]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}+O(\mathtt{h}^{6-3\ell})\,

\displaystyle\qquad=\quad\,-\ell\,\mathfrak{p}_{\ell}^{[\ell]}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\mathfrak{p}_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\mathfrak{p}_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}-{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\ell B_{\ell}^{[\ell]}+O(\mathtt{h}^{6-3\ell})

\displaystyle\qquad=\quad\,-\ell\Big(\frac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-1}\mathtt{h}^{2-3\ell}-\ell\Big(\frac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-1}\frac{17\ell^{2}-9\ell-2}{18}\mathtt{h}^{4-3\ell}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{2}{9}\ell\Big(\frac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-1}(\ell^{2}-1)\mathtt{h}^{4-3\ell}-\ell^{3}\Big(\frac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-1}\mathtt{h}^{4-3\ell}+O(\mathtt{h}^{6-3\ell})

where in the last step we used (
3.44
)-(
3.46
) and (
3.36
).
This gives the second line in (
3.43
).
∎

3.3
Limit of the Stokes wave as
\mathtt{h}\to+\infty

We now prove another
structural property of the
leading Fourier coefficients

\eta_{\ell}^{[\ell]},\psi_{\ell}^{[\ell]}
of the Stokes waves at
any
order in the deep water limit.

Proposition 3.16
.

For any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, the
\ell
-th Fourier coefficients

\eta_{\ell}^{[\ell]},\psi_{\ell}^{[\ell]}
of the
2\pi
-periodic
coefficients
\eta_{\ell}(x)
,
\psi_{\ell}(x)
of the Stokes wave
in (
2.7
) have a finite identical limit as
\mathtt{h}\to+\infty
,

\lim_{\mathtt{h}\to+\infty}\eta_{1}^{[1]}=\lim_{\mathtt{h}\to+\infty}\psi_{1}^{[1]}=1\,,\qquad\lim_{\mathtt{h}\to+\infty}\eta_{\ell}^{[\ell]}=\lim_{\mathtt{h}\to+\infty}\psi_{\ell}^{[\ell]}=\lim_{\mathtt{h}\to+\infty}-\frac{f_{\ell}^{[\ell]}}{\ell-1}\in\mathbb{Q}\,,\quad\forall\ell\geq 2\,,

(3.47)

with
f_{\ell}^{[\ell]}
in (
3.33
).

In order to prove Proposition
3.16
the key result is
Lemma
3.17
,
where for any function
f_{\epsilon}\in\mathtt{Evn}(\epsilon)
and
h_{\epsilon}\in\mathtt{Odd}(\epsilon)
we denote for brevity

f_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;:=\lim_{\mathtt{h}\to\infty}f_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\in\mathbb{Q}\,,\quad g_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;:=\lim_{\mathtt{h}\to\infty}g_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\in\mathbb{Q}\,.

Lemma 3.17
.

Let
f_{\epsilon}\in\mathtt{Evn}(\epsilon)
and
h_{\epsilon}\in\mathtt{Odd}(\epsilon)
according to Definition
3.1
. Then, for any

j,\ell\in\mathbb{N}
,

\lim_{\mathtt{h}\to+\infty}[G_{0}h_{\epsilon}]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}=\ell\,h_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;\,,\quad\lim_{\mathtt{h}\to+\infty}[\partial_{x}f_{\epsilon}\partial_{x}h_{\epsilon}+G_{0}f_{\epsilon}G_{0}h_{\epsilon}]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}=0\,,\quad\lim_{\mathtt{h}\to+\infty}[G_{j}(f_{\epsilon})h_{\epsilon}]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}=0\,.

(3.48)

Proof.

The first identity directly follows by

[G_{0}h_{\epsilon}]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}=\big[|D|\tanh(\mathtt{h}|D|)h_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\sin(\ell x)\big]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}=\ell\tanh(\mathtt{h}\ell)h_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\overset{\mathtt{h}\to+\infty}{\longrightarrow}\ell h_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;\,.

The second identity
follows,
recalling that

f_{\ell}\in\mathtt{Evn}_{\ell}

and
h_{\ell}\in\mathtt{Odd}_{\ell}
, see Definition
2.1
,
by Lemma

3.4
,

\lim_{\mathtt{h}\to+\infty}[\partial_{x}f_{\epsilon}\partial_{x}h_{\epsilon}]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}=-\frac{\ell}{2}\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\ell_{2}f_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;h_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;\,,

and, similarly,

\lim_{\mathtt{h}\to+\infty}[G_{0}f_{\epsilon}G_{0}h_{\epsilon}]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}=\frac{\ell}{2}\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\ell_{2}f_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;h_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;\,.

The third identity follows by induction on
j\in\mathbb{N}
. Indeed, the case
j=1
is exactly the second identity in
(
3.48
),
since

G_{1}(\eta)=-\partial_{x}\eta\partial_{x}-G_{0}\eta G_{0}
,
cfr. (
3.11
). Let
j>1
and suppose that (
3.48
) holds for
j^{\prime}=1,\dots,j-1
.
Then, in view of the recursive expression (
3.8
) of the
Dirichlet-Neumann operator,
the fact that

f_{\epsilon}^{j}\in\mathtt{Evn}(\epsilon)
,
G_{\ell}(f_{\epsilon}^{j})h_{\epsilon}\in\mathtt{Odd}(\epsilon)
, and inductive
hypothesis,
one has

\lim_{\mathtt{h}\to+\infty}[G_{j}(f_{\epsilon})h_{\epsilon}]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{Craig}}}{{=}}-\frac{1}{j!}\ell^{j-1}\lim_{\mathtt{h}\to+\infty}[\partial_{x}f_{\epsilon}^{j}\partial_{x}h_{\epsilon}+|D|f_{\epsilon}^{j}G_{0}h_{\epsilon}]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\,,

(3.49)

which comes from

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{j!}G_{0}|D|^{j-2}\partial_{x}h_{\epsilon}f_{\epsilon}^{j}\partial_{x}h_{\epsilon}-\frac{1}{j!}|D|^{j}f_{\epsilon}^{j}G_{0}h_{\epsilon}\,\quad\text{if}\ j\ \text{is even}\,,

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{j!}|D|^{j-1}\partial_{x}f_{\epsilon}^{j}\partial_{x}h_{\epsilon}-\frac{1}{j!}G_{0}|D|^{j-1}f_{\epsilon}^{j}G_{0}h_{\epsilon}\,\quad\text{if}\ j\ \text{is odd}\,,

whereas the other
terms vanish.
Finally,
the limit in
(
3.49
)
is zero since
f_{\epsilon}^{j}\in\mathtt{Evn}(\epsilon)
, recalling that

G_{0}=|\mathtt{h}|\tanh(\mathtt{h}|D|)

and using the second identity in (
3.48
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
3.16
.
The case
\ell=1
holds by (
2.7
).
For any
\ell\geq 2

the pair
(\eta_{\ell}^{[\ell]},\psi_{\ell}^{[\ell]})
solves the linear system (
3.30
) with
f_{\ell}^{[\ell]}
,
g_{\ell}^{[\ell]}
in (
3.33
) where,
by Lemma
3.17
,

\lim_{\mathtt{h}\to+\infty}g_{\ell}^{[\ell]}=\lim_{\mathtt{h}\to+\infty}\Big[-\widetilde{G}(\eta_{\epsilon})\psi_{\epsilon}\Big]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}=-\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\lim_{\mathtt{h}\to+\infty}\Big[G_{j}(\eta_{\epsilon})\psi_{\epsilon}\Big]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}=0\,,\qquad\forall\ell\in\mathbb{N}\,.

(3.50)

By (
3.50
) and (
3.30
) we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathtt{h}\to+\infty}\begin{bmatrix}\eta_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\cos(\ell x)\\
\psi_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\sin(\ell x)\end{bmatrix}=\lim_{\mathtt{h}\to+\infty}(G_{0}+{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}\partial_{x}^{2})^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}G_{0}&{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\partial_{x}\\
-{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\partial_{x}&1\end{bmatrix}^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}f_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\cos(\ell x)\\
0\end{bmatrix}\,,

namely, passing to the limit
\mathtt{h}\to+\infty
,

\begin{pmatrix}\eta_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;\\[2.84526pt]
\psi_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;\end{pmatrix}=-\frac{1}{\ell(\ell-1)}\begin{pmatrix}\ell&\ell\\
\ell&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}f_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;\\[2.84526pt]
0\end{pmatrix}=-\frac{1}{\ell-1}\begin{pmatrix}f_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;\\[2.84526pt]
f_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;\end{pmatrix}\,.

(3.51)

We have proved that
\eta_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;=\psi_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;

are equal in the deep-water limit. ∎

We deduce a similar property for the coefficients
p_{\epsilon},a_{\epsilon}

of the linearized operator
{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}
in (
2.10
).

Lemma 3.18
.

For any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
the
\ell
-th Fourier
coefficients
p_{\ell}^{[\ell]}
,
a_{\ell}^{[\ell]}
of the
2\pi
-periodic
functions

p_{\ell}(x)
,
a_{\ell}(x)

in (
5.22
) have a finite identical limit as
\mathtt{h}\to+\infty
, i.e.

\lim_{\mathtt{h}\to+\infty}p_{\ell}^{[\ell]}=\lim_{\mathtt{h}\to+\infty}a_{\ell}^{[\ell]}=:\varpi_{\ell}\in\mathbb{Q}\,.

(3.52)

Proof.

Step 1
.
We now prove inductively that
for any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
we have

\eta_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;=\psi_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;=B_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;=V_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;\,.

(3.53)

The case
\ell=1
follows by comparison of formulas (
2.7
) with
[
8
, (B.12)-(B.13)]
.
Let us assume that (
3.53
)
holds up to
\ell-1
. We have

-c_{\epsilon}(\eta_{\epsilon})_{x}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{espVB}}}{{=}}B-(\eta_{\epsilon})_{x}V\,,\quad(\psi_{\epsilon})_{x}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{espVB}}}{{=}}V+(\eta_{\epsilon})_{x}B\,,\quad G(\eta_{\epsilon})\psi_{\epsilon}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{travelingWW}}}{{=}}-c_{\epsilon}(\eta_{\epsilon})_{x}\,.

(3.54)

The first relation
in (
3.54
) gives

-[\eta_{x}]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;=B_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;-[(\eta_{\epsilon})_{x}V]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;

and so,
by Lemma

3.4
,

\ell\eta_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;=B_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\ell_{1}\eta_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;V_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;=B_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\ell_{1}\eta_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;\eta_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;

(3.55)

by the inductive hypothesis.
Similarly,
the second relation
in (
3.54
) gives

\ell\psi_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;=V_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\ell_{1}\eta_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;B_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;=V_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\ell_{1}\eta_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;\eta_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;\,.

(3.56)

Subtracting
(
3.55
)-(
3.56
)
we deduce, since
\eta_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;=\psi_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;
,
that

V_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;=B_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;
.
We now claim that

(\ell-1)\eta_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\ell_{1}\eta_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;\eta_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;\,

(3.57)

which implies, via (
3.55
),
that
\eta_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;=B_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;
and then proves (
3.53
) at order
\ell
.

To prove claim (
3.57
), we observe that, by (
3.47
),

(\ell-1)\eta_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;=-f_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;\,,

(3.58)

where, using
(
3.31
), (
3.33
),
and Lemma

3.4
,

\displaystyle f_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Big[-(\psi_{\epsilon})_{x}^{2}+\frac{(\eta_{\epsilon})_{x}^{2}(c_{\epsilon}-(\psi_{\epsilon})_{x})^{2}}{1+(\eta_{\epsilon})_{x}^{2}}\Big]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{espVB}}}{{=}}\frac{1}{2}\Big[-(\psi_{\epsilon})_{x}^{2}+B^{2}(1+(\eta_{\epsilon})_{x}^{2})\Big]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{espVB}}}{{=}}\frac{1}{2}\Big[-(\psi_{\epsilon})_{x}^{2}+B^{2}+(V-(\psi_{\epsilon})_{x})^{2}\Big]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;=\frac{1}{2}\Big[B^{2}+V^{2}-2V(\psi_{\epsilon})_{x}\Big]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}-B_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;B_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;+V_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;V_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;-2\ell_{1}\psi_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;V_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}-2\ell_{1}\eta_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;\eta_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\ell_{1}\eta_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;\eta_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;

by the inductive assumption (
3.53
).
In view of (
3.58
)
this proves
(
3.57
) and concludes the proof of (
3.53
).

Step 2
. We finally prove (
3.52
).
We have

\displaystyle V_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{espVB}}}{{=}}\ell\psi_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;-\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\ell_{2}B_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;\eta_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;

(3.59)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{allequalsl}}}{{=}}\ell B_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;-\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell}\ell_{2}V_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;B_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;=\big[(c_{\epsilon}-V)B_{x}\big]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;\,.

By (
3.6
) we then have

\big[V\big(x+\mathfrak{p}(x)\big)\big]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;=\big[\big(c_{\epsilon}-V\big(x+\mathfrak{p}(x)\big)\big)B_{x}\big(x+\mathfrak{p}(x)\big)\big]_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\scalebox{0.7}{$(+\infty)$}\;\,,

which, in view of (
3.22
),
proves (
3.52
).
∎

4
Isolas of unstable eigenvalues

To analyize the splitting of the
double eigenvalue
\mathrm{i}\,\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})
of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\mu},0}
in (
2.34
) for

(\mu,\epsilon)
close to
(\underline{\mu},0)
,
we employ Kato’s similarity transformation
theory developed
in
[
6
,
8
,
10
]
and summarized below.
We recall that
{\mathcal{L}}_{\mu,\epsilon}:Y\subset X\to X

has domain
Y:=H^{1}(\mathbb{T}):=H^{1}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{2})
and range
X:=L^{2}(\mathbb{T}):=L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{2})
.

Lemma 4.1
.

(Kato theory for separated eigenvalues of Hamiltonian operators)

Fix
\mathtt{p}\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathtt{p}\geq 2
.
Let
\Gamma
be a closed counter-clocked wise oriented curve winding once around
the double eigenvalue

\mathrm{i}\,\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})
in (
2.34
) of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\mu},0}
,
separating
\sigma_{\mathtt{p}}^{\prime}({\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\mu},0})

from the other part of the spectrum
\sigma_{\mathtt{p}}^{\prime\prime}({\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\mu},0})
.
Then there exist
\epsilon_{0},\delta_{0}>0
such that for any
(\mu,\epsilon)\in B_{\delta_{0}}(\underline{\mu})\times B_{\epsilon_{0}}(0)
the following hold.

1.
The curve
\Gamma
belongs to the resolvent set of
the operator
{\mathcal{L}}_{\mu,\epsilon}:Y\subset X\to X
defined in (
2.12
).
The operators

P(\mu,\epsilon):=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}\,}\oint_{\Gamma}({\mathcal{L}}_{\mu,\epsilon}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathrm{d}\lambda:X\to Y

(4.1)

are projectors commuting with
{\mathcal{L}}_{\mu,\epsilon}
on
Y
, i.e.
P(\mu,\epsilon)^{2}=P(\mu,\epsilon)
and

P(\mu,\epsilon){\mathcal{L}}_{\mu,\epsilon}[y]={\mathcal{L}}_{\mu,\epsilon}P(\mu,\epsilon)[y]\,,\quad\forall y\in Y\,.

(4.2)

The map
(\mu,\epsilon)\mapsto P(\mu,\epsilon)
is
analytic from
B_{\delta_{0}}(\underline{\mu})\times B_{\epsilon_{0}}(0)
to
{\mathcal{L}}(X,Y)
.
The projectors
P(\mu,\epsilon)
are
skew-Hamiltonian and reversibility preserving, i.e.

\mathcal{J}P(\mu,\epsilon)=P(\mu,\epsilon)^{*}\mathcal{J}\,,\quad\overline{\rho}P(\mu,\epsilon)=P(\mu,\epsilon)\overline{\rho}\,.

(4.3)

2.
The domain
Y
of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\mu,\epsilon}
decomposes as the direct sum

Y=\mathcal{V}_{\mu,\epsilon}\oplus\mathcal{K}_{\mu,\epsilon}
of the closed
subspaces

\mathcal{V}_{\mu,\epsilon}:=\text{Rg}(P(\mu,\epsilon))
,

\mathcal{K}_{\mu,\epsilon}:=\text{Ker}(P(\mu,\epsilon))\cap Y
,
which are invariant under
{\mathcal{L}}_{\mu,\epsilon}
, and

\displaystyle\sigma({\mathcal{L}}_{\mu,\epsilon})\cap\{z\in\mathbb{C}\mbox{ inside }\Gamma\}=\sigma({\mathcal{L}}_{\mu,\epsilon}|_{{\mathcal{V}}_{\mu,\epsilon}})=\sigma^{\prime}({\mathcal{L}}_{\mu,\epsilon})\,.

3. The projectors
P(\mu,\epsilon)

are similar one to each other: the transformation operators

U_{\mu,\epsilon}:=\big(\mathrm{Id}-(P(\mu,\epsilon)-P(\underline{\mu},0))^{2}\big)^{-1/2}\big[P(\mu,\epsilon)P(\underline{\mu},0)+(\mathrm{Id}-P(\mu,\epsilon))(\mathrm{Id}-P(\underline{\mu},0))\big]

(4.4)

are bounded and invertible in both
Y
and
X
,
and

U_{\mu,\epsilon}P(\underline{\mu},0)U_{\mu,\epsilon}^{-1}=P(\mu,\epsilon)
.
The map
(\mu,\epsilon)\mapsto U_{\mu,\epsilon}
is analytic from

B_{\delta_{0}}(\underline{\mu})\times B_{\epsilon_{0}}(0)
to
{\mathcal{L}}\big(Y\big)
.
The transformation operators
U_{\mu,\epsilon}
are symplectic and reversibility preserving, namely

U_{\mu,\epsilon}^{*}\mathcal{J}U_{\mu,\epsilon}=\mathcal{J}\,,\qquad\overline{\rho}U_{\mu,\epsilon}=U_{\mu,\epsilon}\overline{\rho}\,.

(4.5)

Finally

\mathcal{V}_{\mu,\epsilon}=U_{\mu,\epsilon}\mathcal{V}_{\underline{\mu},0}

and
\dim\mathcal{V}_{\mu,\epsilon}=\dim\mathcal{V}_{\underline{\mu},0}=2
for any

(\mu,\epsilon)\in B_{\delta_{0}}(\underline{\mu})\times B_{\epsilon_{0}}(0)
.

We consider the basis of the subspace

{\mathcal{V}}_{\mu,\epsilon}=\mathrm{Rg}(P(\mu,\epsilon))
,

{\mathcal{F}}:=\{f^{+}(\mu,\epsilon),f^{-}(\mu,\epsilon)\}\,,\quad f^{+}(\mu,\epsilon):=U_{\mu,\epsilon}f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+},\ \ f^{-}(\mu,\epsilon):=U_{\mu,\epsilon}f_{0}^{-}\,,

(4.6)

obtained applying the transformation operators
U_{\mu,\epsilon}
in (
4.4
)
to the eigenvectors
f_{0}^{-}:=f_{0}^{-}(\underline{\mu},\mathtt{h})
and

f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+}:=f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+}(\underline{\mu},\mathtt{h})

of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\mu},0}
defined in (
2.32
), which are a symplectic and reversible basis of

\mathcal{V}_{\underline{\mu},0}=\mathrm{Rg}(P(\underline{\mu},0))
.
Thanks to (
4.5
)
and (
2.23
)
the basis

{\mathcal{F}}
is symplectic and reversible as well, namely

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{W}}_{c}\big(f^{+}(\mu,\epsilon),f^{+}(\mu,\epsilon)\big)=-\mathrm{i}\,\,,\quad{\mathcal{W}}_{c}\big(f^{-}(\mu,\epsilon),f^{-}(\mu,\epsilon)\big)=\mathrm{i}\,\,,\quad{\mathcal{W}}_{c}\big(f^{+}(\mu,\epsilon),f^{-}(\mu,\epsilon)\big)=0\,,

and
\overline{\rho}f^{\sigma}(\mu,\epsilon)=\sigma f^{\sigma}(\mu,\epsilon)
for any
\sigma=\pm
.
This directly implies the following lemma, cfr.
[
10
, Lemma 2.3]
.

Lemma 4.2
.

(Matrix representation of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\mu,\epsilon}
on
\mathcal{V}_{\mu,\epsilon}
)

For any integer
\mathtt{p}\geq 2
,
the operator
{\mathcal{L}}_{\mu,\epsilon}:\mathcal{V}_{\mu,\epsilon}\to\mathcal{V}_{\mu,\epsilon}
in
(
2.12
)
is represented w.r.t. the basis
{\mathcal{F}}
in (
4.6
) by the
2\times 2
Hamiltonian and reversible matrix

\mathtt{L}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)=\mathtt{J}\mathtt{B}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon),\ \mathtt{J}:=\begin{pmatrix}-\mathrm{i}\,&0\\
0&\mathrm{i}\,\end{pmatrix}\,,\quad\forall(\mu,\epsilon)\in B_{\delta_{0}}(\underline{\mu})\times B_{\epsilon_{0}}(0)\,,

(4.7)

where

\mathtt{B}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)=\begin{pmatrix}(\mathfrak{B}(\mu,\epsilon)f^{+}_{\mathtt{p}},f^{+}_{\mathtt{p}})&(\mathfrak{B}(\mu,\epsilon)f^{-}_{0},f^{+}_{\mathtt{p}})\\
(\mathfrak{B}(\mu,\epsilon)f^{+}_{\mathtt{p}},f^{-}_{0})&(\mathfrak{B}(\mu,\epsilon)f^{-}_{0},f^{-}_{0})\end{pmatrix}=:\begin{pmatrix}\alpha^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)&\mathrm{i}\,\beta^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)\\
-\mathrm{i}\,\beta^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)&\gamma^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)\end{pmatrix}\,,

(4.8)

with

\mathfrak{B}(\mu,\epsilon):=\mathfrak{B}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon):=[P_{\underline{\mu},0}]^{*}\,[U_{\mu,\epsilon}]^{*}\,{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)\,U_{\mu,\epsilon}\,P_{\underline{\mu},0}\,.

(4.9)

It results

\mathtt{L}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,0)=\begin{pmatrix}\mathrm{i}\,\omega_{\mathtt{p}}^{+}(\mu)&0\\
0&\mathrm{i}\,\omega_{0}^{-}(\mu)\end{pmatrix},\quad\text{i.e.}\quad\mathtt{B}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,0)=\begin{pmatrix}-\omega_{\mathtt{p}}^{+}(\mu)&0\\
0&\omega_{0}^{-}(\mu)\end{pmatrix}\,,

(4.10)

where

\omega_{j}^{\sigma}(\mu):=\omega^{\sigma}(j+\mu,\mathtt{h})
is in (
2.18
) and

\omega_{\mathtt{p}}^{+}(\underline{\mu})=\omega_{0}^{-}(\underline{\mu})=\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})
in (
2.33
).
Finally the functions
\alpha^{(\mathtt{p})},\beta^{(\mathtt{p})},\gamma^{(\mathtt{p})}

are analytic w.r.t.
(\mu,\epsilon)\in B_{\delta_{0}}(\underline{\mu})\times B_{\epsilon_{0}}(0)
.

Remark 4.3
.

The functions
\alpha^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon),\beta^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)
and
\gamma^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)

are also analytic
w.r.t.
\mathtt{h}>\mathtt{h}_{*}

for any
\mathtt{h}_{*}>0
,
provided
\epsilon
is small
(depending on
\mathtt{h}_{*}
). This analyticity property
is inherited
from the projectors
P(\mu,\epsilon)

and the similarity operators

U_{\mu,\epsilon}
of Lemma
4.1
which are
are themselves analytic in
\mathtt{h}>0
due to the properties of

{\mathcal{L}}_{\mu,\epsilon}(\mathtt{h})
, as detailed in Remark
2.6
.

The eigenvalues of the matrix
\mathtt{L}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)

in (
4.7
) are

\lambda^{\pm}(\mu,\epsilon)=\tfrac{\mathrm{i}\,}{2}S^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)\pm\tfrac{1}{2}\sqrt{D^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)}

(4.11)

where

\displaystyle S^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon):=-\mathrm{i}\,\text{Tr}\,\mathtt{L}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)=\gamma^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)-\alpha^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)\,,

(4.12)

\displaystyle D^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon):=\text{Tr}^{2}\mathtt{L}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)-4\det\mathtt{L}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)=4(\beta^{(\mathtt{p})})^{2}(\mu,\epsilon)-(T^{(\mathtt{p})})^{2}(\mu,\epsilon)\,,

(4.13)

with

T^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon):=\text{Tr}\,\mathtt{B}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)=\alpha^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)+\gamma^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)\,.

In the following
sections we will prove
the existence of
eigenvalues of the matrix
\mathtt{L}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)
with nonzero real part when
(\mu,\epsilon)
is close to
(\underline{\mu},0)
.
We will apply the following abstract results.

Abstract instability criterion.

Consider a
2\times 2
Hamiltonian and reversible matrix

\mathtt{L}(\mu,\epsilon):=\mathtt{J}\mathtt{B}(\mu,\epsilon),\quad\mathtt{B}(\mu,\epsilon):=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha(\mu,\epsilon)&\mathrm{i}\,\beta(\mu,\epsilon)\\
-\mathrm{i}\,\beta(\mu,\epsilon)&\gamma(\mu,\epsilon)\end{pmatrix}\,,

(4.14)

where
\alpha(\mu,\epsilon),\beta(\mu,\epsilon),\gamma(\mu,\epsilon)

satisfy the following

Assumption 4.4
.

The functions
\alpha(\mu,\epsilon)
,
\beta(\mu,\epsilon)
,
\gamma(\mu,\epsilon)
are
real analytic
in the domain
B_{\delta_{0}}(\underline{\mu})\times B_{\epsilon_{0}}(0)

and admit the expansions, for some integer
\mathtt{p}\geq 2

\displaystyle\alpha(\underline{\mu}+\delta,\epsilon)

\displaystyle=-\alpha_{0}(\delta)+\alpha_{2}\epsilon^{2}+r(\epsilon^{4},\delta\epsilon^{2})\,,

(4.15a)

\displaystyle\beta(\underline{\mu}+\delta,\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\beta_{1}\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}}+\beta_{2}\delta\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}}+r(\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}+2},\delta^{2}\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}})\,,

(4.15b)

\displaystyle\gamma(\underline{\mu}+\delta,\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\gamma_{0}(\delta)+\gamma_{2}\epsilon^{2}+r(\epsilon^{4},\delta\epsilon^{2})\,,

(4.15c)

where

\alpha_{0}(\delta)=\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})}-\alpha_{1}\delta+r(\delta^{2})\,,\qquad\gamma_{0}(\delta)=\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})}+\gamma_{1}\delta+r(\delta^{2})\,,

(4.16)

and

\alpha_{1}+\gamma_{1}>0\,.

(4.17)

We shall prove in Section
5
that
for any
\mathtt{p}\geq 2
the matrix
\mathtt{L}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)
in (
4.7
)
satisfies Assumption
4.4
.

The matrix

\mathtt{L}(\mu,\epsilon)
has eigenvalues with non zero real part if and only if the discriminant

\displaystyle D(\mu,\epsilon)=4\beta^{2}(\mu,\epsilon)-T^{2}(\mu,\epsilon)

\displaystyle=-d_{+}(\mu,\epsilon)d_{-}(\mu,\epsilon)

(4.18)

\displaystyle\text{where}\quad d_{\pm}(\mu,\epsilon):=T(\mu,\epsilon)\pm 2\beta(\mu,\epsilon)

is positive. This holds if

d_{\pm}(\mu,\epsilon)
have different signs and

\beta(\mu,\epsilon)
is large enough with respect to the
trace of
T(\mu,\epsilon):=\alpha(\mu,\epsilon)+\gamma(\mu,\epsilon)
.
Let us analyze these quantities.

By Assumption
4.4

the trace

T(\mu,\epsilon)=\alpha(\mu,\epsilon)+\gamma(\mu,\epsilon)
admits the expansion

\displaystyle T(\underline{\mu}+\delta,\epsilon)=T(\underline{\mu}+\delta,0)+T_{2}\epsilon^{2}+r(\epsilon^{4},\delta\epsilon^{2})\quad\text{where}\quad T_{2}:=\alpha_{2}+\gamma_{2}\quad\text{and}

(4.19)

\displaystyle T(\underline{\mu}+\delta,0)=T_{1}\delta+r(\delta^{2})\quad\text{where}\quad T_{1}:=\alpha_{1}+\gamma_{1}>0\,.

By (
4.19
) and the analytic implicit function theorem there exists
\epsilon_{1}\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
such that, in

B_{\delta_{0}}(\underline{\mu})\times B_{\epsilon_{1}}(0)
,
the sets
\{T(\mu,\epsilon)=0\}

and

\{d_{\pm}(\mu,\epsilon)=0\}

are
graphs of analytic functions

\mu_{0}\,,\mu_{\pm}:(-\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{1})\mapsto\mu_{0}(\epsilon)\,,\ \mu_{\pm}(\epsilon)\,,\ \text{i.e.}\ T(\mu_{0}(\epsilon),\epsilon)\equiv 0\ \text{ and }\ d_{\pm}(\mu_{\pm}(\epsilon),\epsilon)\equiv 0\,,

(4.20)

admitting, by (
4.19
),
the Taylor expansions

\mu_{0}(\epsilon)=\underline{\mu}-\frac{T_{2}}{T_{1}}\epsilon^{2}+r(\epsilon^{4})\,,\quad\mu_{\pm}(0)=\underline{\mu}\,,\ \mu_{\pm}^{\prime}(0)=0\,.

(4.21)

Lemma
4.6
proves that the functions
\mu_{\pm}(\epsilon)
are
actually
\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}})
-close to
\mu_{0}(\epsilon)
.

Since
T_{1}>0
(cfr. (
4.19
)), the functions
d_{\pm}(\mu,\epsilon)
are strictly positive (resp. negative) for
\mu>\mu_{\pm}(\epsilon)
(resp.
\mu<\mu_{\pm}(\epsilon)
). In addition, since
d_{\pm}(\mu_{0}(\epsilon),\epsilon)=\pm 2\beta(\mu_{0}(\epsilon),\epsilon)
we deduce that for any
\epsilon\in(-\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{1})

\begin{cases}\mbox{if }\beta(\mu_{0}(\epsilon),\epsilon)>0&\mbox{then }\ \mu_{+}(\epsilon)<\mu_{0}(\epsilon)<\mu_{-}(\epsilon)\,,\\
\mbox{if }\beta(\mu_{0}(\epsilon),\epsilon)<0&\mbox{then }\ \mu_{-}(\epsilon)<\mu_{0}(\epsilon)<\mu_{+}(\epsilon)\,,\\
\mbox{if }\beta(\mu_{0}(\epsilon),\epsilon)=0&\mbox{then }\ \mu_{0}(\epsilon)=\mu_{+}(\epsilon)=\mu_{-}(\epsilon)\,.\end{cases}

(4.22)

The graphs of these functions are represented in Figure
2
.
By (
4.15b
)
and the expansion of
\mu_{0}(\epsilon)
in (
4.21
),
we have

\beta(\mu_{0}(\epsilon),\epsilon)=\beta_{1}\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}}+r(\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}+2})\,.

(4.23)

Thus, if
\beta_{1}\neq 0

the function
\beta(\mu_{0}(\epsilon),\epsilon)

is not identically zero
and only the first two cases
in (
4.22
) occur so that, for
|\epsilon|<\epsilon_{1}
, we have

\mu_{\wedge}(\epsilon)<\mu_{0}(\epsilon)<\mu_{\vee}(\epsilon)

where

\mu_{\wedge}(\epsilon):=\min\big\{\mu_{+}(\epsilon),\mu_{-}(\epsilon)\big\}\quad\text{and}\quad\mu_{\vee}(\epsilon):=\max\big\{\mu_{+}(\epsilon),\mu_{-}(\epsilon)\big\}\,.

(4.24)

Theorem 4.5
.

Assume that the
2\times 2
Hamiltonian and reversible
matrix
\mathtt{L}(\mu,\epsilon)=\mathtt{J}\mathtt{B}(\mu,\epsilon)
in (
4.14
)
satisfies Assumption
4.4
, and that the coefficient
\beta_{1}
in (
4.15b
) is not zero.
Then
the spectrum of
\,\mathtt{L}(\mu,\epsilon)
, for
(\mu,\epsilon)
close to
(\underline{\mu},0)
, consists of two eigenvalues
\lambda^{\pm}(\mu,\epsilon)
with non-zero real part
if and only if
(\mu,\epsilon)
lies inside
the open region

\displaystyle R:

\displaystyle=\big\{(\mu,\epsilon)\in B_{\delta_{0}}(\underline{\mu})\times B_{\epsilon_{1}}(0)\ :\ \mu_{\wedge}(\epsilon)<\mu<\mu_{\vee}(\epsilon)\big\}\,,

(4.25)

whereas, for
(\mu,\epsilon)\notin R
, the eigenvalues
\lambda^{\pm}(\mu,\epsilon)
are purely imaginary,
see Figure
2
.

Proof.

The eigenvalues of
\mathtt{L}(\mu,\epsilon)

have the form (
4.11
)
and have nonzero real part if and only if

D(\mu,\epsilon)>0
, cfr.(
4.18
),
which happens if and only if
(\mu,\epsilon)\in R
.
∎

It is convenient to
translate the Floquet exponent

\mu
around the value
\mu_{0}(\epsilon)
where
T(\mu_{0}(\epsilon),\epsilon)\equiv 0
, cfr. (
4.20
), introducing the new parameter
\nu
such that

\mu=\mu_{0}(\epsilon)+\nu\,,\quad\text{i.e.}\quad\nu:=\delta+\underline{\mu}-\mu_{0}(\epsilon)\,.

(4.26)

Accordingly we write the functions
\mu_{\pm}(\epsilon)
in (
4.21
) as

\mu_{\pm}(\epsilon)=\mu_{0}(\epsilon)+\nu_{\pm}(\epsilon)
.

Lemma 4.6
.

The analytic functions
\nu_{\pm}(\epsilon):=\mu_{\pm}(\epsilon)-\mu_{0}(\epsilon)
admit the expansion

\nu_{\pm}(\epsilon)=\mp\frac{2\beta_{1}}{T_{1}}\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}}+r(\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}+2})\,.

(4.27)

Proof.

The functions
\nu_{\pm}(\epsilon)
solve

d_{\pm}(\mu_{0}(\epsilon)+\nu_{\pm}(\epsilon),\epsilon)=T(\mu_{0}(\epsilon)+\nu_{\pm}(\epsilon),\epsilon)\pm 2\beta(\mu_{0}(\epsilon)+\nu_{\pm}(\epsilon),\epsilon)=0\,.

Expanding this identity at
\mu=\mu_{0}(\epsilon)
we get

\nu_{\pm}(\epsilon)=\mp\frac{2\beta(\mu_{0}(\epsilon),\epsilon)}{(\partial_{\mu}T)(\mu_{0}(\epsilon))-2(\partial_{\mu}\beta)(\mu_{0}(\epsilon))}+r(\nu_{\pm}^{2})(1+r(\epsilon))\,,

and since

(\partial_{\mu}T)(\mu_{0}(\epsilon))-2(\partial_{\mu}\beta)(\mu_{0}(\epsilon))=T_{1}(1+r(\epsilon))

(cfr. (
4.19
), (
4.15b
)) we deduce, by (
4.23
),
(
4.27
).
∎

We define

\nu_{\wedge}(\epsilon):=\min\{\nu_{+}(\epsilon),\nu_{-}(\epsilon)\}<0\,,\quad\nu_{\vee}(\epsilon):=\max\{\nu_{+}(\epsilon),\nu_{-}(\epsilon)\}>0\,.

(4.28)

Note that
\nu_{\wedge}(\epsilon)
and
\nu_{\vee}(\epsilon)
are the points where the discriminant
D(\mu_{0}(\epsilon)+\nu,\epsilon)
in (
4.18
)
vanishes, whereas for
\nu_{\wedge}(\epsilon)<\nu<\nu_{\vee}(\epsilon)
it results

D(\mu_{0}(\epsilon)+\nu,\epsilon)>0
.

Theorem 4.7

(
Unstable eigenvalues).

Under Assumption
4.4

the matrix
\mathtt{L}(\mu,\epsilon)
in (
4.14
) has the two
eigenvalues

\lambda^{\pm}(\mu_{0}(\epsilon)+\nu,\epsilon)=\begin{cases}\frac{\mathrm{i}\,}{2}S(\mu_{0}(\epsilon)+\nu,\epsilon)\pm\frac{\mathrm{i}\,}{2}\sqrt{|D(\mu_{0}(\epsilon)+\nu,\epsilon)|}&\text{if }\nu\leq\nu_{\wedge}(\epsilon)\textup{ or }\nu\geq\nu_{\vee}(\epsilon)\,,\\[5.69054pt]
\;\frac{\mathrm{i}\,}{2}S(\mu_{0}(\epsilon)+\nu,\epsilon)\pm\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{D(\mu_{0}(\epsilon)+\nu,\epsilon)}&\text{if }\nu_{\wedge}(\epsilon)<\nu<\nu_{\vee}(\epsilon)\,,\end{cases}

(4.29)

where
\mu_{0}(\epsilon)
is defined in (
4.20
) and

\displaystyle D(\mu_{0}(\epsilon)+\nu,\epsilon)

\displaystyle=4\beta_{1}^{2}\epsilon^{2\mathtt{p}}-T_{1}^{2}\nu^{2}+8\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\epsilon^{2\mathtt{p}}\nu+r({\beta_{1}\epsilon^{2\mathtt{p}+2},\nu\epsilon^{2\mathtt{p}+2}},\epsilon^{{2\mathtt{p}+{4}}},\nu^{2}\epsilon^{{2}},\nu^{3})

(4.30)

\displaystyle S(\mu_{0}(\epsilon)+\nu,\epsilon)

\displaystyle=2\omega_{*}+(\gamma_{1}-\alpha_{1})\nu+\Big(\gamma_{2}-\alpha_{2}-(\gamma_{1}-\alpha_{1})\frac{T_{2}}{T_{1}}\Big)\epsilon^{2}+r(\epsilon^{4},\nu\epsilon^{2},\nu^{2})\,.

(4.31)

The maximal absolute value of the real part
of the
eigenvalues in (
4.29
) is

\max\mathfrak{Re}\,\lambda^{\pm}(\mu_{0}(\epsilon)+\nu,\epsilon)=|\beta_{1}|\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}}+r(\epsilon^{{\mathtt{p}+2}})\,.

(4.32)

(Isola)
.

If the coefficients
\beta_{1},\alpha_{1},\gamma_{1}
in (
4.15a
)-(
4.15c
)
satisfy
\beta_{1}\neq 0
and
\alpha_{1}\neq\gamma_{1}
,
then, for
\epsilon
small enough,
the pair of unstable eigenvalues
\lambda^{\pm}(\mu_{0}(\epsilon)+\nu,\epsilon)
depicts in the complex
\lambda
-plane, as
\nu
varies
in
(\nu_{\wedge}(\epsilon),\nu_{\vee}(\epsilon))
a closed analytic curve that
intersects orthogonally the imaginary axis
and encircles a convex region.

Proof.

In view of (
4.15b
), (
4.21
) and (
4.26
),

\beta(\mu_{0}(\epsilon)+\nu,\epsilon)=\beta_{1}\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}}+\beta_{2}\nu\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}}+r(\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}+{2}},\nu^{2}\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}})\,.

(4.33)

We now prove the expansion (
4.30
) of the discriminant, cfr.
(
4.18
),

D(\mu_{0}(\epsilon)+\nu,\epsilon)=4\beta^{2}(\mu_{0}(\epsilon)+\nu,\epsilon)-T^{2}(\mu_{0}(\epsilon)+\nu,\epsilon)\,.

(4.34)

By a Taylor expansion at
\nu=0
, (
4.20
), (
4.19
), (
4.21
) and (
4.26
) we get that

T(\mu_{0}(\epsilon)+\nu,\epsilon)=(\partial_{\mu}T)(\mu_{0}(\epsilon),\epsilon)\nu+r(\nu^{2})=T_{1}\nu+r(\epsilon^{{2}}\nu,\nu^{2})

(4.35)

and the expansion (
4.30
) follows by inserting
(
4.35
) and (
4.33
) into (
4.34
).
By (
4.11
) and (
4.26
), the eigenvalues of
\mathtt{L}(\mu,\epsilon)

have the form (
4.29
).
The expansion (
4.31
) follows from (
4.15a
)-(
4.15c
), (
4.19
) and (
4.21
).
The (opposite) extremal real parts of the eigenvalues are attained at

\nu=\nu_{\mathfrak{Re}}
where
(\partial_{\mu}D)(\mu_{0}(\epsilon)+\nu_{\mathfrak{Re}},\epsilon)=0
.
By (
4.30
) we have the expansion

\nu_{\mathfrak{Re}}(\epsilon)=4\frac{\beta_{1}\beta_{2}}{T_{1}^{2}}\epsilon^{2\mathtt{p}}+r(\epsilon^{2\mathtt{p}+{2}})\,.

(4.36)

By plugging (
4.36
) into (
4.30
)-(
4.29
) one obtains (
4.32
), that holds if
\beta_{1}
is different or equal to zero.

(Isola).

In view of (
4.29
),
for any
\epsilon
small enough,
the unstable eigenvalues branch off from the imaginary axis at
\nu=\nu_{\wedge}(\epsilon)
, evolve specularly as
\nu
increases and rejoin at
\nu=\nu_{\vee}(\epsilon)
thus forming a
closed
curve. If
\alpha_{1}\neq\gamma_{1}
,
the imaginary part of the eigenvalues

I(\nu,\epsilon):=\mathfrak{Im}\,\lambda^{\pm}(\mu_{0}(\epsilon)+\nu,\epsilon)

is monotone w.r.t.
\nu\in\big(\nu_{\wedge}(\epsilon),\nu_{\vee}(\epsilon)\big)
for small
\epsilon
, since
its derivative fulfills

\partial_{\nu}I(\nu,\epsilon)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eigs}}}{{=}}\frac{\gamma_{1}-\alpha_{1}}{2}+r(\epsilon^{2},\nu)\neq 0\,,\qquad\nu_{\wedge}(\epsilon)\leq\nu\leq\nu_{\vee}(\epsilon)\,.

Thus
\nu\mapsto I(\nu,\epsilon)
is a diffeomorphism between the intervals
\big(\nu_{\wedge}(\epsilon),\nu_{\vee}(\epsilon)\big)
and
\big(y_{\wedge}(\epsilon),y_{\vee}(\epsilon)\big)
where

y_{\wedge}(\epsilon):=\min\big\{I(\nu_{\wedge}(\epsilon),\epsilon)\,,I(\nu_{\vee}(\epsilon),\epsilon)\big\}\,,\quad y_{\vee}(\epsilon):=\max\big\{I(\nu_{\wedge}(\epsilon),\epsilon)\,,I(\nu_{\vee}(\epsilon),\epsilon)\big\}\,.

Let
\nu(y,\epsilon)
denote the inverse of
y=I(\nu,\epsilon)
, with
y
varying in
y_{\wedge}(\epsilon)<y<y_{\vee}(\epsilon)
.
The curves depicted by the two unstable eigenvalues in the complex plane is hence covered by the two specular graphs

\Gamma_{r}:=\big\{\big(X(y,\epsilon),y\big)\;:\;y_{\wedge}(\epsilon)<y<y_{\vee}(\epsilon)\big\}\,,\quad\Gamma_{l}:=\big\{\big(-X(y,\epsilon),y\big)\;:\;y_{\wedge}(\epsilon)<y<y_{\vee}(\epsilon)\big\}\,,

where

X(y,\epsilon):=\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{D\big(\mu(y,\epsilon),\epsilon\big)}\,,\quad\mu(y,\epsilon):=\mu_{0}(\epsilon)+\nu(y,\epsilon)\,.

It is easy to check
that at the bottom and top of the isola, i.e. at
y=y_{\wedge}(\epsilon)
and
y=y_{\vee}(\epsilon)
, the real parts

\pm X(y,\epsilon)

of the unstable eigenvalues vanish with derivative that tends to infinity,
and that
the region encircled by the two graphs is convex.
∎

An approximation of
the eigenvalues

\lambda^{\pm}(\mu_{0}(\epsilon)+\nu,\epsilon)

in Theorem
4.7
is obtained
neglecting the remainders
r(\nu^{3})
of
D(\mu_{0}(\epsilon)+\nu,\epsilon)
in (
4.30
) and
r(\nu^{2})
of
S(\mu_{0}(\epsilon)+\nu,\epsilon)
in (
4.31
), namely by the complex numbers
\widetilde{\lambda}^{\pm}(\nu,\epsilon)
with

\begin{cases}x:=\mathfrak{Re}\ \widetilde{\lambda}^{\pm}(\nu,\epsilon)=\pm\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{4\beta_{1}^{2}\epsilon^{2\mathtt{p}}(1+r(\epsilon^{{2}}))-T_{1}^{2}\nu^{2}(1+r(\epsilon^{{2}}))+8\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\nu\epsilon^{2\mathtt{p}}}\,,\\
y:=\mathfrak{Im}\ \widetilde{\lambda}^{\pm}(\nu,\epsilon)=\omega_{*}+\Big(\dfrac{\gamma_{2}-\alpha_{2}}{2}-\dfrac{T_{2}(\gamma_{1}-\alpha_{1})}{2T_{1}}\Big)\epsilon^{2}(1+r(\epsilon^{{2}}))+\dfrac{\gamma_{1}-\alpha_{1}}{2}\nu(1+r(\epsilon^{2}))\,.\end{cases}

(4.37)

The functions
\widetilde{\lambda}^{\pm}(\nu,\epsilon)
are defined for
\nu
in
the interval
\widetilde{\nu}_{\wedge}(\epsilon)\leq\nu\leq\widetilde{\nu}_{\vee}(\epsilon)
where the argument of the square root in (
4.37
) is non-negative.
These approximated eigenvalues describe an ellipse in the
(x,y)
-plane as stated in the following lemma which directly
follows
from (
4.37
).

Lemma 4.8
.

(Approximating ellipse)

Suppose the coefficients
\beta_{1},\alpha_{1},\gamma_{1}
in (
4.15a
)-(
4.15c
)
satisfy
\beta_{1}\neq 0
and
\alpha_{1}\neq\gamma_{1}
.
Then, as
\nu
varies between
\widetilde{\nu}_{\wedge}(\epsilon)
and
\widetilde{\nu}_{\vee}(\epsilon)

the approximated eigenvalues
\widetilde{\lambda}^{\pm}(\nu,\epsilon)
in
(
4.37
)
span an ellipse of equation

x^{2}+\dfrac{T_{1}^{2}(1+r(\epsilon^{2}))}{(\gamma_{1}-\alpha_{1})^{2}}(y-y_{0}(\epsilon))^{2}=\beta_{1}^{2}\epsilon^{2\mathtt{p}}(1+r(\epsilon^{{2}}))\,,

centered at
(0,y_{0}(\epsilon))
where

y_{0}(\epsilon)=\omega_{*}+\Big(\dfrac{\gamma_{2}-\alpha_{2}}{2}-\dfrac{T_{2}(\gamma_{1}-\alpha_{1})}{2T_{1}}\Big)\epsilon^{2}+r(\epsilon^{4})
.

5
Taylor expansion of
\mathtt{L}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)

This section is dedicated to demonstrating that the entries of the matrix
\mathtt{L}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)
given by (
4.7
)
admit a Taylor expansion as stated in
Assumption
4.4
for any integer
\mathtt{p}\geq 2
.
This is proved in Theorem
5.3
.
To begin, we introduce some notation.

Notation 5.1
.

Given a family of linear
operators

A=A(\mu,\epsilon)
,
analytic in
(\mu,\epsilon)
, we define, for any

i,n,\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, its jets
A_{i,n}
and its homogenous component
A_{\ell}
of degree
\ell
,

A_{i,n}:=\frac{1}{i!n!}\big(\partial^{i}_{\mu}\partial^{n}_{\epsilon}A\big)(\underline{\mu},0)\delta^{i}\epsilon^{n}\qquad\text{and}\qquad A_{\ell}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i+n=\ell,\ \atop i,n\geq 0\end{subarray}}\!\!\!A_{i,n}\,.

(5.1)

Matrix representation.

In the sequel we identify a linear
(possibly unbounded)
operator
A
acting on
C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C})
as the infinite matrix
\{A^{j}_{k}\}_{j,k\in\mathbb{Z}}
with respect to the exponential basis, namely
with
matrix entries

A^{j}_{k}:=(Ae^{\mathrm{i}\,jx},\ e^{\mathrm{i}\,kx})_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\,,\quad(f,g)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}}f(x)\overline{g(x)}\,dx\,.

(5.2)

Thus, in purely algebraic terms,
the action of
A
is given by

h(x)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}h_{j}e^{\mathrm{i}\,jx}\mapsto(Ah)(x)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}A^{j}_{k}\,h_{j}\Big)\,e^{\mathrm{i}\,kx}\,.

We do not concern about issues of convergence
as we will solely deal with finitely many matrix entries.

Given
\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}
, we define its
“
\kappa
-band”

A^{[\kappa]}
as the operator

A^{[\kappa]}\equiv\{A^{j}_{k}\}_{k-j=\kappa}

with matrix coefficients supported on the
“band”
k-j=\kappa
. In other words the action of

A^{[\kappa]}
is to “shift
the exponential
e^{\mathrm{i}\,jx}
of

\kappa
harmonics”, namely

A^{[\kappa]}(e^{\mathrm{i}\,jx})=A^{j}_{j+\kappa}e^{\mathrm{i}\,(j+\kappa)x}\,.

(5.3)

If
A=\begin{bmatrix}A_{1}&A_{2}\\
A_{3}&A_{4}\end{bmatrix}
is a
2\times 2
matrix of operators acting on

C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{2})

we define its
\kappa
-band as the
operator

A^{[\kappa]}:=\begin{bmatrix}A^{[\kappa]}_{1}&A^{[\kappa]}_{2}\\
A^{[\kappa]}_{3}&A^{[\kappa]}_{4}\end{bmatrix}.

(5.4)

We also introduce the following “entanglement coefficients”
which are matrix entries of the jets

{\mathcal{B}}_{0,\ell}^{[\kappa]}
of the operator

{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)
with respect to
the basis of eigenvectors
f_{j}^{\sigma}(\underline{\mu})
of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\mu},0}
.

Definition 5.2

(
entanglement coefficients).

For any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, integers
\kappa,j,j^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}
and signs
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}=\pm
,
we define the entanglement coefficients

\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\ell}^{[\kappa]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{j^{\prime},j}^{\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}\end{matrix}:=\big({\mathcal{B}}_{0,\ell}^{[\kappa]}f_{j}^{\sigma},f_{j^{\prime}}^{\sigma^{\prime}}\big)

(5.5)

where
{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)
is the operator in (
2.12
)
and

f_{j}^{\sigma}:=f_{j}^{\sigma}(\underline{\mu})
are the eigenvectors of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\mu},0}
in
(
2.21
).

By (
5.1
), (
5.16
) and the self-adjointness of
{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)
, the entanglement coefficients
fulfill

\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\ell}^{[\kappa]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{j^{\prime},j}^{\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}\end{matrix}=0\quad\text{ if }j^{\prime}\neq j+\kappa\,,\quad\text{ and }\quad\overline{\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\ell}^{[\kappa]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{j^{\prime},j}^{\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}\end{matrix}}=\ \begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\ell}^{[-\kappa]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{j,j^{\prime}}^{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}}\end{matrix}\,.

(5.6)

We further denote

\omega_{j}^{\sigma}:=\omega_{j}^{\sigma}(\mathtt{h}):=\omega^{\sigma}\big(j+\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h}),\mathtt{h}\big)\,,\quad j\in\mathbb{Z}\,,\ \sigma=\pm\,,

(5.7)

where
\omega^{\sigma}(\varphi,\mathtt{h})
are defined in (
2.19
)
and

\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})
in Lemma
2.7
. We set

\displaystyle\Omega_{j}^{(\mathtt{p})}:=\Omega_{j}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h}):=\sqrt{(j+\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h}))\tanh(\mathtt{h}(j+\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})))}\,,

(5.8)

\displaystyle t_{j}^{(\mathtt{p})}:=t_{j}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h}):=\sqrt{\frac{j+\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})}{\tanh(\mathtt{h}(j+\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})))}}\,.

Theorem 5.3
.

(Taylor expansion of
B^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)
)

For any
\mathtt{p}\geq 2
, the

2\times 2
Hermitian
matrix

\mathtt{B}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)&\mathrm{i}\,\beta^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)\\
-\mathrm{i}\,\beta^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)&\gamma^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)\end{pmatrix}

defined in (
4.8
)
is analytic
in
B_{\delta_{0}}(\underline{\mu})\times B_{\epsilon_{0}}(0)
,
its entries satisfy

\epsilon\mapsto\alpha^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)\,,\;\gamma^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)\text{ are even,}\qquad\epsilon\mapsto\beta^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)\text{ is }\begin{cases}\text{even}&\text{if }\mathtt{p}\text{ is even}\,,\\
\text{odd}&\text{if }\mathtt{p}\text{ is odd}\,,\end{cases}

(5.9)

and the properties of
Assumption
4.4
:

I)

(Off-diagonal entry)
The real function

\beta^{(\mathtt{p})}(\underline{\mu}+\delta,\epsilon)
admits the Taylor expansion

\beta^{(\mathtt{p})}(\underline{\mu}+\delta,\epsilon)=\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}}+r(\delta\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}},\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}+2})

(5.10)

where

\displaystyle\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}}=b_{0}^{(\mathtt{p})}\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}}+\sum_{q=1}^{\mathtt{p}-1}\sum_{0<j_{1}<\dots<j_{q}<\mathtt{p}}\sum_{\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{q}=\pm}\sigma_{1}\dots\sigma_{q}[b_{q}^{(\mathtt{p})}]_{j_{1},\dots,j_{q}}^{\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{q}}\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}}\,,

(5.11)
