\displaystyle b_{0}^{(\mathtt{p})}\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}}:=\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\,}\,\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\mathtt{p}}^{[\mathtt{p}]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{\mathtt{p},0}^{+,-}\end{matrix}\,,\quad\big[b_{q}^{(\mathtt{p})}\big]_{j_{1},\dots,j_{q}}^{\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{q}}\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}}:=\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\,}\,\dfrac{\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{j_{1}}^{[j_{1}]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{j_{1},0}^{\sigma_{1},-}\end{matrix}\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{j_{2}-j_{1}}^{[j_{2}-j_{1}]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{j_{2},j_{1}}^{\sigma_{2},\sigma_{1}}\end{matrix}\dots\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\mathtt{p}-j_{q}}^{[\mathtt{p}-j_{q}]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{\mathtt{p},j_{q}}^{+,\sigma_{q}}\end{matrix}}{(\omega_{j_{1}}^{\sigma_{1}}-\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})})\dots(\omega_{j_{q}}^{\sigma_{q}}-\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})})}\,,

\bullet
the
\omega_{j}^{\sigma}
are defined in (
5.7
) and

\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})}
is defined in (
2.30
);

\bullet

the entanglement coefficients have the explicit expression

\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\ell}^{[\ell]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{j+\ell,j}^{\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}\end{matrix}=\frac{\epsilon^{\ell}}{4}\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\sqrt{\sigma^{\prime}}}\sqrt{\Omega_{j}^{(\mathtt{p})}\Omega_{j+\ell}^{(\mathtt{p})}}\big(a_{\ell}^{[\ell]}-p_{\ell}^{[\ell]}(\sigma t_{j}^{(\mathtt{p})}+\sigma^{\prime}t_{j+\ell}^{(\mathtt{p})})\big)\,,\quad j,\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\,,\ \ \sigma,\sigma^{\prime}=\pm\,,

(5.12)

where

p_{\ell}^{[\ell]}
,
a_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\in\mathbb{R}
are the maximal Fourier coefficients of the functions
p_{\ell}(x)
,
a_{\ell}(x)
in (
2.11
) and

\Omega_{j}^{(\mathtt{p})}
,
t_{j}^{(\mathtt{p})}
are in (
5.8
);

\bullet
the function

\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}:(0,+\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
,
\mathtt{h}\mapsto\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})
,
is analytic.

II
)
(Diagonal entries)
.
The entries
\alpha^{(\mathtt{p})}(\underline{\mu}+\delta,\epsilon)
and
\gamma^{(\mathtt{p})}(\underline{\mu}+\delta,\epsilon)
admit
the Taylor expansions
(
4.15a
), (
4.15c
),

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\alpha^{(\mathtt{p})}(\underline{\mu}+\delta,\epsilon)=-\omega_{\mathtt{p}}^{+}(\underline{\mu}+\delta)+\alpha_{2}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})\epsilon^{2}+r(\epsilon^{4},\delta\epsilon^{2})\,,

(5.13)

\displaystyle\gamma^{(\mathtt{p})}(\underline{\mu}+\delta,\epsilon)=\omega_{0}^{-}(\underline{\mu}+\delta)+\gamma_{2}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})\epsilon^{2}+r(\epsilon^{4},\delta\epsilon^{2})\,,

with

\omega_{\mathtt{p}}^{+}(\underline{\mu}+\delta)=\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})}-\alpha_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})\delta+r(\delta^{2})\,,\quad\omega_{0}^{-}(\underline{\mu}+\delta)=\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})}+\gamma_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})\delta+r(\delta^{2})\,,

(5.14)

and

\alpha_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})+\gamma_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})>0\,,\quad\gamma_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})-\alpha_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})>0\,,\quad\forall\mathtt{h}>0\,.

(5.15)

The functions
\alpha_{2}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})
,
\gamma_{2}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})
are analytic in
\mathtt{h}
.

The whole section is devoted to the proof of Theorem
5.3
.

We first give simple properties of an
operator
A
as in (
5.4
).
The adjoint of the
\kappa
-band operator
A^{[\kappa]}
is

\big[A^{[\kappa]}\big]^{*}=(A^{*})^{[-\kappa]}\,.

(5.16)

Formally each
A
is the sum of its
\kappa
-bands

A=\sum_{\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}}A^{[\kappa]}\,\,,

(5.17)

and the
\kappa
-band of the composed operator is

(A\circ B)^{[\kappa]}=\sum_{\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}=\kappa}A^{[\kappa_{1}]}\circ B^{[\kappa_{2}]}\,.

(5.18)

The
\kappa
-band operator
associated to
the multiplication operator
for a matrix of functions
a(x)
is the multiplication operator for the
\kappa
-harmonic of
a(x)
, namely

a_{\kappa}e^{\mathrm{i}\,\kappa x}:\,\ h(x)\mapsto a_{\kappa}e^{\mathrm{i}\,\kappa x}h(x)\qquad\text{where}\qquad a_{\kappa}:=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}}a(x)e^{-\mathrm{i}\,\kappa x}dx\,.

(5.19)

For any
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
the
\kappa
-band operator

[g(D)]^{[\kappa]}
of a Fourier multiplier
g(D)
is zero.

The following definition encodes an important structure of the operators
{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)
in (
2.12
),
P(\mu,\epsilon)
in (
4.1
) and
\mathfrak{B}(\mu,\epsilon)
in (
4.9
).
Roughly they are operators whose jets
A_{i,j}
have “finite-range interactions” of order at most
j
and with the same parity of
j
.

Definition 5.4

(
Spaces
\mathfrak{F}_{n}
and
\mathtt{F}_{\ell}
).

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
we define
\mathfrak{F}_{n}
the space of
2\times 2
matrices of operators
A
fulfilling

A^{[\kappa]}=0\quad\textup{ if }|\kappa|>n\quad\textup{ or}\quad\kappa{\;\scriptsize\begin{matrix}\cancel{\equiv}\end{matrix}\;}n\mod{2}\,.

(5.20)

We denote by

\mathtt{F}_{\ell}
the space of
2\times 2
matrices of operators
A

(cfr. (
5.1
))
such that

A=A_{\ell}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i+n=\ell\\
i,n\geq 0\end{subarray}}A_{i,n}\quad\text{such that}\quad A_{i,n}\in\mathfrak{F}_{n}\,.

In particular
A_{\ell}^{[\kappa]}=0
if
|\kappa|>\ell
.

The following properties of the spaces

\mathtt{F}_{\ell}
will be used repeatedly.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}

and
A\in\mathtt{F}_{\ell}
,
B\in\mathtt{F}_{\ell^{\prime}}
. Then

(i)

Highest interaction:

A_{\ell}^{[\pm\ell]}=A_{0,\ell}^{[\pm\ell]}
;

(ii)

Composition:

A\circ B\in\mathtt{F}_{\ell+\ell^{\prime}}
with

(A\circ B)^{[\pm(\ell+\ell^{\prime})]}=A^{[\pm\ell]}_{0,\ell}\circ B^{[\pm\ell^{\prime}]}_{0,\ell^{\prime}}
;

(iii)

Adjoint:

A^{*}\in\mathtt{F}_{\ell}
;

(iv)

Finite range interaction:
For any
v_{1},v_{2}\in\mathbb{C}^{2}
,

(A_{i,n}v_{1}e^{\mathrm{i}\,j_{1}x},v_{2}e^{\mathrm{i}\,j_{2}x})=0\ \ \mbox{ if }|j_{1}-j_{2}|>n\mbox{ or }j_{1}-j_{2}\not\equiv n\mod 2\ .

(5.21)

Proof.

(i)
The only possibility to have harmonics
[\pm\ell]
is that
n=\ell
, namely
i=0
.

(ii)
By Definition

5.4
of the space
\mathtt{F}_{\ell}
and using (
5.17
), we have

\displaystyle A\circ B=\sum_{{i_{1}+n_{1}=\ell\,,\atop i_{2}+n_{2}=\ell^{\prime}\,,}\atop\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}A_{i_{1},n_{1}}^{[\kappa_{1}]}\circ B_{i_{2},n_{2}}^{[\kappa_{2}]}=\sum_{i+n=\ell+\ell^{\prime},\atop i,n\geq 0,\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}}C_{i,n}^{[\kappa]}\ ,

\displaystyle C_{i,n}^{[\kappa]}:=\sum_{{i_{1}+i_{2}=i\atop n_{1}+n_{2}=n}\atop\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}=\kappa}A_{i_{1},n_{1}}^{[\kappa_{1}]}\circ B_{i_{2},n_{2}}^{[\kappa_{2}]}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{bandAB}}}{{=}}\sum_{{i_{1}+i_{2}=i\atop n_{1}+n_{2}=n}}\left(A_{i_{1},n_{1}}\circ B_{i_{2},n_{2}}\right)^{[\kappa]}

where, since
A_{i_{1},n_{1}}\in\mathfrak{F}_{n_{1}}
and
B_{i_{2},n_{2}}\in\mathfrak{F}_{n_{2}}
,
the harmonic
\kappa_{1}
have modulus not larger than
n_{1}
and with the same parity as
n_{1}
, and
\kappa_{2}
have modulus not larger than
n_{2}
and
with the same parity as
n_{2}
.
Hence each
\kappa=\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}
has modulus not larger than
n=n_{1}+n_{2}
and with the same parity of
n_{1}+n_{2}
.
Hence
A\circ B\in\mathtt{F}_{\ell+\ell^{\prime}}
.
Moreover since
A
has harmonics at most
\ell
and
B
at most
\ell^{\prime}
, to reach harmonics
\pm(\ell+\ell^{\prime})
the only possibility is via the terms
A_{i_{1},n_{1}}^{[\pm\ell]}\circ B_{i_{2},n_{2}}^{[\pm\ell^{\prime}]}
which, again by the properties of the class
\mathtt{F}_{\ell}
, are non-trivial only
when
(i_{1},n_{1})=(0,\ell)
,
(i_{2},n_{2})=(0,\ell^{\prime})
.

(iii)
follows by (
5.16
).

(iv)
follows since

(A_{i,n}v_{1}e^{\mathrm{i}\,j_{1}x},v_{2}e^{\mathrm{i}\,j_{2}x})=(A_{i,n}^{[j_{2}-j_{1}]}v_{1}e^{\mathrm{i}\,j_{1}x},v_{2}e^{\mathrm{i}\,j_{2}x})

which vanishes,
by the properties of the
\mathtt{F}_{\ell}
matrices, if
|j_{1}-j_{2}|>n
or
j_{1}-j_{2}\not\equiv n\mod{2}
.
∎

5.1
Taylor expansion of
{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)
and
P(\mu,\epsilon)

We first describe the Taylor expansion
of the operator
{\mathcal{B}}(\underline{\mu}+\delta,\epsilon)
defined in (
2.12
).
Such operator is the composition of multiplication operators for the analytic functions
a_{\epsilon}(x)
,
p_{\epsilon}(x)
and Fourier multipliers depending on
\mu,\epsilon
.
We first prove that the jets
{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}
of
{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)
at
(\underline{\mu},0)
belong to the class
\mathtt{F}_{\ell}
for any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.
This is due to the fact that,
by (
2.11
) and Definition
2.1
,
the Taylor coefficients
p_{\ell}(x)
,
a_{\ell}(x)
of the functions

p_{\epsilon}(x)
,
a_{\epsilon}(x)
have the form

\displaystyle p_{\ell}(x)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}p_{\ell}^{[0]}+p_{\ell}^{[2]}\cos(2x)+\dots+p_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\cos(\ell x)&\text{if }\ell\text{ is even}\,,\\[5.69054pt]
p_{\ell}^{[1]}\cos(x)+p_{\ell}^{[3]}\cos(3x)+\dots+p_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\cos(\ell x)&\text{if }\ell\text{ is odd}\,,\end{cases}

(5.22)

\displaystyle a_{\ell}(x)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}a_{\ell}^{[0]}+a_{\ell}^{[2]}\cos(2x)+\dots+a_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\cos(\ell x)&\text{if }\ell\text{ is even}\,,\\[5.69054pt]
a_{\ell}^{[1]}\cos(x)+a_{\ell}^{[3]}\cos(3x)+\dots+a_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\cos(\ell x)&\text{if }\ell\text{ is odd}\,,\end{cases}

with real coefficients
a_{\ell}^{[i]}
,
p_{\ell}^{[i]}
, whereas the dependence in
\mu
is only in Fourier multipliers.

Lemma 5.6

(
Expansion of
{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon
).

The jets of the Taylor expansion at
(\mu,\epsilon)=(\underline{\mu},0)
of the self-adjoint operator
{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)
in (
2.12
) at
(\underline{\mu},0)
, namely

{\mathcal{B}}(\underline{\mu}+\delta,\epsilon)=\sum_{\ell\geq 0}{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}\,,

(5.23)

satisfy,
for any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
,

{\mathcal{B}}_{0}\,,\;{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}\in\mathtt{F}_{\ell}\ \text{ and }\quad{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}^{[\pm\ell]}={\mathcal{B}}_{0,\ell}^{[\pm\ell]}=\epsilon^{\ell}\frac{e^{\pm\mathrm{i}\,\ell x}}{2}\begin{bmatrix}a_{\ell}^{[\ell]}&-p_{\ell}^{[\ell]}(\partial_{x}+\mathrm{i}\,\underline{\mu})\\
p_{\ell}^{[\ell]}(\partial_{x}+\mathrm{i}\,\underline{\mu}\pm\mathrm{i}\,\ell)&0\end{bmatrix}\,.

(5.24)

Proof.

Recall that the operator
{\mathcal{B}}_{i,n}^{[\kappa]}

is the
\kappa
-band of the Taylor coefficient of order
\delta^{i}\epsilon^{n}
of the operator
{\mathcal{B}}(\underline{\mu}+\delta,\epsilon)
in
(
2.12
) at
\delta=\epsilon=0
, see (
5.3
) and the definition
(
5.2
)
of matrix elements.
The terms of order
\epsilon^{n}
come either from the terms of order
\epsilon^{n}
of
a_{\epsilon}
and
p_{\epsilon}
which, by (
5.22
) have only harmonics with the same parity of
n
and not larger than
n
, or from the
\epsilon^{n}
Taylor coefficient of the Fourier multiplier
|D+\mu|\tanh\big((\mathtt{h}+\mathtt{f}_{\epsilon})|D+\mu|\big)
, which, in view of
(
2.11
), expand only in even powers of
\epsilon
. Then
{\mathcal{B}}_{i,n}^{[\kappa]}
satisfies (
5.20
), proving that

{\mathcal{B}}_{i,n}\in\mathfrak{F}_{n}
for any
i,n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, hence

{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}\in\mathtt{F}_{\ell}
.
The explicit formula (
5.24
) for
{\mathcal{B}}_{0,\ell}^{[\ell]}
follows by (
2.12
) and
(
5.19
).
∎

We now prove the important fact that the jets
P_{\ell}
of the spectral
projector
P(\mu,\epsilon)
, defined in (
4.1
), belong to the class
\mathtt{F}_{\ell}
. This is the property that allows us to propagate the structure
\mathtt{F}_{\ell}
along the Kato’s reduction scheme.

Lemma 5.7

(
Expansion of
P(\mu,\epsilon)
).

The Taylor expansion at
(\mu,\epsilon)=(\underline{\mu},0)
of
the projector
P(\mu,\epsilon)
in (
4.1
), is

P(\underline{\mu}+\delta,\epsilon)=\sum_{\ell\geq 0}P_{\ell}\,,

(5.25)

where

P_{0}=P(\underline{\mu},0)\,,\qquad P_{1}=\mathcal{P}\big[\textup{${\mathcal{B}}_{1}$}\big]\,,\qquad P_{\ell}=\sum_{q=1}^{\ell}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell_{1}+\dots+\ell_{q}=\ell\\
\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{q}\in\mathbb{N}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{P}[{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell_{q}},\dots,{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell_{1}}]\,,

(5.26)

and, for any
q\in\mathbb{N}
,

\mathcal{P}\big[A_{1},\dots,A_{q}\big]:=\frac{(-1)^{q+1}}{2\pi\mathrm{i}\,}\oint_{\Gamma}({\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\mu},0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}A_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\mu},0}-\lambda)^{-1}\dots\mathcal{J}A_{q}({\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\mu},0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathrm{d}\lambda\,.

(5.27)

It results

P_{\ell}\in\mathtt{F}_{\ell}\quad\forall\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\,.

(5.28)

Furthermore

\mathcal{P}\big[A_{1},\dots,A_{q}\big]_{i,n}^{[\kappa]}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1}+\dots+i_{q}=i\\
n_{1}+\dots+n_{q}=n\\
\kappa_{1}+\dots+\kappa_{q}=\kappa\\
\kappa_{1},\dots,\kappa_{q}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{P}\big[[A_{1}]_{i_{1},n_{1}}^{[\kappa_{1}]},\dots,[A_{q}]_{i_{q},n_{q}}^{[\kappa_{q}]}\big]\,.

(5.29)

Proof.

For any
\lambda
in the resolvent set of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\mu},0}
, we Taylor expand the resolvent
({\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\mu}+\delta,\epsilon}-\lambda)^{-1}
at
\delta=\epsilon=0
via Neumann series, getting by (
5.23
)

\displaystyle({\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\mu}+\delta,\epsilon}-\lambda)^{-1}=\left(\mathrm{Id}+({\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\mu},0}-\lambda)^{-1}\sum_{\ell\geq 1}\mathcal{J}{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}\right)^{-1}({\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\mu},0}-\lambda)^{-1}

\displaystyle=({\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\mu},0}-\lambda)^{-1}+\sum_{\ell\geq 1}\sum_{q=1}^{\ell}(-1)^{q}\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ldots+\ell_{q}=\ell\atop\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{q}\geq 1}({\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\mu},0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell_{1}}({\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\mu},0}-\lambda)^{-1}\dots\mathcal{J}{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell_{q}}({\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\mu},0}-\lambda)^{-1}\,.

Inserting such expansion in (
4.1
) yields (
5.25
)–(
5.27
).
Property (
5.28
) descends from the composition rule of
Lemma
5.5

together with the fact that the operator
\mathcal{J}{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell^{\prime}}
belongs to
\mathtt{F}_{\ell^{\prime}}

(cfr. (
5.24
)) and
{\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\mu},0}
is a Fourier multiplier in

\mathtt{F}_{0}
.
∎

5.2
Taylor expansion of
\mathfrak{B}(\mu,\epsilon)

The next proposition
provides an efficient formula to
compute
the Taylor expansion of the operator
\mathfrak{B}(\mu,\epsilon)
in (
4.9
) at any order in terms of the jets of
{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)
and
P(\mu,\epsilon)
.

Proposition 5.8

(
Expansion of
\mathfrak{B}(\mu,\epsilon)
)
.

The Taylor expansion
at
(\mu,\epsilon)=(\underline{\mu},0)
of the operator
\mathfrak{B}(\mu,\epsilon)
in (
4.9
) is

\mathfrak{B}(\underline{\mu}+\delta,\epsilon)=\sum_{\ell\geq 0}\mathfrak{B}_{\ell}

(5.30)

where, for any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,

\mathfrak{B}_{\ell}=P_{0}^{*}\big({\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}+{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell-1}P_{1}+\dots+{\mathcal{B}}_{1}P_{\ell-1}\big)P_{0}+P_{0}^{*}{\mathcal{N}}_{\ell}P_{0}\,,\quad\mathfrak{B}_{\ell}\in\mathtt{F}_{\ell}\,,

(5.31)

the operators
{\mathcal{B}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell}

and
P_{1},\ldots,P_{\ell-1}
are the jets of the operators
{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)
in (
2.12
) and
P(\mu,\epsilon)
in (
4.1
) at
(\underline{\mu},0)
respectively, and

{\mathcal{N}}_{\ell}\in\mathtt{F}_{\ell}\qquad\textup{satisfy}\qquad{\mathcal{N}}_{0}={\mathcal{N}}_{1}={\mathcal{N}}_{2}=0\quad\textup{ and }\quad{\mathcal{N}}_{\ell}^{[\pm\ell]}=0\,,\quad\forall\ell=3,\dots,\mathtt{p}\,.

(5.32)

In particular

\mathfrak{B}_{i,n}\in\mathfrak{F}_{n}\,,\quad\forall i,n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\,.

(5.33)

The rest of this subsection is devoted to the proof of Proposition
5.8
.

We will use that the operators

{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)
and
P(\mu,\epsilon)
satisfy

\displaystyle({\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)P(\mu,\epsilon))^{*}=P(\mu,\epsilon)^{*}{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)={\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)P(\mu,\epsilon)\,,

(5.34)

\displaystyle Q(P(\mu,\epsilon)-P_{0})^{2}=(P(\mu,\epsilon)-P_{0})^{2}Q\quad\mbox{ with }\quad Q\in\{P(\mu,\epsilon),P_{0}\}\,,

(5.35)

where
P_{0}=P(\underline{\mu},0)
.
Property
(
5.34
) follows by (
4.2
),
(
4.3
),

\mathcal{J}^{2}=-\text{Id}

and the fact that

{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)
is self-adjoint.
Property (
5.35
)
is satisfied by any pair of projectors.

As a first step to prove Proposition
5.8
we prove the following lemma.
For any operator
A

we set
\textup{{Sym}}[A]:=\frac{1}{2}A+\frac{1}{2}A^{*}
.

Lemma 5.9

(
Splitting of
\mathfrak{B}(\mu,\epsilon)
).

The operator
\mathfrak{B}(\mu,\epsilon)

in (
4.9
) can be written as

\displaystyle\textup{$\mathfrak{B}$}(\mu,\epsilon)=P_{0}^{*}\textup{$\mathbf{Sym}$}\big[{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)+\mathring{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)+{\mathcal{R}}(\mu,\epsilon)\big]P_{0}

(5.36)

\displaystyle\text{where}\quad\mathring{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon):={\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)-{\mathcal{B}}_{0}\,,\ \mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon):=P(\mu,\epsilon)-P_{0}\,,

and

{\mathcal{R}}_{\ell}\in\mathtt{F}_{\ell}\qquad\textup{satisfy}\qquad{\mathcal{R}}_{0}={\mathcal{R}}_{1}={\mathcal{R}}_{2}=0\,,\quad{\mathcal{R}}_{\ell}^{[\pm\ell]}=0\,,\quad\forall\ell=3,\dots,\mathtt{p}\,.

(5.37)

Proof.

By (
4.4
)
and denoting

g(x):=(1-x)^{-\frac{1}{2}}-1
,
we first write

U_{\mu,\epsilon}P_{0}=\big(\mathrm{Id}+g(\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{2})\big)P(\mu,\epsilon)P_{0}\,.

Then, by (
4.9
) and using also (
5.35
) with
Q=P(\mu,\epsilon)
, we get

\displaystyle{\textup{$\mathfrak{B}$}}(\mu,\epsilon)

\displaystyle=P_{0}^{*}U_{\mu,\epsilon}^{*}{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)U_{\mu,\epsilon}P_{0}

(5.38)

\displaystyle=P_{0}^{*}\big(\mathrm{Id}+g^{*}(\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{2})\big)P(\mu,\epsilon)^{*}{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)P(\mu,\epsilon)\big(\mathrm{Id}+g(\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{2})\big)P_{0}\,.

Next using
(
5.34
) we obtain

\displaystyle{\textup{$\mathfrak{B}$}}(\mu,\epsilon)

\displaystyle=P_{0}^{*}\big(\mathrm{Id}+g^{*}(\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{2})\big){\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)P(\mu,\epsilon)\big(\mathrm{Id}+g(\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{2})\big)P_{0}

(5.39)

\displaystyle=P_{0}^{*}\textup{$\mathbf{Sym}$}\big[{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)P(\mu,\epsilon)+2{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)P(\mu,\epsilon)g(\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{2})+g^{*}(\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{2}){\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)P(\mu,\epsilon)g(\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{2})\big]P_{0}\,.

Finally, since
P(\mu,\epsilon)P_{0}=(\mathrm{Id}+\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon))P_{0}
we write

{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)P(\mu,\epsilon)P_{0}={\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)P_{0}+\mathring{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)P_{0}+{\mathcal{B}}_{0}\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)P_{0}

and,
using
that
g(\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{2})

commutes with
P_{0}
by
(
5.35
) with
Q=P_{0}
,
we conclude that

\textup{$\mathfrak{B}$}(\mu,\epsilon)=P_{0}^{*}\textup{$\mathbf{Sym}$}\big[{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)+\mathring{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)+\mathcal{R}(\mu,\epsilon)\big]P_{0}

(5.40)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(\mu,\epsilon)

\displaystyle:=P_{0}^{*}\big[{{\mathcal{B}}_{0}\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)+2{\mathcal{B}}_{0}g(\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{2})}+2\mathring{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)g(\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{2})

(5.41)

\displaystyle+2{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)g(\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{2})+g^{*}(\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{2}){\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)P(\mu,\epsilon)g(\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{2})\big]P_{0}\,.

We now show that
\mathcal{R}(\mu,\epsilon)
satisfies the properties claimed in
(
5.37
).

{\mathcal{R}}_{\ell}\in\mathtt{F}_{\ell}
.

It is a consequence of Lemma
5.5
, (
5.24
)
and the fact that

\big[g(\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{2})\big]_{\ell}\in\mathtt{F}_{\ell}
, which,
writing

\big[g(\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{2})\big]_{\ell}=\sum_{k\geq 1}c_{k}\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ldots+\ell_{2k}=\ell\atop\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{2k}\geq 1}P_{\ell_{1}}\circ\cdots\circ P_{\ell_{2k}}

(the
c_{k}
’s are the Taylor coefficients of
g(x)
at
x=0
),
follows by
(
5.28
) and the composition property of
Lemma
5.5
.

\mathcal{R}_{0}=\mathcal{R}_{1}=\mathcal{R}_{2}=0
.

We first exhibit a cancellation in the first two terms in (
5.41
). Precisely we claim that

P_{0}^{*}{\mathcal{B}}_{0}\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)P_{0}+2P_{0}^{*}{\mathcal{B}}_{0}g(\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{2})P_{0}=2P_{0}^{*}{\mathcal{B}}_{0}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mu,\epsilon}\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{4}P_{0}\,,\quad{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mu,\epsilon}:=\widetilde{g}(\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{2})\,,

(5.42)

where
\widetilde{g}(x)
is the analytic function such that
g(x)=\frac{1}{2}x+\widetilde{g}(x)x^{2}
.
Since the operator

{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mu,\epsilon}
in (
5.42
) is
\mathcal{O}_{4}

and all the other terms in (
5.41
) are at least
\mathcal{O}_{3}
, we deduce that

\mathcal{R}_{0}=\mathcal{R}_{1}=\mathcal{R}_{2}=0
.
Moreover as above one has
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\ell}\in\mathtt{F}_{\ell}
(argue as for
{\mathcal{R}}_{\ell}
).
Let us prove (
5.42
).
By definition of
\widetilde{g}(x)
we have

g(\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{2})=\tfrac{1}{2}\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{2}+{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mu,\epsilon}\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{4}\,,

and, using also (
5.34
),

\displaystyle P_{0}^{*}{\mathcal{B}}_{0}\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)P_{0}+2P_{0}^{*}{\mathcal{B}}_{0}g(\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{2})P_{0}=

(5.43)

\displaystyle\underbrace{P_{0}^{*}{\mathcal{B}}_{0}P_{0}\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)P_{0}+P_{0}^{*}{\mathcal{B}}_{0}\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{2}P_{0}}_{=0}+2P_{0}^{*}{\mathcal{B}}_{0}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mu,\epsilon}\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{4}P_{0}

where the underbraced term vanishes because of the identity
P_{0}\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)P_{0}=-\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{2}P_{0}
, which comes from the projector identity
P_{0}+\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)=(P_{0}+\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon))^{2}
.
This proves (
5.42
).

{\mathcal{R}}_{\ell}^{[\pm\ell]}=0
for any
\ell=3,\dots,\mathtt{p}
.

We first note that, by (
5.41
)-(
5.42
) and (
5.35
) with
Q=P_{0}
, the operator
\mathcal{R}(\mu,\epsilon)
has the form

\textup{${\mathcal{R}}(\mu,\epsilon)$}=P_{0}^{*}\textup{$\mathcal{E}$}(\mu,\epsilon)P_{0}\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{2}P_{0}

(5.44)

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(\mu,\epsilon)

\displaystyle:=2{\mathcal{B}}_{0}\mathcal{Q}_{\mu,\epsilon}\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{2}+2\mathring{{\mathcal{B}}}(\mu,\epsilon)\,\breve{g}(\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{2})+2{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)\,\breve{g}(\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{2})

(5.45)

\displaystyle+g^{*}(\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{2}){\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)P(\mu,\epsilon)\,\breve{g}(\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)^{2})\,,\quad\mathcal{E}_{0}=0\,,

where
\breve{g}(x)
is the analytic function such that
g(x)=\breve{g}(x)x
. By Lemma
5.5
, (
5.23
), (
5.25
) we deduce that

\mathcal{E}_{\ell}\in\mathtt{F}_{\ell}\,,\qquad\ell\in\mathbb{N}\,.

(5.46)

We now show that
{\mathcal{R}}_{\ell}^{[\pm\ell]}=0
,
for any

\ell=3,\dots,\mathtt{p}
, or, equivalently,

\big({\mathcal{R}}_{\ell}^{[\pm\ell]}\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{thisR}}}{{=}}\big(P_{0}^{*}{\mathcal{R}}_{\ell}^{[\pm\ell]}P_{0}\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\big)=\big({\mathcal{R}}_{\ell}^{[\pm\ell]}P_{0}\phi_{1},P_{0}\phi_{2}\big)=0\,,\quad\forall\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{T})\,,

which boils down to prove that, for any
\ell=3,\dots,\mathtt{p}
,

({\mathcal{R}}_{\ell}^{[\pm\ell]}f_{0}^{-},f_{0}^{-})=0\,,\quad({\mathcal{R}}_{\ell}^{[\pm\ell]}f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+},f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+})=0\,,\quad({\mathcal{R}}_{\ell}^{[\pm\ell]}f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+},f_{0}^{-})=0\,,\quad({\mathcal{R}}_{\ell}^{[\pm\ell]}f_{0}^{-},f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+})=0\,.

(5.47)

Since
{\mathcal{R}}_{\ell}\in\mathtt{F}_{\ell}
, by directly
comparing the
harmonics in the left and right vectors
of the scalar product,
all these identities are trivial
except for
({\mathcal{R}}_{\mathtt{p}}^{[\mathtt{p}]}f_{0}^{-},f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+})=0
and
({\mathcal{R}}_{\mathtt{p}}^{[-\mathtt{p}]}f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+},f_{0}^{-})=0
.
In view of (
5.44
)
and Lemma
5.5

we have

\big({\mathcal{R}}_{\mathtt{p}}^{[\mathtt{p}]}f_{0}^{-},f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+}\big)=\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}+\ell_{3}=\mathtt{p}\atop\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\ell_{3}\geq 1}\big(\mathcal{E}_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\underbrace{P_{0}P_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}P_{\ell_{3}}^{[\ell_{3}]}f_{0}^{-}}_{=:\phi},f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+}\big)\,.

(5.48)

Since
\phi=\alpha f_{0}^{-}+\beta f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\underline{\mu},0}
and, by (
5.46
),

\mathcal{E}_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}\in\mathtt{F}_{\ell_{1}}

with
1\leq\ell_{1}\leq\mathtt{p}-1
, the scalar product in (
5.48
) vanishes.
Similarly
({\mathcal{R}}_{\mathtt{p}}^{[-\mathtt{p}]}f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+},f_{0}^{-})=0
. Thus (
5.47
) and consequently the last identity in (
5.37
) hold.
∎

In the next lemma we
simplify the first terms in (
5.36
).

Lemma 5.10
.

One has

P_{0}^{*}\textup{$\mathbf{Sym}$}\big[{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)+\mathring{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)\big]P_{0}=P_{0}^{*}\big({\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)+\mathring{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)\big)P_{0}\,.

(5.49)

Proof.

We begin by proving the identities

P_{0}^{*}\textup{${\mathcal{B}}_{0}$}=-\mathrm{i}\,\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})}P_{0}^{*}\textup{$\mathcal{J}$}\,,\qquad P_{0}^{*}\textup{${\mathcal{B}}_{0}$}P(\mu,\epsilon)P_{0}=P_{0}^{*}P(\mu,\epsilon)^{*}\textup{${\mathcal{B}}_{0}$}P_{0}\,.

(5.50)

The first identity descends from the fact that,
for any
f,g
in
L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{2})
,

\big(P_{0}^{*}\textup{${\mathcal{B}}_{0}$}f,g\big)=\big(f,\textup{${\mathcal{B}}_{0}$}P_{0}g\big)=\big(\mathcal{J}f,{\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\mu},0}P_{0}g\big)=-\mathrm{i}\,\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})}\big(\mathcal{J}f,P_{0}g\big)=-\mathrm{i}\,\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})}\big(P_{0}^{*}\mathcal{J}f,g\big)\,.

The second identity descends from the first one, since

\displaystyle P_{0}^{*}{\mathcal{B}}_{0}P(\mu,\epsilon)P_{0}=-\mathrm{i}\,\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})}P_{0}^{*}\mathcal{J}P(\mu,\epsilon)P_{0}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{propPU}}}{{=}}P_{0}^{*}P(\mu,\epsilon)^{*}(-\mathrm{i}\,\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})}P_{0}^{*}\mathcal{J})

\displaystyle=P_{0}^{*}P(\mu,\epsilon)^{*}P_{0}^{*}{\mathcal{B}}_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{BP}}}{{=}}P_{0}^{*}P(\mu,\epsilon)^{*}{\mathcal{B}}_{0}P_{0}\,.

We use (
5.50
) to deduce that
P_{0}^{*}\big({\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)+\mathring{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)\big)P_{0}

is selfadjoint. Indeed

\displaystyle P_{0}^{*}\big({\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)+\mathring{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)\mathring{P}(\mu,\epsilon)\big)P_{0}

\displaystyle=P_{0}^{*}\big({\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)+{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)P(\mu,\epsilon)+{\mathcal{B}}_{0}P_{0}-{\mathcal{B}}_{0}P(\mu,\epsilon)-{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)P_{0}\big)P_{0}

\displaystyle=P_{0}^{*}\big(\overbrace{{\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)P(\mu,\epsilon)}^{(\mathrm{I})}+\overbrace{{\mathcal{B}}_{0}P_{0}}^{(\mathrm{II})}-\overbrace{{\mathcal{B}}_{0}P(\mu,\epsilon)}^{(\mathrm{III})}\big)P_{0}\,.

The terms (I) and (II) are self-adjoint by (
5.34
).
Also the operator
P_{0}^{*}(\mathrm{III})P_{0}
is self-adjoint by the second identity in (
5.50
). This proves (
5.49
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.8
. By (
5.36
) and (
5.49
) we have

\mathfrak{B}(\mu,\epsilon)=P_{0}^{*}\big({\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)+({\mathcal{B}}(\mu,\epsilon)-{\mathcal{B}}(\underline{\mu},0))(P(\mu,\epsilon)-P(\underline{\mu},0))+{\mathcal{N}}(\mu,\epsilon)\big)P_{0}

where

{\mathcal{N}}(\mu,\epsilon):=\textup{$\mathbf{Sym}$}[{\mathcal{R}}(\mu,\epsilon)]
.
Consequently the jets

\mathfrak{B}_{\ell}
of
\mathfrak{B}(\mu,\epsilon)
have the form in (
5.31
) and, by Lemma
5.5
, the fact that
{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell^{\prime}},P_{\ell^{\prime}}\in\mathtt{F}_{\ell^{\prime}}

for any
1\leq\ell^{\prime}\leq\ell

(cfr. (
5.24
), (
5.28
)),
and (
5.37
) we conclude that
\mathfrak{B}_{\ell}\in\mathtt{F}_{\ell}
.
By (
5.37
) the operator
{\mathcal{N}}(\mu,\epsilon)
fulfills (
5.32
), using also Lemma
5.5
for the last identity. ∎

5.3
Proof of the weak conjecture
(
W
)

We now start proving that the entries of the matrix
\mathtt{L}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)
given by (
4.7
)
satisfy Assumption
4.4

as stated in Theorem
5.3
.

Lemma 5.11
.

The entries of the matrix
\mathtt{B}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)
in (
4.8
) satisfy the parity properties
(
5.9
).

Proof.

On
B_{\delta_{0}}(\underline{\mu})\times B_{\epsilon_{0}}(0)

we have the Taylor expansions

\alpha^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)=\big(\mathfrak{B}(\mu,\epsilon)f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+},f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+}\big)=\sum_{n\geq 0}a^{(\mathtt{p})}_{n}(\mu)\epsilon^{n}=\sum_{i,n\geq 0}a_{i,n}^{(\mathtt{p})}\delta^{i}\epsilon^{n}

(5.51)

where, by (
5.33
) and
recalling Definition
5.4
,

a_{i,n}^{(\mathtt{p})}=\big(\mathfrak{B}_{i,n}f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+},f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+}\big)=0\qquad\text{if }n\text{ is odd}\,,\quad\forall i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\,.

This proves that
a^{(\mathtt{p})}_{n}(\mu)\equiv 0
for any
n
odd, namely

\epsilon\mapsto\alpha^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)
is even. Similarly
we deduce that

\epsilon\mapsto\gamma^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)=\big(\mathfrak{B}(\mu,\epsilon)f_{0}^{+},f_{0}^{+}\big)

is even.
Furthermore

\beta^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)=-\mathrm{i}\,\big(\mathfrak{B}(\mu,\epsilon)f_{0}^{-},f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+}\big)=\sum_{n\geq 0}b^{(\mathtt{p})}_{n}(\mu)\epsilon^{n}=\sum_{i,n\geq 0}b_{i,n}^{(\mathtt{p})}\delta^{i}\epsilon^{n}

(5.52)

where, by (
5.33
) and
recalling Definition
5.4
and (
5.21
),

b_{i,n}^{(\mathtt{p})}=-\mathrm{i}\,\big(\mathfrak{B}_{i,n}f_{0}^{-},f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+}\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{Bijs}}}{{=}}0\qquad\text{if }n\not\equiv\mathtt{p}\mod 2\,,\quad\forall i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\,.

(5.53)

This proves that the function

\epsilon\mapsto\beta^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)
has the parity of
\mathtt{p}
. The lemma is proved.
∎

We now prove
that
\beta^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)
vanish up to order
\mathtt{p}-1
in
\epsilon

as well as the order

\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}+1}
.

Lemma 5.12
.

Expansion (
5.10
) holds with
\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}}:=-\mathrm{i}\,\big(\mathfrak{B}_{0,\mathtt{p}}f_{0}^{-},f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+}\big)
.

Proof.

On
B_{\delta_{0}}(\underline{\mu})\times B_{\epsilon_{0}}(0)

we have the Taylor expansion
(
5.52
)

\beta^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)=\sum_{n=0}^{\mathtt{p}-1}b^{(\mathtt{p})}_{n}(\mu)\epsilon^{n}+b^{(\mathtt{p})}_{\mathtt{p}}(\mu)\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}}+b^{(\mathtt{p})}_{\mathtt{p}+1}(\mu)\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}+1}+r(\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}+2})\,,

(5.54)

where, for any
n
,

b_{n}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu)=\sum_{i\geq 0}b_{i,n}^{(\mathtt{p})}\delta^{i}\,,\qquad b_{i,n}^{(\mathtt{p})}=-\mathrm{i}\,\big(\mathfrak{B}_{i,n}f_{0}^{-},f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+}\big)\,.

(5.55)

Now, since
f_{0}^{-}
,
f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+}
in (
1.13
) are
Fourier supported respectively on the
0
and the
\mathtt{p}
harmonic,
using (
5.33
),
(
5.21
) and (
5.53
)
we have that
the functions

b^{(\mathtt{p})}_{n}(\mu)\equiv 0\,,\quad\forall n<\mathtt{p}\,,\qquad b^{(\mathtt{p})}_{\mathtt{p}+1}(\mu)\equiv 0\,.

(5.56)

By (
5.54
)
and (
5.56
)
we deduce that

\beta^{(\mathtt{p})}(\underline{\mu}+\delta,\epsilon)=-\mathrm{i}\,\big(\mathfrak{B}(\mu,\epsilon)f_{0}^{-},f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+}\big)=b^{(\mathtt{p})}_{\mathtt{p}}(\underline{\mu}+\delta)\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}}+r(\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}+2})=\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}}+r(\delta\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}},\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}+2})\,,

where

\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}}:=b_{\mathtt{p}}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\underline{\mu})\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}}=b_{0,\mathtt{p}}^{(\mathtt{p})}\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}}=-\mathrm{i}\,\big(\mathfrak{B}_{0,\mathtt{p}}f_{0}^{-},f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+}\big)
by
(
5.55
).
The lemma is proved.
∎

We now turn our attention to the second part of Theorem
5.3
.

Lemma 5.13
.

The expansions (
5.13
), (
5.14
) and (
5.15
) hold.

Proof.

By (
4.10
) and the Taylor expansion
(
5.51
) we have

\displaystyle\alpha^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)

\displaystyle=-\omega_{\mathtt{p}}^{+}(\underline{\mu}+\delta)+a^{(\mathtt{p})}_{2}(\underline{\mu}+\delta)\epsilon^{2}+r(\epsilon^{4})

\displaystyle\gamma^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mu,\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\omega_{0}^{-}(\underline{\mu}+\delta)+\gamma^{(\mathtt{p})}_{2}(\underline{\mu}+\delta)\epsilon^{2}+r(\epsilon^{4})

proving (
5.13
) after Taylor expanding

a^{(\mathtt{p})}_{2}(\underline{\mu}+\delta)
,
\gamma^{(\mathtt{p})}_{2}(\underline{\mu}+\delta)
at
\delta=0
and denoting

\alpha^{(\mathtt{p})}_{2}(\mathtt{h}):=a^{(\mathtt{p})}_{2}(\underline{\mu})
.

We deduce
(
5.14
)
with

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h}):=-(\partial_{\mu}\omega^{+})(\mathtt{p}+\underline{\mu},\mathtt{h})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{omeghino}}}{{=}}-{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}+(\partial_{\varphi}\Omega)(\mathtt{p}+\underline{\mu},\mathtt{h})\,,

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h}):=(\partial_{\mu}\omega^{-})(\underline{\mu},\mathtt{h})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{omeghino}}}{{=}}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}+(\partial_{\varphi}\Omega)(\underline{\mu},\mathtt{h})\,.

Note that, for any

\mathtt{h}>0
, by (
2.20
)

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})+\gamma_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})=(\partial_{\varphi}\Omega)(\underline{\mu}+\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})+(\partial_{\varphi}\Omega)(\underline{\mu},\mathtt{h})>0

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})-\alpha_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})=2{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}+(\partial_{\varphi}\Omega)(\underline{\mu},\mathtt{h})-(\partial_{\varphi}\Omega)(\underline{\mu}+\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})>2{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}

because
the function
x\mapsto(\partial_{\varphi}\Omega)(\underline{\mu}+x,\mathtt{h})
is strictly decreasing,
by (
2.20
).
Thus
also (
5.15
) is proved.
∎

We now have to compute the explicit expression (
5.11
)
of the first leading term
\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}}
.

5.4
Formula for
\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})

Let us consider depths
\mathtt{h}>0

such that
\underline{\mu}=\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})
in Lemma
2.7
is
not
an integer and
the eigenvectors
\{f_{j}^{\sigma}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z},\sigma=\pm}
,

f_{j}^{\sigma}=f_{j}^{\sigma}(\underline{\mu})
in
(
2.21
) form a
complex symplectic
basis
of
L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{2})
, cfr.
Remark
2.10
. In Lemma
5.14

we represent the action of the jets of the
Kato projectors in this eigenfunction basis,
and not just the exponential basis (of course these basis are deeply related). This is very convenient for
the computation of
\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})
.

We also remark that this is with no loss of generality.
Indeed, since the function
\mathtt{h}\mapsto\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})
is non zero, real-analytic and bounded (see Lemma
2.7
),
the excluded depths are isolated and form a closed set, thus its complementary is dense in

\mathtt{h}>0
. In particular, the formula for
\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})
in (
5.11
), although proved for
\mathtt{h}
in a dense set of
(0,+\infty)
, holds for
all

\mathtt{h}>0
by continuity
(the function
\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})
is actually analytic in
\mathtt{h}>0
).

The next lemma, proved in
[
10
, Lemma 4.1]
(Item (
ii
) is formula
[
10
, (4.19)]
), provides effective formulas to compute the action of the operators
{\mathcal{P}}[{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell_{1}},\dots,{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell_{q}}]
on the
eigenvector basis.

Lemma 5.14
.

Let
\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\ell}^{[\kappa]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{j^{\prime},j}^{\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}\end{matrix}
denote the entanglement coefficients in (
5.5
) and
f_{j}^{\sigma}:=f_{j}^{\sigma}(\underline{\mu})
in (
2.21
), with
\underline{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\mathbb{Z}
. Then:

(i) for any
q\in\mathbb{N}
,
\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{q}\in\mathbb{N}
integers
j,j^{\prime},\kappa_{1},\dots,\kappa_{q}\in\mathbb{Z}

and
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}=\pm
, one has

\displaystyle\big({\mathcal{B}}_{0,\ell_{q+1}}^{[\kappa_{q+1}]}\mathcal{P}[{\mathcal{B}}_{0,\ell_{q}}^{[\kappa_{q}]},\dots,{\mathcal{B}}_{0,\ell_{1}}^{[\kappa_{1}]}]f_{j}^{\sigma},f_{j^{\prime}}^{\sigma^{\prime}}\big)

(5.57)

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{q}=\pm}\sigma_{1}\dots\sigma_{q}\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\ell_{1}}^{[\kappa_{1}]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{j_{1},j}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma}\end{matrix}\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\ell_{2}}^{[\kappa_{2}]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{j_{2},j_{1}}^{\sigma_{2},\sigma_{1}}\end{matrix}\dots\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\ell_{q}}^{[\kappa_{q}]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{j_{q},j_{q-1}}^{\sigma_{q},\sigma_{q-1}}\end{matrix}\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\ell_{q+1}}^{[\kappa_{q+1}]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{j^{\prime},j_{q}}^{\sigma^{\prime},\sigma_{q}}\end{matrix}\mathtt{R}{\scriptsize\begin{matrix}\sigma,&\sigma_{1},&\dots,&\sigma_{q}\\[1.99168pt]
j,&j_{1},&\dots,&j_{q}\end{matrix}}

where
j_{1}:=j+\kappa_{1}
,
j_{2}:=j_{1}+\kappa_{2}
, …,
j_{q}=j_{q-1}+\kappa_{q}
and

\mathtt{R}{\scriptsize\begin{matrix}\sigma,&\sigma_{1},&\dots,&\sigma_{q}\\[1.99168pt]
j,&j_{1},&\dots,&j_{q}\end{matrix}}:=\dfrac{(-\mathrm{i}\,\!)^{q}}{2\pi\mathrm{i}\,}\oint_{\Gamma}\dfrac{\mathrm{d}\lambda}{(\lambda-\mathrm{i}\,\omega_{j}^{\sigma})(\lambda-\mathrm{i}\,\omega_{j_{1}}^{\sigma_{1}})\dots(\lambda-\mathrm{i}\,\omega_{j_{q}}^{\sigma_{q}})}\,,

(5.58)

where
\Gamma
is a circuit winding once around
\mathrm{i}\,\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})}

counterclockwise
(here
\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})}:=\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})
is given in (
2.24
))
and
\omega_{j}^{\sigma}
are in (
5.7
).

(ii)
Let
j_{1},\dots,j_{q}\in\mathbb{Z}
and
\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{q}=\pm
, with
(j_{1},\sigma_{1}),\dots,(j_{q},\sigma_{q})\notin\{(0,-),(\mathtt{p},+)\}
. Then

\mathtt{R}{\scriptsize\begin{matrix}-,&\sigma_{1},&\dots,&\sigma_{q}\\[1.99168pt]
0,&j_{1},&\dots,&j_{q}\end{matrix}}=\mathtt{R}{\scriptsize\begin{matrix}+,&\sigma_{1},&\dots,&\sigma_{q}\\[1.99168pt]
p,&j_{1},&\dots,&j_{q}\end{matrix}}=\dfrac{1}{(\omega_{j_{1}}^{\sigma_{1}}-\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})})\ldots(\omega_{j_{q}}^{\sigma_{q}}-\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})})}

(5.59)

with
\omega_{j}^{\sigma}
in (
5.7
) and
\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})}=\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})
in (
2.24
).

Recall that in Lemma
5.12
we have proved that expansion (
5.10
) holds.
We now compute explicitly the coefficient
\mathrm{i}\,\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}}
.

Lemma 5.15
.

The coefficient
\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})
in (
5.10
) is the function defined
in (
5.11
).

Proof.

By Lemma
5.12
we have
(\textup{$\mathfrak{B}$}_{0,\mathtt{p}}f_{0}^{-},f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+})=(\textup{$\mathfrak{B}$}_{0,\mathtt{p}}^{[\mathtt{p}]}f_{0}^{-},f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+})
.
By (
5.31
), (
5.32
), and using that
{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell},P_{\ell}\in\mathtt{F}_{\ell}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{i}\,\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}}

\displaystyle\,\;\,=\;\,\,({\mathcal{B}}_{0,\mathtt{p}}^{[\mathtt{p}]}f_{0}^{-},f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+})+([{\mathcal{B}}_{0,\mathtt{p}-1}P_{0,1}]^{[\mathtt{p}]}f_{0}^{-},f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+})+\dots+([{\mathcal{B}}_{0,1}P_{0,\mathtt{p}-1}]^{[\mathtt{p}]}f_{0}^{-},f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Lemma}\,\ref{TFjprop}(i),(ii)}}{{=}}({\mathcal{B}}_{0,\mathtt{p}}^{[\mathtt{p}]}f_{0}^{-},f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+})+({\mathcal{B}}_{0,\mathtt{p}-1}^{[\mathtt{p}-1]}P_{0,1}^{[1]}f_{0}^{-},f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+})+\dots+({\mathcal{B}}_{0,1}^{[1]}P_{0,\mathtt{p}-1}^{[\mathtt{p}-1]}f_{0}^{-},f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+})\,.

We now express it in terms of the entanglement coefficients in (
5.5
). We have

\displaystyle\mathrm{i}\,\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}}

\displaystyle=\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\mathtt{p}}^{[\mathtt{p}]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{\mathtt{p},0}^{+,-}\end{matrix}+\sum_{i=1}^{\mathtt{p}-1}({\mathcal{B}}_{0,\mathtt{p}-i}^{[\mathtt{p}-i]}P_{0,i}^{[i]}f_{0}^{-},f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{Psani1}}}{{=}}\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\mathtt{p}}^{[\mathtt{p}]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{\mathtt{p},0}^{+,-}\end{matrix}+\sum_{i=1}^{\mathtt{p}-1}\sum_{q=1}^{i}\sum_{\ell_{1}+\dots+\ell_{q}=i}({\mathcal{B}}_{0,\mathtt{p}-i}^{[\mathtt{p}-i]}\mathcal{P}[{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell_{q}},\dots,{\mathcal{B}}_{\ell_{1}}]_{0,i}^{[i]}f_{0}^{-},f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+})

\displaystyle=\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\mathtt{p}}^{[\mathtt{p}]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{\mathtt{p},0}^{+,-}\end{matrix}+\sum_{i=1}^{\mathtt{p}-1}\sum_{q=1}^{i}\sum_{\ell_{1}+\dots+\ell_{q}=i}({\mathcal{B}}_{0,\mathtt{p}-i}^{[\mathtt{p}-i]}\mathcal{P}[{\mathcal{B}}_{0,\ell_{q}}^{[\ell_{q}]},\dots,{\mathcal{B}}_{0,\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}]f_{0}^{-},f_{\mathtt{p}}^{+})

where in the last equality we used formula (
5.29
) in which
\kappa_{a}\leq\ell_{a}
(since
{\mathcal{B}}_{0,\ell_{a}}\in\mathfrak{F}_{\ell_{a}}
), and here the only possibility is that

\kappa_{a}=\ell_{a}
for
a=1,\ldots,q
.

We now use Lemma
5.14
to compute explicitly the terms in the sum above.

\displaystyle\mathrm{i}\,\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{cBactspar}}}{{=}}\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\mathtt{p}}^{[\mathtt{p}]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{\mathtt{p},0}^{+,-}\end{matrix}+\sum_{i=1}^{\mathtt{p}-1}\sum_{q=1}^{i}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell_{1}+\dots+\ell_{q}=i\\
\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{q}=\pm\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\!\sigma_{1}\dots\sigma_{q}\mathtt{R}{\scriptsize\begin{matrix}-,\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{q}\\[1.99168pt]
0,\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{q}\end{matrix}}\,\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{j_{1},0}^{\sigma_{1},-}\end{matrix}\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{j_{2},j_{1}}^{\sigma_{2},\sigma_{1}}\end{matrix}\dots\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\ell_{q}}^{[\ell_{q}]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{j_{q},j_{q-1}}^{\sigma_{q},\sigma_{q-1}}\end{matrix}\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\mathtt{p}-i}^{[\mathtt{p}-i]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{\mathtt{p},j_{q}}^{+,\sigma_{q}}\end{matrix}

with

j_{1}:=\ell_{1}\,,\ j_{2}:=j_{1}+\ell_{2}=\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}\,,\ \dots\,,\ j_{q}:=j_{q-1}+\ell_{q}=\ell_{1}+\dots+\ell_{q}=i\,.

Thus

1\leq j_{1}<\ldots<j_{q}\leq\mathtt{p}-1
and

(j_{1},\sigma_{1}),\dots,(j_{q},\sigma_{q})\notin\{(0,-),(\mathtt{p},+)\}
so that,
using (
5.59
), we have

\mathrm{i}\,\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}}=\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\mathtt{p}}^{[\mathtt{p}]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{\mathtt{p},0}^{+,-}\end{matrix}+\sum_{i=1}^{\mathtt{p}-1}\sum_{q=1}^{i}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell_{1}+\dots+\ell_{q}=i\\
\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{q}=\pm\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\!\sigma_{1}\dots\sigma_{q}\dfrac{\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{j_{1},0}^{\sigma_{1},-}\end{matrix}\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{j_{2},j_{1}}^{\sigma_{2},\sigma_{1}}\end{matrix}\dots\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\ell_{q}}^{[\ell_{q}]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{j_{q},j_{q-1}}^{\sigma_{q},\sigma_{q-1}}\end{matrix}\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\mathtt{p}-i}^{[\mathtt{p}-i]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{\mathtt{p},j_{q}}^{+,\sigma_{q}}\end{matrix}}{(\omega_{j_{1}}^{\sigma_{1}}-\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})})\ldots(\omega_{j_{q}}^{\sigma_{q}}-\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})})}\,.

(5.60)

We sum differently
(
5.60
) noting that the following map between sets of natural indices is a bijection

\displaystyle\phi:\big\{(i,q;\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{q})\,:\,1\leq i\leq\mathtt{p}-1\,,\,1\leq q\leq i\,,\,\ell_{1}+\dots+\ell_{q}=i\big\}\rightarrow

(5.61)

\displaystyle\big\{(q;j_{1},\dots,j_{q})\,:\,1\leq q\leq\mathtt{p}-1\,,\ 0<j_{1}<\dots<j_{q}<\mathtt{p}\big\}

\displaystyle(i,q;\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{q})\mapsto(q;j_{1}:=\ell_{1},j_{2}:=j_{1}+\ell_{2},\dots,j_{q}:=j_{q-1}+\ell_{q}=i)

with inverse

\phi^{-1}(q,j_{1},\dots,j_{q})=\big(j_{q},q,j_{1},j_{2}-j_{1},\dots,j_{q}-j_{q-1}\big)\,.

Thus, by (
5.61
), identity (
5.60
) becomes

\mathrm{i}\,\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}\epsilon^{\mathtt{p}}=\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\mathtt{p}}^{[\mathtt{p}]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{\mathtt{p},0}^{+,-}\end{matrix}+\sum_{q=1}^{\mathtt{p}-1}\ \sum_{0<j_{1}<\dots<j_{q}<\mathtt{p}}\ \sum_{\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{q}=\pm}\sigma_{1}\dots\sigma_{q}\dfrac{\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\ell_{1}}^{[\ell_{1}]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{j_{1},0}^{\sigma_{1},-}\end{matrix}\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\ell_{2}}^{[\ell_{2}]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{j_{2},j_{1}}^{\sigma_{2},\sigma_{1}}\end{matrix}\dots\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\ell_{q}}^{[\ell_{q}]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{j_{q},j_{q-1}}^{\sigma_{q},\sigma_{q-1}}\end{matrix}\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\mathtt{p}-j_{q}}^{[\mathtt{p}-j_{q}]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{\mathtt{p},j_{q}}^{+,\sigma_{q}}\end{matrix}}{(\omega_{j_{1}}^{\sigma_{1}}-\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})})\ldots(\omega_{j_{q}}^{\sigma_{q}}-\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})})}

with
\ell_{1}:=j_{1}
,
\ell_{2}:=j_{2}-j_{1}
,
\dots
,
\ell_{q}:=j_{q}-j_{q-1}
. This proves formula (
5.11
).∎

Lemmata

5.12
and
5.15
prove formula
(
5.11
).

We finally compute the entanglement
coefficients in (
5.12
), in terms of the Fourier-Taylor coefficients of the Stokes wave.
We use that

\Omega_{j}^{(\mathtt{p})}
and
t_{j}^{(\mathtt{p})}
in (
5.8
) satisfy

\tanh\big(\mathtt{h}(j+\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h}))\big)=\tfrac{\Omega_{j}^{(\mathtt{p})}}{t_{j}^{(\mathtt{p})}}\,,\quad j+\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})=\Omega_{j}^{(\mathtt{p})}t_{j}^{(\mathtt{p})}\,,\quad j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\,,

(5.62)

since
j+\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})>0
for any
j\geq 0
.

Lemma 5.16
.

For any
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}=\pm 1
,
\ell=1,\dots,\mathtt{p}
the entanglement
coefficient
\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\ell}^{[\ell]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{j+\ell,j}^{\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}\end{matrix}
in (
5.5
) has the expression in (
5.12
).

Proof.

By (
5.24
), (
2.21
) and (
5.8
) we have

{\mathcal{B}}_{0,\ell}^{[\pm\ell]}f_{j}^{\sigma}=\frac{e^{\mathrm{i}\,(j\pm\ell)x}}{2\sqrt{2\Omega_{j}^{(\mathtt{p})}}}\begin{bmatrix}-a_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\sqrt{\sigma}\Omega_{j}^{(\mathtt{p})}-\mathrm{i}\,\sqrt{-\sigma}p_{\ell}^{[\ell]}(j+\underline{\mu})\\
-\mathrm{i}\,\sqrt{\sigma}p_{\ell}^{[\ell]}(j\pm\ell+\underline{\mu})\Omega_{j}^{(\mathtt{p})}\end{bmatrix}\epsilon^{\ell}\,,

whence, by (
5.5
), (
2.21
) we obtain

\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\ell}^{[\pm\ell]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{j\pm\ell,j}^{\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}\end{matrix}=\frac{\epsilon^{\ell}}{4\sqrt{\Omega_{j}^{(\mathtt{p})}\Omega_{j\pm\ell}^{(\mathtt{p})}}}\big(\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\sqrt{\sigma^{\prime}}}a_{\ell}^{[\ell]}\Omega_{j}^{(\mathtt{p})}\Omega_{j\pm\ell}^{(\mathtt{p})}+\mathrm{i}\,\sqrt{-\sigma}\overline{\sqrt{\sigma^{\prime}}}p_{\ell}^{[\ell]}(j+\underline{\mu})\Omega_{j\pm\ell}^{(\mathtt{p})}-\mathrm{i}\,\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\sqrt{-\sigma^{\prime}}}p_{\ell}^{[\ell]}(j\pm\ell+\underline{\mu})\Omega_{j}^{(\mathtt{p})}\big)\,.

By exploiting the second identity in (
5.62
)
and using that, for
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}=\pm
,

\mathrm{i}\,\sqrt{-\sigma}\overline{\sqrt{\sigma^{\prime}}}=-\sigma\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\sqrt{\sigma^{\prime}}},\quad-\mathrm{i}\,\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\sqrt{-\sigma^{\prime}}}=-\sigma^{\prime}\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\sqrt{\sigma^{\prime}}},\quad\sqrt{-\sigma}\overline{\sqrt{-\sigma^{\prime}}}=\sigma\sigma^{\prime}\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\sqrt{\sigma^{\prime}}}\,,

we obtain formula (
5.12
).
∎

The function
\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})
is analytic in
\mathtt{h}

as the coefficients

p_{\ell}^{[\ell]}(\mathtt{h})
,
a_{\ell}^{[\ell]}(\mathtt{h})

(cfr. Lemma
2.5
)
and
\Omega_{j}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})
,
t_{j}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})

in (
5.8
).
The proof of Theorem
5.3
is complete.

6
Non-degeneracy of

\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})

In this section we prove that for
any

\mathtt{p}\geq 2

the analytic function
\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})
in (

)
satisfies (
1.5
).

6.1
Proof of the strong conjecture (
S
)

We now provide the
asymptotic behavior of
\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})
as
\mathtt{h}\to 0^{+}
.

Theorem 6.1

(
Shallow-water limit of
\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})
).

For any
\mathtt{p}\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathtt{p}\geq 2
,
the function
\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})
in (

)

\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})=-\sqrt{\frac{\mathtt{p}^{2}-1}{3}}\Big(\frac{3}{8}\Big)^{\mathtt{p}-1}\frac{\mathtt{p}^{2}(\mathtt{p}+1)^{2}}{24}\mathtt{h}^{\frac{11}{2}-3\mathtt{p}}+O\big(\mathtt{h}^{\frac{15}{2}-3\mathtt{p}}\big)

(6.1)

as
\mathtt{h}\to 0^{+}
.

The rest of the section is devoted to the proof of Theorem
6.1
.

The following asymptotic expansions
will be often used in the sequel.

Lemma 6.2
.

For any
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}

the quantities
\Omega_{j}^{(\mathtt{p})}
and

\displaystyle=\mathtt{h}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(j+\frac{\mathtt{p}^{3}-\mathtt{p}-2j^{3}}{12}\mathtt{h}^{2}+\frac{38j^{5}-30j^{2}\mathtt{p}(\mathtt{p}^{2}-1)-4\mathtt{p}^{5}-15\mathtt{p}^{3}+19\mathtt{p}}{720}\mathtt{h}^{4}+O(\mathtt{h}^{6})\Big)\,,

(6.2)

\displaystyle t_{j}^{(\mathtt{p})}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{h}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big(1+\frac{\mathtt{h}^{2}j^{2}}{6}+O(\mathtt{h}^{4})\Big)\,.

It results

\sqrt{\Omega_{0}^{(\mathtt{p})}\Omega_{\mathtt{p}}^{(\mathtt{p})}}=\frac{1}{2}\mathtt{h}^{\frac{3}{2}}\mathtt{p}\sqrt{\frac{\mathtt{p}^{2}-1}{3}}\Big(1-\frac{3\mathtt{p}^{2}+8}{40}\mathtt{h}^{2}+O(\mathtt{h}^{4})\Big)\,.

(6.3)

Furthermore

in (

,

\sigma=\pm
,

\displaystyle\omega_{j}^{-}-\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})}

\displaystyle=2j\mathtt{h}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(1-\frac{j^{2}+1}{12}\mathtt{h}^{2}+O(\mathtt{h}^{4})\Big)\,.

(6.4)

Furthermore for any

and

\displaystyle a_{\ell}^{[\ell]}-p_{\ell}^{[\ell]}(\sigma t_{j}^{(\mathtt{p})}+\sigma^{\prime}t_{j+\ell}^{(\mathtt{p})})

(6.5)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}3\ell\Big(\dfrac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-1}\mathtt{h}^{2-3\ell}\big(1+\dfrac{19\ell^{2}+12j\ell-27\ell+12j^{2}+2}{54}\mathtt{h}^{2}+O(\mathtt{h}^{4})\big)\,,&\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}=+\,,\\[5.69054pt]
-5\ell\Big(\dfrac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-1}\mathtt{h}^{2-3\ell}\big(1+O(\mathtt{h}^{2})\big)\,,&\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}=-\,,\\[5.69054pt]
-\ell\Big(\dfrac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-1}\mathtt{h}^{2-3\ell}\big(1+\dfrac{25\ell^{2}-12j\ell-9\ell+2}{18}\mathtt{h}^{2}+O(\mathtt{h}^{4})\big)\,,&\sigma\neq\sigma^{\prime}=+\,,\\[5.69054pt]
-\ell\Big(\dfrac{3}{8}\Big)^{\ell-1}\mathtt{h}^{2-3\ell}\big(1+O(\mathtt{h}^{2})\big)\,,&\sigma\neq\sigma^{\prime}=-\,.\end{cases}

Proof.

By (
5.8
) and (
2.28
) we deduce (
6.2
) and (
6.3
). Similarly (

), (

) and (

).

.
∎

We first show that many of the addends in (

), namely those with

,

This is an important computational simplification.

Lemma 6.3
.

For any integer
q=1,\dots,\mathtt{p}-1
and any signs
\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{q}\in\{\pm\}
we denote
\vec{\sigma}:=(\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{q})
and its cardinality by

|\vec{\sigma}|:=|\{\sigma_{j}=-\}|
. Then, for any

0<j_{1}<\dots<j_{q}<\mathtt{p}
,
the term

[b_{q}^{(\mathtt{p})}]_{j_{1},\dots,j_{q}}^{\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{q}}

in (
5.11
) satisfies, as
\mathtt{h}\to 0^{+}
,

[b_{q}^{(\mathtt{p})}]_{j_{1},\dots,j_{q}}^{\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{q}}=O\big(\mathtt{h}^{\frac{7}{2}-3\mathtt{p}+2|\vec{\sigma}|}\big)\,.

(6.6)

Proof.

By (
5.12
), (
6.2
) and (
6.5
), for any
j,\ell>0

we have

\epsilon^{-\ell}\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\ell}^{[\ell]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{j+\ell,j}^{\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}\end{matrix}=O(\mathtt{h}^{\frac{5}{2}-3\ell})
whereas when
j=0
using (
6.3
) we get
\epsilon^{-\ell}\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\ell}^{[\ell]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{\ell,0}^{\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}\end{matrix}=O(\mathtt{h}^{\frac{7}{2}-3\ell})
.
Therefore

\epsilon^{-\mathtt{p}}\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{j_{1}}^{[j_{1}]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{j_{1},0}^{\sigma_{1},-}\end{matrix}\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{j_{2}-j_{1}}^{[j_{2}-j_{1}]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{j_{2},j_{1}}^{\sigma_{2},\sigma_{1}}\end{matrix}\dots\begin{matrix}{\mathtt{B}_{\mathtt{p}-j_{q}}^{[\mathtt{p}-j_{q}]}}&{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}{|}^{|}}}_{\mathtt{p},j_{q}}^{+,\sigma_{q}}\end{matrix}=O(\mathtt{h}^{\frac{5}{2}q+\frac{7}{2}-3\mathtt{p}})\,.

(6.7)

On the other hand, by (
6.4
) and since
j_{i}\geq 1

and
j_{i}^{3}-j_{i}+\mathtt{p}-\mathtt{p}^{3}<0
for any

, we deduce that

(\omega_{j_{1}}^{\sigma_{1}}-\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})})\dots(\omega_{j_{q}}^{\sigma_{q}}-\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})})\sim C_{j_{1},\dots,j_{q}}^{\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{q}}\mathtt{h}^{\frac{5}{2}(q-|\vec{\sigma}|)}\mathtt{h}^{\frac{1}{2}|\vec{\sigma}|}\qquad\text{for some}\quad C_{j_{1},\dots,j_{q}}^{\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{q}}\neq 0\,.

(6.8)

The estimate (
6.6
) follows by (
5.11
), (
6.7
) and (
6.8
).
∎

We now exhibit the explicit asymptotic expansion of the function
\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})
in (

). We provide the expansion up to the order

since,

vanishes.

Proposition 6.4
.

The function
\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})
in (
5.11
) admits the asymptotic expansion, as
\mathtt{h}\to 0^{+}
,

\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})=-\sqrt{\frac{\mathtt{p}^{2}-1}{3}}\frac{3^{\mathtt{p}-1}\mathtt{p}}{8^{\mathtt{p}}}\Big\{A^{(\mathtt{p})}\mathtt{h}^{\frac{7}{2}-3\mathtt{p}}+\Big[\Big(\frac{299}{1080}\mathtt{p}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\mathtt{p}-\frac{4}{45}\Big)A^{(\mathtt{p})}+C^{(\mathtt{p})}\Big]\mathtt{h}^{\frac{11}{2}-3\mathtt{p}}+O(\mathtt{h}^{\frac{15}{2}-3\mathtt{p}})\Big\}

(6.9)

where

\displaystyle A^{(\mathtt{p})}:=\mathtt{p}+\sum_{q=1}^{\mathtt{p}-1}\sum_{0<j_{1}<\dots<j_{q}<\mathtt{p}}\mathtt{n}_{q}^{(\mathtt{p})}(j_{1},\dots,j_{q})\,,

(6.10)

\displaystyle C^{(\mathtt{p})}:=\frac{28}{27}\mathtt{p}^{3}+\sum_{q=1}^{\mathtt{p}-1}\sum_{0<j_{1}<\dots<j_{q}<\mathtt{p}}\mathtt{n}_{q}^{(\mathtt{p})}(j_{1},\dots,j_{q})\mathtt{g}^{(\mathtt{p})}_{q}(j_{1},\dots,j_{q})\,,

(6.11)

with

\mathtt{n}_{q}^{(\mathtt{p})}(j_{1},\dots,j_{q}):=(-12)^{q}j_{1}\dots j_{q}\frac{j_{1}(j_{2}-j_{1})\dots(j_{q}-j_{q-1})(\mathtt{p}-j_{q})}{(\mathtt{p}^{3}-\mathtt{p}+j_{1}-j_{1}^{3})\dots(\mathtt{p}^{3}-\mathtt{p}+j_{q}-j_{q}^{3})}

(6.12)

and

\displaystyle\mathtt{g}^{(\mathtt{p})}_{q}(j_{1},\dots,j_{q}):=\frac{1}{3}\Big[\frac{49}{45}q+\frac{32}{9}j_{1}^{2}-\frac{4}{9}-\frac{4}{9}\frac{\mathtt{p}^{3}-\mathtt{p}}{j_{1}}+\frac{5}{18}(\mathtt{p}^{3}-\mathtt{p})\Big(\frac{1}{j_{1}}+\dots+\frac{1}{j_{q}}\Big)

(6.13)

\displaystyle+\frac{38}{15}\big(j_{1}^{2}+\dots+j_{q}^{2}\big)+\frac{\mathtt{p}^{3}-\mathtt{p}}{5}\Big(\frac{\mathtt{p}+j_{1}}{\mathtt{p}^{2}+j_{1}^{2}+\mathtt{p}j_{1}-1}+\dots+\frac{\mathtt{p}+j_{q}}{\mathtt{p}^{2}+j_{q}^{2}+\mathtt{p}j_{q}-1}\Big)-\frac{13}{9}\big(j_{1}j_{2}+j_{2}j_{3}+\dots+j_{q}\mathtt{p}\big)\Big]\,.

Proof.

We split the proof in different steps.

Step 1: reduction
. Lemma
6.3
implies that the asymptotic expansion of
\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})
as
\mathtt{h}\to 0^{+}
is given only by terms with at most one minus sign appearing in (
5.11
), i.e.

\displaystyle\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})

\displaystyle=b_{0}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})+\sum_{q=1}^{\mathtt{p}-1}\sum_{0<j_{1}<\dots<j_{q}<\mathtt{p}}[b_{q}^{(\mathtt{p})}]_{j_{1},\dots,j_{q}}^{+,\dots,+}(\mathtt{h})

(6.14)

\displaystyle-\sum_{q=1}^{\mathtt{p}-1}\sum_{i=1}^{q}\sum_{0<j_{1}<\dots<j_{q}<\mathtt{p}}[b_{q}^{(\mathtt{p})}]_{j_{1},\dots,j_{i-1},j_{i},j_{i+1},\dots,j_{q}}^{+,\dots,\ +\ ,\ -,\ +,\dots,+}(\mathtt{h})+O\big(\mathtt{h}^{\frac{15}{2}-3\mathtt{p}}\big)\,.

We compute the expansion at
\mathtt{h}\to 0^{+}
of these coefficients.

Step
2
: computation of

.

By (
5.11
), (
5.12
), (
6.3
) and (

\displaystyle b_{0}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})=-\sqrt{\frac{\mathtt{p}^{2}-1}{3}}\frac{3^{\mathtt{p}-1}\mathtt{p}}{8^{\mathtt{p}}}\Big[\mathtt{p}\mathtt{h}^{\frac{7}{2}-3\mathtt{p}}+\mathtt{b}_{0}^{(\mathtt{p})}\mathtt{h}^{\frac{11}{2}-3\mathtt{p}}+O\big(\mathtt{h}^{\frac{15}{2}-3\mathtt{p}}\big)\Big]

(6.15)

with

\mathtt{b}_{0}^{(\mathtt{p})}:=\Big(\frac{473}{360}\mathtt{p}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\mathtt{p}-\frac{4}{45}\Big)\mathtt{p}\,.

(6.16)

Step
3
: computation of
[b_{q}^{(\mathtt{p})}]_{j_{1},\dots,j_{q}}^{+,\dots,+}(\mathtt{h})
.

For any
q=1,\dots,\mathtt{p}-1
and any
integer

, using (

) with
\sigma_{1}=\dots=\sigma_{q}=+

)-(

(6.17)

where, neglecting terms

,

\displaystyle N^{+}

\displaystyle:=\Big(\frac{1}{4}\Big)^{q+1}\sqrt{\Omega_{0}^{(\mathtt{p})}\Omega_{\mathtt{p}}^{(\mathtt{p})}}\Omega_{j_{1}}^{(\mathtt{p})}\dots\Omega_{j_{q}}^{(\mathtt{p})}\big(a_{j_{1}}^{[j_{1}]}-p_{j_{1}}^{[j_{1}]}(-t_{0}^{(\mathtt{p})}+t_{j_{1}}^{(\mathtt{p})})\big)

(6.18)

\displaystyle\ \ \times\big(a_{j_{2}-j_{1}}^{[j_{2}-j_{1}]}-p_{j_{2}-j_{1}}^{[j_{2}-j_{1}]}(t_{j_{1}}^{(\mathtt{p})}+t_{j_{2}}^{(\mathtt{p})})\big)\dots\big(a_{\mathtt{p}-j_{q}}^{[\mathtt{p}-j_{q}]}-p_{\mathtt{p}-j_{q}}^{[\mathtt{p}-j_{q}]}(t_{\mathtt{p}}^{(\mathtt{p})}+t_{j_{q}}^{(\mathtt{p})})\big)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{h}^{\frac{7}{2}+\frac{5}{2}q-3\mathtt{p}}\frac{3^{\mathtt{p}-1}}{8^{\mathtt{p}}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathtt{p}^{2}-1}{3}}\mathtt{p}2^{q}\Big(1-\tfrac{3\mathtt{p}^{2}+8}{40}\mathtt{h}^{2}\Big)j_{1}\dots j_{q}\prod_{i=1}^{q}\Big(1+\tfrac{\mathtt{p}^{3}-\mathtt{p}-2j_{i}^{3}}{12j_{i}}\mathtt{h}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\ \ \times(-j_{1})(j_{2}-j_{1})\dots(\mathtt{p}-j_{q})\big(1+\tfrac{25j_{1}^{2}-9j_{1}+2}{18}\mathtt{h}^{2}\big)\Big[\prod_{i=1}^{q-1}\big(1+\tfrac{19(j_{i+1}-j_{i})^{2}+12j_{i}(j_{i+1}-j_{i})-27(j_{i+1}-j_{i})+12j_{i}^{2}+2}{54}\mathtt{h}^{2}\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\ \ \times\big(1+\tfrac{19(\mathtt{p}-j_{q})^{2}+12j_{q}(\mathtt{p}-j_{q})-27(\mathtt{p}-j_{q})+12j_{q}^{2}+2}{54}\mathtt{h}^{2}\big)

and

D^{+}:=\mathtt{h}^{\frac{5}{2}q}(-6)^{-q}\underbrace{(\mathtt{p}^{3}-\mathtt{p}-j_{1}^{3}+j_{1})\dots(\mathtt{p}^{3}-\mathtt{p}-j_{q}^{3}+j_{q})}_{=:D_{1}^{+}}\prod_{i=1}^{q}\Big[1-\Big(\tfrac{19}{60}(j_{i}^{2}+1)+\tfrac{1}{15}\tfrac{\mathtt{p}(\mathtt{p}^{2}-1)(j_{i}+\mathtt{p})}{j_{i}^{2}+j_{i}\mathtt{p}+\mathtt{p}^{2}-1}\Big)\mathtt{h}^{2}\Big]\,.

(6.19)

We write the terms
N^{+},D^{+}

in (
6.18
) and
(

Lemma 6.9
.

Proof.

Identity

\displaystyle S_{j_{1},\dots,j_{q}}=-4\nu_{j_{q}}^{(\mathtt{p})}j_{q}\,S_{j_{1},\dots,j_{q-1}}\,,\ \forall q\geq 2\,,

(6.40)

\displaystyle S_{j_{1}}=0\,.

(6.41)

Proof of (

).

For any

we have
