(6.43)

with

\displaystyle\Delta_{q}\,:=(1-\sigma_{q-1}\nu_{j_{q-1}}^{(\mathtt{p})}-\nu_{j_{q}}^{(\mathtt{p})})(1-\nu_{j_{q}}^{(\mathtt{p})}-\nu_{\mathtt{p}}^{(\mathtt{p})})(2j_{q}-\mathtt{p}+1+2\nu_{j_{q}}^{(\mathtt{p})})

(6.44)

\displaystyle-(1-\sigma_{q-1}\nu_{j_{q-1}}^{(\mathtt{p})}+\nu_{j_{q}}^{(\mathtt{p})})(1+\nu_{j_{q}}^{(\mathtt{p})}-\nu_{\mathtt{p}}^{(\mathtt{p})})(2j_{q}-\mathtt{p}+1-2\nu_{j_{q}}^{(\mathtt{p})})\,.

Setting

a:=1-\sigma_{q-1}\nu_{j_{q-1}}^{(\mathtt{p})}\,,\quad b:=1-\nu_{\mathtt{p}}^{(\mathtt{p})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{tjpinfinito}}}{{=}}-\frac{\mathtt{p}-1}{2}\,,\quad c:=2j_{q}-\mathtt{p}+1\,,\quad\nu:=\nu_{j_{q}}^{(\mathtt{p})}\,,

(6.45)

the term
\Delta_{q}
in (
6.44
) is given by

\displaystyle\Delta_{q}

\displaystyle=(a-\nu)(b-\nu)(c+2\nu)-(a+\nu)(b+\nu)(c-2\nu)=\big(4ab-2(a+b)c+4\nu^{2}\big)\nu

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{setabcv}}}{{=}}-4\nu_{j_{q}}^{(\mathtt{p})}j_{q}\Big(\frac{1-\mathtt{p}}{2}-\sigma_{q-1}\nu_{j_{q-1}}^{(\mathtt{p})}\Big)=-4\nu_{j_{q}}^{(\mathtt{p})}j_{q}\Big(1-\sigma_{q-1}\nu_{j_{q-1}}^{(\mathtt{p})}-\nu_{\mathtt{p}}^{(\mathtt{p})}\Big)

(6.46)

since
\frac{\mathtt{p}+1}{2}=\nu_{\mathtt{p}}^{(\mathtt{p})}

by (
6.35
).
By (
6.42
), (
6.43
), (
6.46
) we conclude that

S_{j_{1},\dots,j_{q}}=-4\nu_{j_{q}}^{(\mathtt{p})}j_{q}\,S_{j_{1},\dots,j_{q-1}}=(-4)^{q-1}\nu_{j_{2}}^{(\mathtt{p})}j_{2}\dots\nu_{j_{q}}^{(\mathtt{p})}j_{q}\,S_{j_{1}}\,.

Proof of (
6.41
).

By (
6.38
),
S_{j_{1}}=B_{j_{1}}^{+}-B_{j_{1}}^{-}=:\Delta_{1}
which is still given by formula (
6.44
)
setting
(j_{0},\sigma_{0}):=(0,-)
. By (
6.46
) we have

S_{j_{1}}=\Delta_{1}=-4\nu_{j_{1}}^{(\mathtt{p})}j_{1}\Big(1+\nu_{0}^{(\mathtt{p})}-\frac{\mathtt{p}+1}{2}\Big)=0

by (
6.35
).
The proof of the lemma is complete.
∎

Proof of Proposition
6.7
.
In view of (
5.11
), (
6.36
) and Lemma
6.9
we conclude that

\lim_{\mathtt{h}\to+\infty}\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})=\lim_{\mathtt{h}\to+\infty}b_{0}^{(\mathtt{p})}+\sum_{q=1}^{\mathtt{p}-1}\sum_{0<j_{1}<\dots<j_{q}<\mathtt{p}}A_{j_{1},\dots,j_{q}}\sum_{\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{q}=\pm}\sigma_{1}\dots\sigma_{q}B_{j_{1},\dots,j_{q}}^{\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{q}}=0

as claimed. ∎

Proof of Theorem
2.11
.
Theorem
2.11
follows by Theorems
5.3
,
6.1
and Proposition
6.7
. ∎

Remark 6.10
.

If
\mathtt{p}=2,3,4
, inserting in (
5.11
)-(
5.12
) the explicit values of the Taylor-Fourier coefficients
p_{\ell}^{[\ell]}
,
a_{\ell}^{[\ell]}
,
\ell=1,\dots,4
computed in
[
9
, Proposition A.7]
we have explicit
formulas for
\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})
.
They are plotted in Figures
3
,
obtained by the Mathematica sheet
beta1.nb

encoded in the Mathematica library
CoeffsLin.m
, collected in

https://git-scm.sissa.it/amaspero/isolas
.
The zeros of
\beta_{1}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})

coincide, for
\mathtt{p}=2,3
, with the numerical
values given in
[
19
,
30
]
.

Appendix A
Spectral collisions

In this Appendix we prove Lemmata
2.7
and
2.9
.

Proof of Lemma
2.7
.
We shall use that, since
\Omega(\varphi,\mathtt{h})=\sqrt{\varphi\tanh(\mathtt{h}\varphi)}

is strictly concave for
\varphi>0

(cfr. (
2.20
))
and
\Omega(0,\mathtt{h})=0
, then it is strictly sub-additive

\Omega(\varphi+z,\mathtt{h})<\Omega(\varphi,\mathtt{h})+\Omega(z,\mathtt{h})\,,\qquad\forall\varphi,z>0\,,

(A.1)

and

\Omega(z,\mathtt{h})<z\Omega(1,\mathtt{h})\,,\quad\forall z>1\,.

(A.2)

Proof of Item
i
)
.
We first prove that the equations

\omega^{\sigma}(\varphi,\mathtt{h})=\omega^{\sigma}(\varphi+\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})
,
for any
\sigma=\pm
,
have no solution, see Figure
4
.
Recalling (
2.19
),

\omega^{+}(\varphi,\mathtt{h})=\omega^{+}(\varphi+\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})\qquad\Leftrightarrow\qquad{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\mathtt{p}-\Omega(\varphi+\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})=-\Omega(\varphi,\mathtt{h})\,.

(A.3)

By (
A.2
)
the function
d(\varphi,\mathtt{h}):={\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\mathtt{p}-\Omega(\varphi+\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})

satisfies
d(0,\mathtt{h})>0
(since
\mathtt{p}\geq 2
) and

d(\varphi,\mathtt{h})>-\Omega(\varphi+\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})\geq-\Omega(\varphi,\mathtt{h})

for any
\varphi\leq-\mathtt{p}
as well as

d(\varphi,\mathtt{h})>0>-\Omega(\varphi,\mathtt{h})

for any
\varphi\in(-\mathtt{p},0)
, see the right hand side of
Figure
4
. For any
\varphi>0

we deduce by (
A.1
) and (
A.2
) that

\Omega(\varphi+\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})<\Omega(\varphi,\mathtt{h})+{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\mathtt{p}
and therefore
the equation (
A.3
) has no solutions.

Since
\omega^{-}(\varphi,\mathtt{h})=-\omega^{+}(-\varphi,\mathtt{h})

the equation
\omega^{-}(\varphi,\mathtt{h})=\omega^{-}(\varphi+\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})

has no solutions as well.

Proof of Item
ii
)
.
We now prove that
there exists a unique positive solution

\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})>0
of

\omega^{-}(\varphi,\mathtt{h})=\omega^{+}(\varphi+\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})\qquad\Leftrightarrow\qquad{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\mathtt{p}-\Omega(\varphi+\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})=\Omega(\varphi,\mathtt{h})\,.

(A.4)

Indeed

d(\varphi,\mathtt{h})={\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\mathtt{p}-\Omega(\varphi+\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})

satisfies
d(0,\mathtt{h})>0
and it
is strictly decreasing to
-\infty
,
whereas
\Omega(0,\mathtt{h})=0
and

\Omega(\cdot,\mathtt{h})
is strictly increasing to
+\infty
,
see Figure
5
. This proves (
2.24
).
By simmetry

\varphi=-\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})-\mathtt{p}
is the unique
solution of (
A.4
)
in
(-\infty,-\mathtt{p})
proving (
2.25
).
We claim that for
-\mathtt{p}\leq\varphi\leq 0
there are no further intersection points
between the graphs of
d(\cdot,\mathtt{h})
and
\Omega(\cdot,\mathtt{h})

if
\mathtt{p}\geq 3
and one intersection point
\varphi=-\frac{\mathtt{p}}{2}=-1
if
\mathtt{p}=2
,
see Figure
5
.
Indeed
d(\varphi,\mathtt{h})>\Omega(\varphi,\mathtt{h})
, for
\varphi\in[-\mathtt{p},0]
, if and only if

\mathtt{p}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}>\Omega\Big(\frac{\mathtt{p}}{2}-y,\mathtt{h}\Big)+\Omega\Big(\frac{\mathtt{p}}{2}+y,\mathtt{h}\Big)=:g(y)\,,\quad\forall y\in\Big[-\frac{\mathtt{p}}{2},\frac{\mathtt{p}}{2}\Big]\,.

The function
g
is even and, by (
2.20
), strictly concave. Hence
it attains at
y=0
its maximum value

g(0)=2\Omega\Big(\frac{\mathtt{p}}{2},\mathtt{h}\Big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{valzero}}}{{<}}2\frac{\mathtt{p}}{2}\Omega\big(1,\mathtt{h}\big)=\mathtt{p}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\,,\quad\forall\mathtt{p}\geq 3\,,

with equality holding for
\mathtt{p}=2
.

Proof of Item
iii
)
.
It follows because
\omega^{-}(\varphi,\mathtt{h})=-\omega^{+}(-\varphi,\mathtt{h})

amounts to

\Omega(\varphi,\mathtt{h})+\Omega(\varphi+\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})=-{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\mathtt{p}
and the function
\Omega(\varphi,\mathtt{h})
is non-negative.

We now prove the properties of the positive function
\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})>0

stated in Lemma
2.7
.

Proof of Item
1
.

For any
\mathtt{h}>0

we have

\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{p}}(\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h}),\mathtt{h})=0\qquad\text{where}\qquad\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{p}}(\varphi,\mathtt{h}):=\Omega(\varphi,\mathtt{h})+\Omega(\varphi+\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})-{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\mathtt{p}\,.

(A.5)

Since
\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{p}}(\varphi,\mathtt{h})
is analytic and

\partial_{\varphi}\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{p}}(\varphi,\mathtt{h})>0
by (
2.20
),
we deduce by the analytic implicit function theorem
that
\mathtt{h}\mapsto\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})
is analytic.

Proof of item
2
.

By (
A.5
) and recalling that
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}=\Omega(1,\mathtt{h})

we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{p}+1}(\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h}),\mathtt{h})

\displaystyle=\Omega(\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h}),\mathtt{h})+\Omega(\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})+\mathtt{p}+1,\mathtt{h})-{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}(\mathtt{p}+1)

\displaystyle=\Omega(\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})+\mathtt{p}+1,\mathtt{h})-\Omega(\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})+\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})-\Omega(1,\mathtt{h})<0

(A.6)

by the sub-additivity property in
(
A.1
) with
\varphi=\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})+\mathtt{p}
and
z=1
.
Then (
A.6
) and the fact that

\partial_{\varphi}\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{p}+1}(\varphi,\mathtt{h})>0

imply that
\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})<\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p}+1,\mathtt{h})
, for any
\mathtt{p}\geq 2
, proving
the first part of (
2.26
).
For any
\mathtt{h}>0

the monotone sequence

\mathtt{p}\mapsto\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})
admits a limit as
\mathtt{p}\to+\infty
.
Let us prove that
\lim_{\mathtt{p}\to+\infty}\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})=+\infty
.
By contradiction suppose that
\sup_{\mathtt{p}\geq 2}\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})<+\infty
is finite.
Then by (
A.5
) we deduce the contradiction

{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}=\lim_{\mathtt{p}\to\infty}\mathtt{p}^{-1}\Omega(\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h}))+\mathtt{p}^{-1}\Omega(\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})+\mathtt{p})=0
.

Proof of item
3
.

Recalling (
A.5
),
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}=\Omega(1,\mathtt{h})
, (
A.2
) and
that

\Omega(\varphi,\mathtt{h})
is strictly increasing for
\varphi>0
,
we have that

\mathcal{F}_{2}(0,\mathtt{h})=\Omega(2,\mathtt{h})-2\Omega(1,\mathtt{h})<0\,,\quad\mathcal{F}_{2}(1,\mathtt{h})=\Omega(3,\mathtt{h})-\Omega(1,\mathtt{h})>0\,,\quad\forall\mathtt{h}>0\,,

and therefore the unique zero
\underline{\varphi}(2,\mathtt{h})>0
of

\mathcal{F}_{2}(\varphi,\mathtt{h})=0

satisfies
\underline{\varphi}(2,\mathtt{h})\in(0,1)
for any
\mathtt{h}>0
.

For
\mathtt{p}\geq 3
we already know by Item
2
that

\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{p}}(\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p}-1,\mathtt{h}),\mathtt{h})<0

and, since
\tfrac{1}{4}(\mathtt{p}-1)^{2}\geq 1
for any
\mathtt{p}\geq 3
, we have

\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{p}}\big(\tfrac{1}{4}(\mathtt{p}-1)^{2},\mathtt{h}\big)=\frac{\mathtt{p}-1}{2}\underbrace{\sqrt{\tanh\big(\tfrac{\mathtt{h}}{4}(\mathtt{p}-1)^{2}\big)}}_{\geq\sqrt{\tanh(\mathtt{h})}}+\frac{\mathtt{p}+1}{2}\underbrace{\sqrt{\tanh\big(\tfrac{\mathtt{h}}{4}(\mathtt{p}+1)^{2}\big)}}_{>\sqrt{\tanh(\mathtt{h})}}-\mathtt{p}\sqrt{\tanh(\mathtt{h})}>0\,.

(A.7)

Therefore, since
\Omega(\varphi,\mathtt{h})
is strictly increasing for
\varphi>0
,
we deduce that

\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})\in\big(0,\tfrac{1}{4}(\mathtt{p}-1)^{2}\big)
for any
\mathtt{p}\geq 3
.
Furthermore,
by the asymptotic expansion

\sqrt{\tanh(z)}=\Big(1-2\frac{e^{-2z}}{1+e^{-2z}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}=1-e^{-2z}+O(e^{-4z})\qquad\text{as}\qquad z\to+\infty\,,

(A.8)

we deduce that

\mathcal{F}_{2}\Big(\frac{1}{4},\mathtt{h}\Big)=\frac{1}{2}\big(1-e^{-\frac{\mathtt{h}}{2}}+O(e^{-\mathtt{h}})\big)+\frac{3}{2}\big(1+O(e^{-\mathtt{h}})\big)-2\big(1+O(e^{-\mathtt{h}})\big)<0\,,\quad\text{for }\mathtt{h}\geq\mathtt{h}_{*}\textup{ large enough}\,,

which implies that
\underline{\varphi}(2,\mathtt{h})>1/4
for any
\mathtt{h}\geq\mathtt{h}_{*}
.

Proof of item
4
.

For any
\mathtt{p}\geq 2
, by the previous item the function
\mathtt{h}\mapsto\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})
is bounded and

\mathtt{h}\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})\to 0^{+}
as
\mathtt{h}\to 0^{+}
. By (
A.5
)
and the Taylor expansion

\sqrt{\tanh(z)}=\sqrt{z}\big(1-\frac{z^{2}}{6}+\frac{19}{360}z^{4}+O(z^{6})\big)
as
z\to 0^{+}

we first deduce that
\underline{\varphi}:=\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})

\displaystyle\underline{\varphi}\Big(1-\tfrac{1}{6}\mathtt{h}^{2}\underline{\varphi}^{2}+\tfrac{19}{360}\mathtt{h}^{4}\underline{\varphi}^{4}+O(\mathtt{h}^{6})\Big)+\big(\underline{\varphi}+\mathtt{p}\big)\Big(1-\tfrac{1}{6}\mathtt{h}^{2}\big(\underline{\varphi}+\mathtt{p}\big)^{2}+\tfrac{19}{360}\mathtt{h}^{4}\big(\underline{\varphi}+\mathtt{p}\big)^{4}+O(\mathtt{h}^{6})\Big)

(A.9)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{p}\Big(1-\tfrac{1}{6}\mathtt{h}^{2}+\tfrac{19}{360}\mathtt{h}^{4}+O(\mathtt{h}^{6})\Big)\,,

and then

\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})\big(1+\mathcal{O}(\mathtt{h}^{2})\big)=\frac{\mathtt{p}}{12}\mathtt{h}^{2}\big[(\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})+\mathtt{p})^{2}-1]+O(\mathtt{h}^{4})\,.

This proves that
\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})=O(\mathtt{h}^{2})
as
\mathtt{h}\to 0^{+}

and then reinserting the result in (
A.9
) we obtain the sharper Taylor expansion
in (
2.28
).

In order to prove that

\lim_{\mathtt{h}\to+\infty}\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})=(\mathtt{p}-1)^{2}/4

it is sufficient to show that
for any sequence
\mathtt{h}_{n}\to\ +\infty
there is a subsequence

\mathtt{h}_{n_{k}}
such that
\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h}_{n_{k}})\to(\mathtt{p}-1)^{2}/4
.
By item
3
we have that

1/4\leq\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h}_{n})\leq(\mathtt{p}-1)^{2}/4<+\infty
for
n\geq n_{0}
large enough.
By compactness, there exists a subsequence
\mathtt{h}_{n_{k}}\to+\infty

such that
\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h}_{n_{k}})\to\ell\in[1/4,(\mathtt{p}-1)^{2}/4]
. Thus
by (
A.5
) we deduce that

\underset{k\to+\infty}{\lim}\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{p}}(\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h}_{n_{k}}),\mathtt{h}_{n_{k}})=\sqrt{\ell}+\sqrt{\ell+\mathtt{p}}-\mathtt{p}=0\,,

(A.10)

namely
\ell=\tfrac{1}{4}(\mathtt{p}-1)^{2}
, being the unique solution of (
A.10
).
This proves that
\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})\to\tfrac{1}{4}(\mathtt{p}-1)^{2}
as
\mathtt{h}\to+\infty
.

We now prove the sharper asymptotic expansion in (
2.29
).
By (
A.5
) and (
A.8
) we have, as
\mathtt{h}\to+\infty
,

0=\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{p}}(\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h}),\mathtt{h})=\underbrace{\sqrt{\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})}+\sqrt{\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})+\mathtt{p}}-\mathtt{p}}_{=\frac{2\mathtt{p}}{\mathtt{p}^{2}-1}y_{\mathtt{p}}(\mathtt{h})+O(y_{\mathtt{p}}^{2}(\mathtt{h}))}-\sqrt{\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})}e^{-2\mathtt{h}\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})}+\mathtt{p}e^{-2\mathtt{h}}+O(e^{-4\mathtt{h}\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})})+O(e^{-4\mathtt{h}})

(A.11)

writing
\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})=\frac{(\mathtt{p}-1)^{2}}{4}+y_{\mathtt{p}}(\mathtt{h})
where
y_{\mathtt{p}}(\mathtt{h})\to 0

as
\mathtt{h}\to+\infty
.
We first consider the case
\mathtt{p}=2
.
By Items
2
and
3

we know that

y_{2}(\mathtt{h})\to 0^{+}
as
\mathtt{h}\to+\infty
.
Then, by (
A.11
)
and since
e^{-2\mathtt{h}y_{2}(\mathtt{h})}\leq 1
, we deduce that

\frac{4}{3}y_{2}(\mathtt{h})=\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}+y_{2}(\mathtt{h})}\,e^{-\frac{\mathtt{h}}{2}}e^{-2\mathtt{h}y_{2}(\mathtt{h})}+O(y_{2}^{2}(\mathtt{h}))+O(e^{-\mathtt{h}})\,.

(A.12)

Therefore
|y_{2}(\mathtt{h})|\leq Ce^{-\frac{\mathtt{h}}{2}}
and then,
reinserting this bound in (
A.12
) we deduce that

y_{2}(\mathtt{h})=\tfrac{3}{8}e^{-\frac{\mathtt{h}}{2}}+o(e^{-\frac{\mathtt{h}}{2}})
which is
the asymptotic
expansion (
2.29
) for
\mathtt{p}=2
.
The cases
\mathtt{p}=3
and
\mathtt{p}\geq 4
follow similarly.
By (
A.11
),
writing
\underline{\varphi}(3,\mathtt{h})=1+y_{3}(\mathtt{h})
where
y_{3}(\mathtt{h})\to 0^{-}
, we have

\frac{3}{4}y_{3}(\mathtt{h})=-3e^{-2\mathtt{h}}+\sqrt{1+y_{3}(\mathtt{h})}e^{-2\mathtt{h}}e^{-2\mathtt{h}y_{3}(\mathtt{h})}+O(y_{3}^{2}(\mathtt{h}))+O(e^{-4\mathtt{h}})

and therefore (
2.29
) for
\mathtt{p}=3
follows.
Finally for any
\mathtt{p}\geq 4
we deduce by (
A.11
)
and since
\frac{(\mathtt{p}-1)^{2}}{4}>1
that

\frac{2\mathtt{p}}{\mathtt{p}^{2}-1}y_{\mathtt{p}}(\mathtt{h})=-\mathtt{p}e^{-2\mathtt{h}}+O(y_{\mathtt{p}}(\mathtt{h})^{2})+O(e^{-4\mathtt{h}})

and therefore (
2.29
) holds for any
\mathtt{p}\geq 4
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
2.9
.
In view of (
2.18
) the operator
{\mathcal{L}}_{\mu,0}
in (
2.17
) has a non-simple eigenvalue if and only if the equation

\lambda_{j}^{\sigma}(\mu,\mathtt{h})=\lambda_{j^{\prime}}^{\sigma^{\prime}}(\mu,\mathtt{h})

has a solution
(j,\sigma)\neq(j^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})
with
j,j^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}
and
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}=\pm
. This amounts to solve

\omega^{\sigma}(j+\mu,\mathtt{h})=\omega^{\sigma^{\prime}}(j+\mu+\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})\quad\text{for some}\quad\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\{\pm\}\,,\ j\in\mathbb{Z}\,,\ \mathtt{p}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\,,

namely

\omega^{\sigma}(\varphi,\mathtt{h})=\omega^{\sigma^{\prime}}(\varphi+\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})\quad\text{for some}\quad\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\{\pm\}\,,\ \mathtt{p}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\,,\ \varphi-\mu\in\mathbb{Z}\,.

(A.13)

In view of Lemma
2.7
and Remark
2.8

the equation (
A.13
) has a solution
with

\omega^{\sigma}(\varphi,\mathtt{h})=\omega^{\sigma^{\prime}}(\varphi+\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})\neq 0

if and only
if
\sigma=-
,
\sigma^{\prime}=+
,
\mathtt{p}\geq 2
and
\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})-\mu\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Then Item
1
follows. For Item
2
, if
\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})-\mu=:k\in\mathbb{Z}
then,
in view of (
2.24
), (
2.18
), (
2.21
) and since
\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})\neq 0
then

\mathrm{i}\,\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})
in (
2.30
)
is a double eigenvalue of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\mu,0}

with associated eigenvectors (
2.32
).
Furthermore
(
2.31
) follows by (
2.24
)
and (
2.26
) because
\varphi\mapsto{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\varphi+\Omega(\varphi,\mathtt{h})
is increasing for

\varphi>0
.
Finally by (
2.25
),
the opposite double eigenvalue
-\mathrm{i}\,\omega_{*}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\mathtt{h})

is obtained for
\varphi=-\underline{\varphi}(\mathtt{p},\mathtt{h})-\mathtt{p}
and belongs to the spectrum of
{\mathcal{L}}_{-\mu,0}
.
∎

Appendix B
Proof of the cancellation (
6.33
)

In this Appendix we prove (
6.33
).
We first show
in Proposition
B.1

that
A^{(\mathtt{p})}
is proportional to the determinant of a
\mathtt{p}\times\mathtt{p}
matrix
\mathrm{I\!I\!I}
. Then
in Lemma
B.2
we show that

\det\mathrm{I\!I\!I}=0
and so
A^{(\mathtt{p})}=0
.

Proposition B.1
.

For any integer
\mathtt{p}\geq 2
, the term
A^{(\mathtt{p})}
in (
6.10
) is

A^{(\mathtt{p})}=\frac{1}{\prod\limits_{j=1}^{\mathtt{p}-1}(\mathtt{p}^{3}-\mathtt{p}+j-j^{3})}\det\,\mathrm{I\!I\!I}\,,

(B.1)

where

\mathrm{I\!I\!I}
is the
\mathtt{p}\times\mathtt{p}
tridiagonal matrix with integer entries

\mathrm{I\!I\!I}_{k}^{j}:=\begin{cases}2\mathtt{p}^{3}-2\mathtt{p}-2j^{3}-10j&\mbox{if }k=j\leq\mathtt{p}-1\,,\\
j^{3}-j-\mathtt{p}^{3}+\mathtt{p}&\mbox{if }k=j+1\textup{ or }k=j-1\,,\\
1&\mbox{if }j=k=\mathtt{p}\,,\\
0&\mbox{otherwise },\end{cases}

(B.2)

with
k
and
j
being respectively the row and column indices.

Before proving Proposition
B.1
, we show that
\ker\mathrm{I\!I\!I}\neq\{0\}
.
A direct calculus shows that:

Lemma B.2
.

For any integer
\mathtt{p}\geq 2
,
the vector

\mathbf{v}:=\begin{pmatrix}1,&2,&\dots,&\mathtt{p}-1,&3\mathtt{p}(\mathtt{p}-1)^{2}\end{pmatrix}^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathtt{p}}

belongs to the kernel of the matrix
\mathrm{I\!I\!I}
defined in (
B.2
). Thus

\det\,\mathrm{I\!I\!I}=0
.

The rest of the Appendix is devoted to the proof of Proposition
B.1
.

For any
1\leq q\leq\mathtt{p}-1
, we regard
any set of
q
indices
\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{q}\}
satisfying

1\leq j_{1}<\ldots<j_{q}\leq\mathtt{p}-1

as

\phi^{-1}(1)=\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{q}\}

where the function

\phi:\{1,\dots,\mathtt{p}-1\}\to\{0,1\}
,

j\mapsto\phi(j)
,
attains value
\phi(j_{1})=\ldots=\phi(j_{q})=1
,
and
\phi(j)=0
if and only if

j\neq j_{1},\dots,j_{q}
.
In this way
we rewrite (
6.10
) as

A^{(\mathtt{p})}=\mathtt{p}+\sum_{q=1}^{\mathtt{p}-1}\sum_{1\leq j_{1}<\dots<j_{q}\leq\mathtt{p}-1}\!\!\!\!\!\!\mathtt{n}_{q}^{(\mathtt{p})}(j_{1},\dots,j_{q})=\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{\phi:\{1,\dots,\mathtt{p}-1\}\to\{0,1\}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\mathtt{n}_{|\phi^{-1}(1)|}^{(\mathtt{p})}\big(\phi^{-1}(1)\big)\,,\quad\textup{with }\mathtt{n}_{0}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\emptyset):=\mathtt{p}\,,

(B.3)

summing on all the
functions

\phi:\{1,\dots,\mathtt{p}-1\}\to\{0,1\}
,
arranging
\phi^{-1}(1)=\{j_{1}<\ldots<j_{q}\}
in ascending order, and
denoting
q:=|\phi^{-1}(1)|
its cardinality. If
\phi\equiv 0

we have
\phi^{-1}(1)=\emptyset
.

In the sequel we denote

\delta_{j,k}:=1
if
j=k

and
\delta_{j,k}:=0
otherwise, i.e. the Kronecker delta.

Lemma B.3
.

Let
\phi:\{1,\dots,\mathtt{p}-1\}\to\{0,1\}
.
Then the terms in (
6.12
) are

\mathtt{n}_{|\phi^{-1}(1)|}^{(\mathtt{p})}\big(\phi^{-1}(1)\big)=\frac{1}{\prod\limits_{j=1}^{\mathtt{p}-1}(\mathtt{p}^{3}-\mathtt{p}+j-j^{3})}\det\widetilde{M}_{\phi}

(B.4)

where
\widetilde{M}_{\phi}
is the
\mathtt{p}\times\mathtt{p}
matrix with entries

[\widetilde{M}_{\phi}]_{k}^{j}:=\begin{cases}-12j\min\{j,k\}&\textup{if }\phi(j)=1\,,1\leq j\leq\mathtt{p}-1\,,\ \forall\,k=1,\ldots\mathtt{p}\,,\\
(\mathtt{p}^{3}-\mathtt{p}+j-j^{3})\delta_{j,k}&\textup{if }\phi(j)=0\,,1\leq j\leq\mathtt{p}-1\,,\ \forall\,k=1,\ldots\mathtt{p}\,,\\
k&\textup{if }j=\mathtt{p}\,,\ \forall\,k=1,\ldots\mathtt{p}\,.\end{cases}

(B.5)

Proof.

If
\phi\equiv 0
the matrix

\widetilde{M}_{\phi}
in (
B.5
) is triangular with diagonal entries

[\widetilde{M}_{\phi}]_{j}^{j}=\mathtt{p}^{3}-\mathtt{p}+j-j^{3}
for any
1\leq j\leq\mathtt{p}-1
and

[M_{\phi}]_{\mathtt{p}}^{\mathtt{p}}=\mathtt{p}
. Thus (
B.4
) holds, since

\mathtt{n}_{0}^{(\mathtt{p})}(\emptyset):=\mathtt{p}
, cfr. (
B.3
).
Thus in the sequel
\phi\not\equiv 0
.

Step 1
.

The determinant of
the
\mathtt{p}\times\mathtt{p}
matrix

M_{\phi}
with entries

[M_{\phi}]_{k}^{j}:=\begin{cases}\min\{j,k\}&\textup{if }\phi(j)=1\,,\,j\in\{1,\ldots,\mathtt{p}-1\}\,,\textup{ or }j=\mathtt{p}\,,\\
\delta_{j,k}&\textup{if }\phi(j)=0\,,\,j\in\{1,\ldots,\mathtt{p}-1\}\,,\end{cases}

(B.6)

is equal to

\det M_{\phi}=j_{1}(j_{2}-j_{1})\dots(j_{q}-j_{q-1})(\mathtt{p}-j_{q})\,,

(B.7)

where

\{1\leq j_{1}<\ldots<j_{q}\leq\mathtt{p}-1\}=\phi^{-1}(1)
.
We write

j_{1}(j_{2}-j_{1})\dots(j_{q}-j_{q-1})(\mathtt{p}-j_{q})=\det\underbrace{{\footnotesize\begin{pmatrix}j_{1}&0&0&\dots&0\\
j_{1}&j_{2}-j_{1}&0&\dots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\ddots&\vdots\\
j_{1}&j_{2}-j_{1}&\dots&j_{q}-j_{q-1}&0\\
j_{1}&j_{2}-j_{1}&\dots&j_{q}-j_{q-1}&\mathtt{p}-j_{q}\end{pmatrix}}}_{=:Q_{\phi}}=\det\underbrace{{\footnotesize\begin{pmatrix}j_{1}&j_{1}&j_{1}&\dots&j_{1}\\
j_{1}&j_{2}&j_{2}&\dots&j_{2}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\ddots&\vdots\\
j_{1}&j_{2}&\dots&j_{q}&j_{q}\\
j_{1}&j_{2}&\dots&j_{q}&\mathtt{p}\end{pmatrix}}}_{=:N_{\phi}}

(B.8)

where the
j
-th column of the
matrix
N_{\phi}

is the sum of the first
j
columns of the matrix
Q_{\phi}
.
It results

\det N_{\phi}=\det\widehat{M}

(B.9)

where

\widehat{M}
is the
\mathtt{p}\times\mathtt{p}
matrix
defined as follows:
for any
j=1,\dots,\mathtt{p}

•

for any
1\leq j\leq\mathtt{p}-1
if
\phi(j)=0
,
i.e.
j\neq j_{1},\dots,j_{q}
,
then the
j
-th column of
\widehat{M}
is the vector
e_{j}
of the standard basis of
\mathbb{R}^{\mathtt{p}}
;

•

for any
1\leq j\leq\mathtt{p}-1
if
\phi(j)=1
, i.e.
j=j_{\iota}
with
\iota=1,\dots,q
, then the
j
-th column of
\widehat{M}
has its
j_{1},\ldots,j_{q}
-th rows equal to the
1,\ldots,q
-th rows of the
i
-th column of
N_{\phi}
, i.e.
[\widehat{M}]^{j_{\iota}}_{j_{\kappa}}=[N_{\phi}]^{\iota}_{\kappa}
for any
\iota,\kappa\in\{1,\ldots,q\}
;

•

the remaining elements
\widehat{M}_{k}^{j}
are equal to
\min\{j,k\}
, in particular the
\mathtt{p}
-th column is

\widehat{M}_{k}^{\mathtt{p}}=k
.

The identity (
B.9
)
follows expanding the determinant by the above bullets.
We actually claim that

\widehat{M}=M_{\phi}\,.

(B.10)

Indeed the
\mathtt{p}
-th column of
\widehat{M}
and
M_{\phi}
are identical. If
for
1\leq j\leq\mathtt{p}-1
,

\phi(j)=0
, then
by the first bullet, the
j
-th column of

\widehat{M}
and
M_{\phi}
coincide.
We now check that also the

j
-th columns of
\widehat{M}
and
M_{\phi}
coincide, if
j=j_{\iota}
,
\iota\in\{1,\ldots,q\}
.
If
k\notin\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{q}\}

by the third bullet

\widehat{M}_{k}^{j}=\min\{j,k\}=[M_{\phi}]_{k}^{j}
as in (
B.6
).
If

k=j_{\kappa}
for some
\kappa\in\{1,\dots,q\}
, then,
by the second bullet
(and since
j_{1},\dots,j_{q}
are arranged in ascending order)

\widehat{M}_{k}^{j}=\widehat{M}_{j_{\kappa}}^{j_{\iota}}=[N_{\phi}]_{\kappa}^{\iota}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{toenlarge}}}{{=}}j_{\min\{\kappa,\iota\}}=\min\{j_{\iota},j_{\kappa}\}=\min\{j,k\}=[M_{\phi}]_{k}^{j}
.
This proves (
B.10
). Finally (
B.7
) follows from
(
B.10
), (
B.9
) and (
B.8
).

Step 2
.
The matrix
\widetilde{M}_{\phi}

in (
B.5
) is obtained
by multiplying the
j
-th column,
1\leq j\leq\mathtt{p}-1
,
of the matrix
M_{\phi}
in
(
B.6
) by the factor
-12j
, if
\phi(j)=1
, or
(\mathtt{p}^{3}-\mathtt{p}+j-j^{3})
if
\phi(j)=0
. Thus, by computing the determinant of
\widetilde{M}_{\phi}
, using
(
B.7
) and recalling (
6.12
), we deduce (
B.4
).
∎

We now write
A^{(\mathtt{p})}
as the determinant of a matrix.

Lemma B.4
.

For any integer
\mathtt{p}\geq 2
, the term
A^{(\mathtt{p})}
in (
6.10
) is

A^{(\mathtt{p})}=\frac{1}{\prod\limits_{j=1}^{\mathtt{p}-1}(\mathtt{p}^{3}-\mathtt{p}+j-j^{3})}\det\mathtt{M}

(B.11)

where

\mathtt{M}
is the
\mathtt{p}\times\mathtt{p}
matrix with integer entries

\mathtt{M}_{k}^{j}:=\begin{cases}-12j\min\{j,k\}&\textup{if }k\neq j\leq\mathtt{p}-1\,,\\
\mathtt{p}^{3}-\mathtt{p}-j^{3}-12j^{2}+j&\textup{if }k=j\leq\mathtt{p}-1\,,\\
k&\textup{if }j=\mathtt{p}\,,\,\forall k=1,\ldots,\mathtt{p}\,.\end{cases}

(B.12)

Proof.

By (
B.3
) and
(
B.4
) we have

A^{(\mathtt{p})}=\tfrac{1}{\prod\limits_{j=1}^{\mathtt{p}-1}(\mathtt{p}^{3}-\mathtt{p}+j-j^{3})}\sum_{\phi:\{1,\dots,\mathtt{p}-1\}\to\{0,1\}}\det\widetilde{M}_{\phi}\,,

(B.13)

and the matrix
\widetilde{M}_{\phi}
in (
B.5
) can be written as

\widetilde{M}_{\phi}=\begin{pmatrix}col_{1;\phi(1)}\ \big|&col_{2;\phi(2)}\ \big|&\dots\ \big|&col_{\mathtt{p}-1;\phi(\mathtt{p}-1)}\ \big|&\widetilde{col}_{1}\end{pmatrix}

(B.14)

with column vectors

col_{j;1}:=\big(-12j\min\{j,k\}\big)_{k=1,\dots,\mathtt{p}}\,,\quad col_{j;0}:=\big((\mathtt{p}^{3}-\mathtt{p}+j-j^{3})\delta_{j,k}\big)_{k=1,\dots,\mathtt{p}}\,,\quad\widetilde{col}_{1}:=\big(1,\dots,\mathtt{p}\big)^{\top}\,.

(B.15)

By (
B.13
), (
B.14
) and
the multilinearity of the determinant we deduce

A^{(\mathtt{p})}=\tfrac{1}{\prod\limits_{j=1}^{\mathtt{p}-1}(\mathtt{p}^{3}-\mathtt{p}+j-j^{3})}\det\underbrace{\begin{pmatrix}col_{1;0}+col_{1;1}\ \big|&col_{2;0}+col_{2;1}\ \big|&\dots\ \big|&col_{\mathtt{p}-1;0}+col_{\mathtt{p}-1;1}\ \big|&\widetilde{col}_{1}\end{pmatrix}}_{=\mathtt{M}}

where, by inspection, cfr.(
B.15
),
the entries of the matrix
\mathtt{M}
are those given in (
B.12
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
B.1
.

The determinant of
\mathtt{M}
is equal to

\det\mathtt{M}=\det\mathtt{H}

(B.16)

where the matrix

\mathtt{H}
is obtained by
\mathtt{M}
in (
B.12
)
substituting each row, except the first one, with the difference between itself and the previous row,
in formulas

\mathtt{H}_{1}^{j}:=\mathtt{M}_{1}^{j}
,
\mathtt{H}_{k}^{j}:=\mathtt{M}_{k}^{j}-\mathtt{M}_{k-1}^{j}
,
\forall
,
k=2,\dots,\mathtt{p}
,

\forall\,j=1,\dots,\mathtt{p}
.
In view of (
B.12
)
its entries are

\mathtt{H}_{k}^{j}:=\begin{cases}-12j&\textup{if }k<j\leq\mathtt{p}-1\,,\\
\mathtt{p}^{3}-\mathtt{p}-j^{3}-11j&\textup{if }k=j\leq\mathtt{p}-1\,,\\
-(\mathtt{p}^{3}-\mathtt{p}-j^{3}+j)&\textup{if }k=j+1\,,\\
0&\textup{if }j+2\leq k\leq\mathtt{p}\,,\\
1&\textup{if }j=\mathtt{p}\,,\,\forall k=1,\ldots,\mathtt{p}\,.\end{cases}

(B.17)

Furthermore

\det\mathtt{H}=\det\mathrm{I\!I\!I}

(B.18)

where the matrix

\mathrm{I\!I\!I}
is obtained from
\mathtt{H}
by substituting each row, except the last one, with the difference between itself and the next row, in formulas

\mathrm{I\!I\!I}_{\mathtt{p}}^{j}:=\mathtt{H}_{\mathtt{p}}^{j}
,
\mathrm{I\!I\!I}_{k}^{j}:=\mathtt{H}_{k}^{j}-\mathtt{H}_{k+1}^{j}
,
\forall\,k=1,\dots,\mathtt{p}-1
,
\forall j=1,\dots,\mathtt{p}
.
In view of (
B.17
), the matrix
\mathrm{I\!I\!I}
is the tridiagonal one in (
B.2
).

In conclusion (
B.11
), (
B.16
) and
(
B.18
) imply (
B.1
). ∎
